## U-20260922-02 · 2026-09-22 · Windows recorder captures mouse side buttons (x1/x2) · #incident #recording

- **What**: `Win32InputHook._mouse_event` now records `WM_XBUTTONDOWN` / `WM_XBUTTONUP` (0x020B / 0x020C) as `mouse_down` / `mouse_up` with `button` `"x1"` / `"x2"`. The button id lives in the high word of `mouseData` (like the wheel's notch count), so the message-id-keyed `_MOUSE_BUTTONS` table could not express it and side clicks were silently dropped. `_RECORDED_BUTTON` in `input_macro.py` gained `x1` / `x2` in the same change: its fallback for an unknown name is `mouse_left`, so emitting `x1` without the replay mapping would have turned a side click into a real left click. Unknown high-word values are dropped, not guessed, for the same reason.
- **Not changed**: `LEGACY_MOUSE_COMMAND` (the down-events-only `stop_record()` queue) still has no side buttons — there is no `AC_mouse_x1` executor alias, and naming one would fail at replay instead of at record. Comment added in `recorder_base.py`.
- **Result / numbers**: `test/unit_test/headless/test_input_hook_side_buttons.py`, 10 tests (press/release per button, the two buttons told apart, unknown value dropped, replay map covers every name the hook emits). Windows-only module; skips elsewhere.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/windows/record/win32_input_hook.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/input_macro/input_macro.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/input_macro/recorder_base.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-03 · 2026-09-22 · hotkey/type_keyboard release held keys on failure · #incident #input

- **What**: `wrapper/auto_control_keyboard.py` — `hotkey` and `type_keyboard` now release from `finally` through `_release_still_held(still_held, is_shift)`. A key is recorded as held only after its press succeeded, so a key that never went down is never released (that would cancel a key the user is physically holding); release order is reverse press order; a release failing inside the cleanup is logged and the loop continues, and nothing is raised from `finally`.
- **Why `finally`, not a wider `except`**: the functions catch `(OSError, RuntimeError, AttributeError, TypeError, ValueError)`, and `AutoControlKeyboardException` (an `AutoControlException`) is under none of them, so the most likely failures skipped the `except` entirely. A test pins that premise.
- **Result / numbers**: `test/unit_test/headless/test_keyboard_release_on_failure.py`, 11 tests; mutating the cleanup to re-raise fails 3 of them. The cleanup-error test uses two distinct exception objects so identity shows which one reached the caller.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/wrapper/auto_control_keyboard.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (§5.2 row, §7 note, line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-04 · 2026-09-22 · Line-count gate: 71 map rows were never measured · #incident #docs

- **What**: `test_doc_line_counts.py`'s `_resolve()` tried a name with a `/` in it only against the package root, never against the heading's package. Every row written as a sub-path — the Windows / macOS / X11 backend tables (`record/win32_input_hook.py` under `windows/`), `tools/_factories.py` / `tools/_handlers.py` under `utils/mcp_server/`, the whole USB table (`usb/passthrough/…`, `usbip/…` under `utils/usb/`), and the GUI skeleton table (context comes from the `###` heading's `gui/`) — resolved to nothing and was skipped silently. Rows quoting one figure per file (`82 / 78`) were skipped too, because the single-number pattern does not match them.
- **Fix**: `_resolve` tries the heading package, then its ancestors (for sub-paths), then the package and repo roots; a `###` heading's path is the default context for `####` headings that name none, and `##` resets it; `_rewrite_slashed_row` measures each file of a slashed row. New `test_every_row_names_something_in_the_tree` fails on any row the resolver cannot place, so a skip can no longer be silent.
- **Result / numbers**: 55 figures corrected on the first `--fix`, e.g. `tools/_factories.py` 8,739 → 8,974, `tools/_handlers.py` 4,651 → 4,791, `keyboard/win32_ctype_keyboard_control.py` 55 → 98, `usb/usb_devices.py` 286 → 294. Unresolved rows: 71 → 0. Tests in the module: 2 → 5.
- **Files**: `test/unit_test/headless/test_doc_line_counts.py`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures, scan date).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-06 · 2026-09-22 · USB watcher: stop outlasts enumeration, restart can't revive · #incident #usb

- **What**: `utils/usb/usb_watcher.py`. `stop()` joined the poller for 2 s; one enumeration is bounded only by the 10 s subprocess timeout, so `stop()` could return with the thread alive. `start()` then `clear()`ed the shared stop event and the stale poller resumed beside the new one, untracked. Now: a fresh `threading.Event` per run, passed to `_loop(stop)`; the initial enumeration returns without touching the snapshot if its run was stopped meanwhile; `stop()` joins for `_STOP_JOIN_TIMEOUT_S = SUBPROCESS_TIMEOUT_S + 1.0` and warns if the thread is still running. `usb_devices._SUBPROCESS_TIMEOUT_S` became public `SUBPROCESS_TIMEOUT_S` (no other user in the tree).
- **How it surfaced**: `test_adapter_registry_sweep.py::test_command_dispatches_and_records_json[AC_usb_watch_start]` errored once in a large `-k` run (its no-leftover-thread fixture), passed alone and 3/3 as a file — a cold PowerShell start under load outlasting the 2 s join.
- **Result / numbers**: `test_usb_watcher.py` 9 → 11 tests; `test_restart_does_not_revive_a_poller_that_outlived_stop` fails against the shared-event `clear()` (mutation checked).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/usb/usb_watcher.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/usb/usb_devices.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_usb_watcher.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-12 · 2026-09-22 · 16 background services: a restart no longer revives the old loop · #incident #threading

- **What**: The bug fixed in the USB watcher (U-20260922-06) was a pattern, not a one-off. `grep "_stop.clear()"` found it in 16 more places, all shaped the same: `stop()` sets `self._stop`, joins with a timeout, drops the thread; `start()` calls `self._stop.clear()` and starts a new thread whose loop reads `self._stop`. A loop still inside a long iteration when the join timed out is revived by the next `start()` and runs beside the new one, untracked. Where the loop body acts, that doubles the action: `Scheduler._run` executes jobs inline, `HotkeyDaemon` fires bindings, `TriggerEngine` / `EmailTriggerWatcher` run action lists, `PopupWatchdog` clicks.
- **Fix**: one `threading.Event` per run, created in `start()` (never `clear()`ed) and passed to the loop as a required argument; `stop()` is unchanged and sets the current run's event. Files: `utils/{accessibility/recorder, clipboard_history/clipboard_history, mcp_server/plugin_watcher, observer/observer, profiler/resource_profiler, remote_desktop/file_sync, scheduler/scheduler, tls_acme/renewal, triggers/email_trigger, triggers/trigger_engine, usb/passthrough/loopback, watchdog/popup_watchdog}.py` (scripted, each replacement asserted to match once), `utils/hotkey/hotkey_daemon.py` (event goes through `BackendContext.stop_event`), `utils/usbip/server.py` (the accept loop hands its event on to every URB worker), `osx/listener/osx_listener.py`, `utils/chatops/slack_bot.py` (`run_forever` runs on the caller's thread).
- **Result / numbers**: new `test/unit_test/headless/test_background_restart_keeps_old_run_stopped.py`, 15 tests: each service's `threading` is swapped for a thread that never finishes (what `stop()` sees when the loop is stuck), and after stop → start the first run's event must still be set and the loop must have been handed it. Against the old code every case fails (no event is handed to the loop). The services' existing tests: 325 passed. The macOS tap is covered by review only (its `start()` blocks on a readiness event a fake thread never sets).
- **Not in this change**: remote desktop host / relay / viewer, whose loops are split across mixins — next entry.
- **Files**: the 16 modules above, the new test, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-13 · 2026-09-22 · Remote desktop host/relay/viewer: per-run events on restart · #incident #remote-desktop

- **What**: The remote desktop half of U-20260922-12. `RemoteDesktopHost._start_locked`, `RemoteDesktopRelay.start` and `RemoteDesktopViewer.connect` called `self._shutdown.clear()`; their loops (`host._accept_loop`, `host_capture._capture_loop` / `_cursor_loop`, `relay._accept_loop`, `viewer._recv_loop`) read `self._shutdown` on every iteration. Now each run creates its own event and hands it to its loops; `is_running` / `connected` still read the current one. `host_capture.py` no longer needs the host's `_shutdown` at all (docstring and `TYPE_CHECKING` declaration updated).
- **Host, concretely**: `_accept_loop` runs the auth/TLS handshake (`_open_channel`, bounded by `_AUTH_TIMEOUT_S` = 60 s) and only then re-checks the stop flag under `_clients_lock` — the fix from `test_a_client_finishing_its_handshake_after_stop_is_not_attached`. With a restart inside that window the flag had been cleared, so the stale handler joined the new run. New `test_a_handshake_from_the_stopped_run_does_not_join_the_next_one` fails on the old code (run in a detached worktree at 6f7a58b) and passes now.
- **Viewer, concretely**: a receiver finishing after a reconnect set `_connected = False` and called `_notify_error` for its closing socket, both landing on the new connection. `_recv_loop(channel, stop)` now only clears `_connected`, and `_read_and_dispatch` only reports a read error, while `self._channel is channel`. New `test_remote_desktop_viewer_reconnect.py` (2 tests, one a positive control that a genuinely dropped connection is still reported).
- **Result / numbers**: every remote desktop / relay / rdusb / webrtc-host test module: 665 passed, 2 skipped.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/{host,host_capture,relay,viewer}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_remote_desktop_host_lifecycle.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_remote_desktop_viewer_reconnect.py` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-14 · 2026-09-22 · Scheduler: never start a job that is still running · #incident #scheduler

- **What**: The residue of U-20260922-12 in `utils/scheduler/scheduler.py`. `_fire` updates `next_run_ts` / `runs` only after `self._execute(actions)` returns, so a job keeps looking due for as long as it runs. After a `stop()` that timed out inside a long job and a new `start()`, the new loop saw that job as due and executed it again beside the old one (and `runs` was incremented twice, which could overshoot `max_runs`). `_tick_once` now marks each due job in `self._in_flight` in the same locked pass that selects it, skips jobs already marked, and unmarks each in a per-job `finally`; an outer `finally` unmarks only the jobs it never reached, so it cannot clear a marker another loop set after this one finished the job.
- **Result / numbers**: `test_scheduler.py` 4 → 5 tests; `test_a_job_still_running_is_not_started_again` drives two `_tick_once` calls at once with a history-store stub (nothing written) and fails on the old code by construction (the second call executes the job).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/scheduler/scheduler.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_scheduler.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-15 · 2026-09-22 · Windows key table: `down` is VK_DOWN; touchpad wheel accumulates · #incident #input #recording

- **What**: The two AutoControlGUI halves of root `progress.md` X-4, reported from Jeffrey_RPA (`Jeffrey_RPA/Progress.md`, 2026-09-21 entry). (1) `wrapper/_platform_windows.py` built `keyboard_keys_table` from every constant in `win32_vk.py`, so 19 entries were mouse-event flags, `KEYEVENTF_*` flags or the `MapVirtualKey` type rather than keys; `down` was `MOUSEEVENTF_XDOWN` = 0x80 = `VK_F17`. `down` is now `VK_DOWN` (Linux and macOS already mapped it to the arrow) and the other 18 are removed, so they raise "unknown key" instead of pressing whatever shares their value. The constants stay in `win32_vk.py`, where the mouse backend imports them directly; nothing read them through the key table. (2) `windows/record/win32_input_hook.py` floored each `WM_MOUSEWHEEL` separately (`raw // 120`), which a precision touchpad's ±30 turns into 0 up and -1 down. `_wheel_notches` now carries the remainder per hook, emits a `scroll` event only for whole notches (at the position of the event that completed one), and drops the remainder when the direction reverses.
- **Result / numbers**: 35 new tests (`test_input_hook_wheel.py` 11, `test_win32_key_table.py` 24); with the existing hook, side-button, Unicode-batch and seam-contract modules 104 passed. Table: 192 → 174 names (`down` and `vk_down` now share 0x28). macOS needs no change: `kCGScrollWheelEventDeltaAxis1` is already whole lines.
- **Downstream**: Jeffrey_RPA reads this tree through its editable install, so its override is stale at once. `Jeffrey_RPA/test/test_gui_control.py`: 588 passed, 1 failed — `test_every_library_name_override_is_still_needed`, the test built to go red when upstream is fixed; its message says to delete `down` from `_gui_control._LIBRARY_NAME_OVERRIDES`. Not changed from here: another session had uncommitted work in Jeffrey_RPA's progress and architecture files. The remaining half is root `progress.md` X-4.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/wrapper/_platform_windows.py`, `je_auto_control/windows/record/win32_input_hook.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_input_hook_wheel.py` (new), `test/unit_test/headless/test_win32_key_table.py` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: root `progress.md` X-4 (Jeffrey_RPA side).

## U-20260922-16 · 2026-09-22 · Dependabot S-11: pypdf floor, direct pillow floor, lock refresh · #incident #security #deps

- **What**: The AutoControlGUI half of root `progress.md` S-11: 26 open Dependabot alerts on `main` (1 critical, 13 high), checked one by one for reachability. Three groups. (1) **pypdf** (12 alerts, 2 high, all fixed by 6.16.1): malformed-PDF infinite loops and memory blow-ups; reachable, because `utils/pdf/pdf_reader.py` opens caller-supplied files. The `pdf` extra's floor goes `>=4.0` → `>=6.16.1`. (2) **Pillow in `requirements.txt`** (13 alerts, created 2026-07-21): the file has never listed Pillow; GitHub resolved the then-unbounded `je_auto_control` to 0.0.214 (pillow 12.2.0), and the floor added in `5958292` never replaced that recorded entry (the SBOM still lists pillow 12.2.0 as a direct dependency next to 12.3.0). Added `pillow>=12.3.0` as a direct line so the file's own entry is non-vulnerable. (3) **`uv.lock`**: anyio 4.13.0 → 4.14.2 (critical, TLS host-name encoding; pulled by `starlette`), cryptography 49.0.0 → 50.0.1 (high, PKCS#7 EnvelopedData decryption oracle — not reachable, the package does no PKCS#7 decryption, so the `>=48.0.1` floor stays), pypdf → 6.19.0; uv also moved pyopenssl 26.3.0 → 26.4.0 and the stale project entry 0.0.195 → 0.0.221.
- **Result / numbers**: alerts close only once this reaches `main`. Tests selected by `pdf or arm64 or dependency or marker`: 40 passed, 3 skipped, with pypdf 6.19.0 installed. Windows arm64 re-probed the same day: `opencv-python` still has no win_arm64 wheel and `cryptography` still stops at 46.0.3, so that `Progress.md` item is unchanged.
- **Files**: `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `uv.lock`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## U-20260923-01 · 2026-09-23 · Log file out of the cwd: home default, JE_AUTOCONTROL_LOG_FILE · #done #logging

- **What**: The AutoControlGUI part of root `progress.md` X-6. `utils/logging/logging_instance.py` opened the relative path `AutoControlGUI.log` at import. Because the package registers a `pytest11` plugin and the plugin is a submodule, every pytest run on a machine with the package installed imported the whole facade before any conftest and left a log in its cwd: the workspace root, WebRunner, FileAutomation, PyBreeze and JEDITOR all carried one, and Jeffrey_RPA needed `-p no:je_auto_control` plus a cwd-parking import hook to keep tests out of its production log. The file is now `$JE_AUTOCONTROL_LOG_FILE` (relative resolves against the cwd at import; `os.devnull` disables it) or `~/.je_auto_control/logs/AutoControlGUI.log`, the directory the package already uses for its other per-user state. Shared by every process, so: append mode (the old `mode="w"` was already forced to append by CPython because `maxBytes > 0`; the architecture note claiming "truncated" was wrong), `%(process)d` in the format, and rotation to `.1` past 10 MiB done only at open, since a rename inside `emit()` fails on Windows while another process holds the file and would then fail on every later record. The old handler never rotated at all (`backupCount=0`). The package's handler is built with `delay=True` and opens (and creates its directory) on the first record, and the four import-time `Load … Setting` info lines in `wrapper/_platform_{windows,linux,wayland,osx}.py` are removed, so importing writes no file at all: the first version opened the file at import and `test_facade_import_is_light.test_importing_the_facade_writes_no_state_of_its_own` failed on it, which is the property the project already holds for `~/.je_auto_control/`. Those four lines were also most of Jeffrey_RPA's 9,716-line log. A file that cannot be opened is swapped for `os.devnull` with one `RuntimeWarning`; it used to fail the import.
- **Result / numbers**: `test_logging_location.py`, 13 tests; two run a fresh interpreter with a temporary home and cwd: `import je_auto_control` leaves both empty (on the old code the cwd gets `AutoControlGUI.log`), and one logged record lands in the home file. With `test_logging_encoding.py` and `test_facade_import_is_light.py`: 20 passed.
- **Not changed, recorded in `Progress.md`**: the same module still sets the root logger to DEBUG at import; downstream `caplog` use may depend on it.
- **Downstream**: Jeffrey_RPA's `test/conftest.py` cwd-parking hook for this module no longer redirects anything; setting `JE_AUTOCONTROL_LOG_FILE` before the lazy import replaces it. Its bot's GUI commands now log to the home file instead of the repo root. Both are reported to the Jeffrey_RPA session rather than edited from here.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/logging/logging_instance.py`, `je_auto_control/wrapper/_platform_{windows,linux,wayland,osx}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_logging_location.py` (new), `README.md`, `README/README_zh-TW.md`, `README/README_zh-CN.md`, `architecture.md` (§6 import-time contracts), `architecture_explore.md` (row + figures), `CHANGELOG.md`, `Progress.md`.
- **Open items**: root `progress.md` X-6 (WebRunner, JEDITOR, FrontEngine loggers); `Progress.md` root-logger item.

## U-20260923-02 · 2026-09-23 · USB watcher: a stopped (not superseded) run still primes · #incident #usb #ci

- **What**: CI on PR #486 at `8cf0f93` went red in one cell, `pytest-headless (windows-2022, 3.13)`: `test_usb_watcher.py::test_callback_is_called_for_each_event` got `{'1', '2', '3'}` instead of `{'2', '3'}` (1 failed, 8,142 passed). Cause: U-20260922-06 made `_loop` drop its priming enumeration whenever `stop.is_set()`, to keep a stale run from overwriting a newer run's snapshot. But five tests in that file drive `start(); stop(); poll_once()` and rely on the priming, which now happened only when the enumeration returned before `stop()` set the event — a race a fast runner loses. The tests describe the documented behavior (start() primes); the guard was broader than its reason. It now skips priming only when superseded (`stop is not self._stop`, read under `_lifecycle_lock`, with the snapshot write inside the same lock so no `start()` can slip between the check and the write; no other path takes the two locks in the opposite order).
- **Result / numbers**: two new tests with a blocking enumerator, no timing luck: `test_a_stop_during_priming_still_primes` fails on the previous code (checked in a detached worktree of `5714a59`) and passes now; `test_a_superseded_run_does_not_overwrite_the_new_snapshot` holds the original reason for the guard. `test_usb_watcher.py`: 13 passed.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/usb/usb_watcher.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_usb_watcher.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-03 · 2026-09-23 · Every swallowing broad except says why; AST gate in CI · #done #quality

- **What**: The AutoControlGUI bullet of root `progress.md` S-6 (`auto_control_keyboard.py`'s release-path `except Exception` had no `# reason:`), widened to the whole package, because `CLAUDE.md`'s "suppressions need an inline justification" was checked by nothing: CI's `ruff` has no config, so it runs the default rules without `BLE`, and pylint is not a CI job. An AST scan found 24 handlers that catch `Exception`/`BaseException` and swallow without a `reason:` on the `except` line — some with a bare `# noqa: BLE001`, some with a reason in prose the convention does not recognise (`# noqa: BLE001 - preserved contract`), some with nothing (`failure_bundle/bundle.py` ×2, the X11 hotkey backend). Each now carries `# noqa: BLE001  # reason: …` stating why swallowing is right there (callback isolation in the remote desktop viewer/host client/file receiver, best-effort collectors that record the failure in the bundle manifest, saga compensation, chaos probes/faults/rollbacks, the outbox counting a failed delivery, URB status translation, the "never raises" contracts of `send_*_event_to_window`). None was narrowed: each is a boundary where the callee is caller-supplied or the contract is "never raise".
- **Gate**: `test/unit_test/headless/test_broad_except_reasons.py` walks every module's AST and fails on a broad, non-re-raising handler without `reason:` on its line; handlers that clean up and re-raise are exempt. Two positive controls keep an empty result from meaning "blind". `CLAUDE.md` names the test next to the rule.
- **Result / numbers**: 24 → 0 unjustified handlers; 3 tests. No handler behavior changed, so no CHANGELOG entry.
- **Files**: 11 modules under `je_auto_control/` (`utils/chaos`, `utils/failure_bundle`, `utils/hotkey/backends/linux_backend.py`, `utils/outbox`, `utils/remote_desktop/{file_transfer,host_client,viewer}.py`, `utils/saga`, `utils/usbip/libusb_backend.py`, `wrapper/auto_control_{keyboard,mouse}.py`), `test/unit_test/headless/test_broad_except_reasons.py` (new), `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-04 · 2026-09-23 · Pillow getdata → get_flattened_data (removed in Pillow 14) · #migration #deps

- **What**: `Image.getdata()` is deprecated since Pillow 12.1.0 and removed in Pillow 14 (2027-10-15); `get_flattened_data()`, added in the same release, returns the same pixels as a tuple ([12.1.0 notes](https://pillow.readthedocs.io/en/stable/releasenotes/12.1.0.html), [deprecations](https://pillow.readthedocs.io/en/stable/deprecations.html)). The three calls — `utils/color_stats/color_stats.py` and both hashes in `utils/image_dedup/perceptual_hash.py` — now use it; the package pins `pillow==12.3.0`, so no fallback is needed. Checked on 12.3.0 that both return identical sequences for RGB and L images.
- **Result / numbers**: these three calls were the source of all 13 warnings in the last full headless run (8,166 passed). `-k "color_stats or perceptual or dedup or hash or facade_import" -W error::DeprecationWarning`: 76 passed, 3 skipped. Also found the local `.venv` on Pillow 12.2.0 (the version with the 13 CVEs) despite the pin, and reinstalled 12.3.0.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/color_stats/color_stats.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/image_dedup/perceptual_hash.py`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-05 · 2026-09-23 · Line limit enforced by ruff; stub regenerated (199 → 739) · #done #quality #typing

- **What**: `CLAUDE.md` said the 120-character line limit was the one limit CI rejects (through `ruff`). It was not: the repo had no ruff configuration, so `ruff check je_auto_control/` ran the default rule set (`E4`, `E7`, `E9`, `F`), which has no `E501`, and 67 lines were over 120. `pyproject.toml` now sets `[tool.ruff] line-length = 120` with `extend-select = ["E501"]`, and every flagged line is back under it. Ruff's own rule still exempts a line that ends in a pragma (`# noqa`, `# nosec`), since a marker cannot move.
- **The generated stub**: 31 of the 67 were in `je_auto_control/actions.pyi`, the IDE stub `utils/stubs/generator.py` writes. Regenerating it showed it was far out of date: **199 of 739** executor commands, several with old signatures (`AC_a11y_click` without `window_title`/`contains`). The generator now puts one parameter per line when a `def` line would exceed `MAX_LINE_LENGTH` (120), splitting only at top-level commas so `Dict[str, Any]` stays whole, and the stub is regenerated with it (739 signatures, no line over 120). A test now fails when the stub's command set differs from `executor.event_dict`; names only, because annotations render differently across Python versions (3.14 prints `typing.Union` as `X | Y`).
- **The other 36 lines**: most carried a `NOSONAR` or `nosemgrep` marker followed by a paragraph of justification. The markers stay on the line the analyzer reports (Codacy honours `nosemgrep` only there, and `NOSONAR` only works same-line) and the prose moved to a comment directly above, with `# reason: see above` where the rule calls for an inline reason. The rest were long strings (three UI strings each in the English and Japanese catalogues, one error message), imports and `except` tuples, wrapped with no change in value. `diagnostics.py` imports `import_module` directly so its marked line fits; `xml_file.py` parenthesises one import so its marker stays on the statement's first line.
- **Result / numbers**: `ruff check je_auto_control/` clean with E501 at 120; `test_stub_generator.py` 15 → 19 tests. The `CLAUDE.md` sentence is now true, so it stays as written.
- **Files**: `pyproject.toml`, `je_auto_control/utils/stubs/generator.py`, `je_auto_control/actions.pyi`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_stub_generator.py`, 22 modules for wrapped lines (`gui/language_wrapper/{english,japanese}.py`, `gui/remote_desktop/{webrtc_known_hosts,webrtc_panel}.py`, `gui/usb_browser_tab.py`, `gui/usb_passthrough_prompt.py`, `utils/admin/admin_client.py`, `utils/agent/backends/openai.py`, `utils/callback/callback_function_executor.py`, `utils/clipboard/clipboard.py`, `utils/diagnostics/diagnostics.py`, `utils/exception/exception_tags.py`, `utils/remote_desktop/{host,viewer,webrtc_host,webrtc_stats}.py`, `utils/test_suite/reports.py`, `utils/usb/passthrough/winusb_backend.py`, `utils/usbip/libusb_backend.py`, `utils/vision/backends/openai_backend.py`, `utils/xml/xml_file/xml_file.py`, `wrapper/auto_control_{keyboard,record}.py`), `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-06 · 2026-09-23 · Test runs no longer write the shared package log · #incident #logging #testing

- **What**: Reported by the Jeffrey_RPA session an hour after U-20260923-01: this repo's own test suite was writing into the new shared `~/.je_auto_control/logs/AutoControlGUI.log` (5,360 lines by 00:38, all of them from pytest processes — this suite's runs plus one other repo's run through the `pytest11` plugin), the same leak Jeffrey_RPA's repo-root file had and a file its bot will append to after its next restart. A `conftest.py` cannot set `JE_AUTOCONTROL_LOG_FILE` early enough if the handler resolves it at construction, because the plugin imports the package before any conftest runs. The package handler now re-reads `default_log_file()` each time it opens the file (it opens on the first record), and the new `test/conftest.py` overwrites the variable with `<tempdir>/je_auto_control_pytest/AutoControlGUI.log` for every test directory. The polluted file was deleted after checking every writer pid was a pytest process.
- **Result / numbers**: two tests in `test_logging_location.py` (15 now): a delayed handler built before the variable is set writes to the path set afterwards, and the running suite's `default_log_file()` is not the shared file.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/logging/logging_instance.py`, `test/conftest.py` (new), `test/unit_test/headless/test_logging_location.py`, `architecture.md` (§6 import-time contracts), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## U-20260923-07 · 2026-09-23 · Tests get their own home; no state path resolved at import · #done #testing #state

- **What**: The `Progress.md` item added an hour earlier (U-20260923-06's open item): the suite wrote the account's real per-user state. `test/conftest.py` now gives each run a fresh temporary home (`HOME` and `USERPROFILE`, removed at exit) as well as its own log file — but that only works for paths the package resolves when it uses them, and nine did not: the action signing and encryption keys, the host fingerprint and known hosts, the host service config, the WebRTC inbox, and the A/B locator, cost and self-healing log paths, all bound while their module was imported (which the `pytest11` plugin does before any conftest). They are functions now. Four module-level singletons built during the import (`default_ab_store`, `default_cost_store`, `default_heal_log`, `default_secret_manager`) kept an explicit path only and resolve the default on each use, and `default_history_store` takes the resolver itself, so `HistoryStore` accepts a callable path and resolves it once, on first use.
- **How it is held**: `test_state_paths_follow_home.py` (14 tests). An AST scan rejects any home lookup outside a function body, with a positive control for each import-time form (module level, class body, default argument). A subprocess imports the package under a distinctively named home and searches every module global, class attribute and module-level object attribute for that path — which is how `default_history_store._path` and the four stores were found; the AST scan alone missed them, because they read the home through a helper called at module level. Each resolver is also checked against a `HOME` set after the import.
- **Result / numbers**: before, one full run modified `audit.db`, `address_book.json`, `quarantine.json` and `run_history.sqlite` in the real home (and `artifacts/` had grown to 419 error screenshots from earlier runs). After, a full run leaves every file under `~/.je_auto_control/` untouched — 426 files, zero differences in size or mtime.
- **Files**: `test/conftest.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/{ab_locator/store,cost_telemetry/store,self_healing/heal_log,secrets/secret_store,action_signing/cipher,action_signing/signer,run_history/history_store}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/{fingerprint,host_service,webrtc_files}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_state_paths_follow_home.py` (new), `test/unit_test/headless/{test_webrtc_viewer_control,test_cost_telemetry,test_stub_generator,test_plugin_sdk_batch}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture.md`, `Progress.md`.
- **Also**: `test_plugin_sdk_batch.py` registered `AC_plugin_loaded` on the shared executor and never removed it, which the new stub-drift test caught (it compares against a fresh `Executor()` now, and the plugin test restores the table).
- **Open items**: none. The 419 screenshots and the state files already in the real home are left alone — they are the account's, not this repo's.

## U-20260923-08 · 2026-09-23 · Import no longer sets the root logger to DEBUG · #done #logging

- **What**: The `Progress.md` item "`import je_auto_control` sets the root logger to DEBUG". `utils/logging/logging_instance.py` called `logging.root.setLevel(logging.DEBUG)` at import, so any program that imported this package had every third-party library's DEBUG records pushed into its own handlers. `autocontrol_logger` now carries the DEBUG level itself; nothing about this package's own records changes, because a logger's effective level is its own once set.
- **The blocker, checked instead of assumed**: the item said downstream might silently depend on it (pytest's `caplog` reports DEBUG only if some level allows it). Measured: Jeffrey_RPA, PyBreeze and TestPioneer contain **no** `caplog`, `assertLogs` or `LogCapture` use at all; WebRunner's one `assertLogs` passes `level=logging.WARNING`; this repo's seven `caplog` uses all call `at_level` explicitly. PyBreeze even has a test asserting that a library must not force the root level or call `basicConfig` — the same rule, from the other side.
- **Result / numbers**: new `test_importing_the_package_leaves_the_root_logger_alone` runs a fresh interpreter and asserts `logging.root.level == WARNING` and the package logger's effective level is DEBUG. `test_logging_location.py`, `test_logging_encoding.py` and `test_mcp_http_sessions.py` (the only tests that read log records): 43 passed.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/logging/logging_instance.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_logging_location.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture.md`, `Progress.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-10 · 2026-09-23 · Review follow-ups: priming race, stub width, key-table contract · #done #review

- **What**: A code review of this session's whole range (`369067a..HEAD`, 93 files) confirmed the handler split is a pure move (640 function bodies byte-identical, no duplicates, `_factories.py` unchanged apart from its import block) and raised four things, all fixed here.
- **1. USB watcher, a second priming race**: after U-20260923-02 made a stopped-but-not-superseded run still prime, a poller that outlived `stop()`'s join could overwrite a snapshot `poll_once()` had written in the meantime — and the next poll would report that reading's devices as *removed*. Snapshot writes now bump `_snapshot_epoch`, and priming applies only if the epoch it read before enumerating is still current. `test_priming_does_not_overwrite_a_snapshot_taken_while_it_ran` fails on the previous commit (checked in a detached worktree) and passes now.
- **2. Stub generator width**: `MAX_LINE_LENGTH` bounded the `def` line only, so a handler with a 110+ character docstring summary would make the regenerated `actions.pyi` fail the `E501` gate enabled in the same batch. `_render_docstring` wraps instead; the test asserts the wrapped text still parses to the same summary.
- **3. Key tables were an unrecorded cross-project contract**: Jeffrey_RPA validates every key name a user types against `platform_wrapper.keyboard_keys_table` and reverse-looks-up recorded virtual keys through it, so removing 18 names from it (U-20260922-15) could have rejected a hotkey there. It does not — its own overrides cover the one name that mattered — but neither `architecture.md` §6 nor `test_cross_project_contracts.py` knew about the dependency. Both do now: shape and mouse names on every platform, the Windows key names on Windows (where Jeffrey_RPA runs and its own fixture skips elsewhere).
- **4. The delegation table's exemption was claimed under the wrong clause**: `CLAUDE.md`'s flat-data-tables clause covers "one mapping or list of entries", not 252 function definitions. `_handlers_executor_bridge.py` is listed as an ordinary over-limit exemption instead, with the reason it is not split further: the only further cut is by MCP factory domain (159 of them), which would scatter one uniform kind of delegation across a dozen files with no semantic boundary.
- **Result / numbers**: `test_usb_watcher.py` 14 tests, `test_stub_generator.py` 20, `test_cross_project_contracts.py` 53.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/usb/usb_watcher.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/stubs/generator.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_usb_watcher,test_stub_generator,test_cross_project_contracts}.py`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `Progress.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-12 · 2026-09-23 · Complexity limit measured in CI; the one function over it split · #done #quality

- **What**: `CLAUDE.md` set cyclomatic complexity at 10 and named `radon cc -nc` as the tool, but nothing ran it — it sat in the pre-commit list, "read by a human", which is how the file-length list had drifted too (U-20260923-11). Measured across the package: exactly **one** function was over the limit, `gui/triggers_tab.py::TriggersTab._on_combine`. So the limit was cheap to hold, and `test/unit_test/headless/test_complexity_budget.py` now measures it with the same `radon` a developer runs (added to `dev_requirements.txt` and to `quality.yml`'s install line, so it is a gate in CI rather than a skip).
- **The one function**: `_on_combine` read the selection, validated it, resolved ids to triggers, removed the originals and built the composite in one body. The selection is `_selected_ids`, the resolution is the module-level `children_to_combine(ids, triggers)` — no Qt, so the part worth testing is testable — and the mode-to-class mapping is the module constant `COMPOSITE_BY_MODE`. Behaviour is unchanged, including that ids the table still shows but the engine no longer has are dropped rather than raising.
- **Result / numbers**: `test_triggers_tab_combine.py` (7 tests) covers the combining logic, which had none — the tab's only tests were of the trigger engine behind it. `test_complexity_budget.py` 2 tests, one a positive control on synthetic code so an empty result cannot mean the measurement stopped working.

- **Open items**: none. Cognitive complexity, function length, parameter count and nesting depth are still review standards; `radon` measures the first of those four only.

## U-20260923-13 · 2026-09-23 · Flow-control audit: seven defects reproduced and fixed · #incident #executor

- **What**: A focused audit of `utils/executor/flow_control.py` and its dispatch path, each finding reproduced on a fresh `Executor()` before it was accepted. Seven were real: (1) `AC_parallel` turned a branch's failed assertion into `AutoControlActionException`, so it no longer propagated under `raise_on_error=False`; (2) an exhausted `AC_retry` did the same; (3) `AC_break` / `AC_continue` with no enclosing loop (or in a macro called outside one) escaped `execute_action` as a bare `Exception`, past every `AutoControlException` boundary, skipping the rest of the script; (4) macros had no depth bound — self-recursion ended in a `RecursionError` recorded at the deepest level with the top-level call looking successful, and two self-calls per body ran for ~2^depth; (5) a macro without a body, (6) an empty `AC_assert_duration` body or `AC_parallel` branch failed with "JSON action is null" instead of being the no-op every other block treats an empty body as; (7) `AC_wait_image` / `AC_wait_pixel` with `timeout <= 0` never probed.
- **Fixes**: assertions are kept per branch and re-raised as themselves after `join()`; `AC_retry` re-raises an assertion it could not outlast; the top-level (non-`_validated`) `execute_action` turns a stray loop signal into a recorded "outside a loop" failure while nested bodies still re-raise it to their loop; `AC_call_macro` counts depth per thread (`MAX_MACRO_DEPTH = 50`) and raises `MacroDepthExceeded`, which the executor lets unwind through nested bodies so it is recorded at the top — the same path as the loop signals; empty bodies go through `_run_branch`; the waits probe before checking the deadline.
- **Tests**: `test/unit_test/flow_control/test_flow_control_audit.py`, 14 tests; 11 fail on the previous commit (checked in a detached worktree), the other 3 are positive controls. `test_parallel_surfaces_a_failed_branch` had produced its failing branch with `[]` — exactly the inconsistency in (6) — and was passing on the up-front validation error's wording; it now uses a JSON-string `branches` so the failure happens inside the worker as intended.
- **Checked and not bugs**: `finally` runs on success, caught error, re-raise and break; break/continue inside if/try/retry reach the innermost loop; loop counts; nested-body interpolation is deferred; caller action lists are not mutated; parallel branches get isolated variable scopes.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/executor/{flow_control,flow_data_commands,action_executor}.py`, `test/unit_test/flow_control/{test_flow_control,test_flow_control_audit}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-14 · 2026-09-23 · WebRTC host audit: approval without token, dead sessions · #incident #security #remote-desktop

- **What**: A focused audit of the multi-viewer WebRTC host (`multi_viewer.py`, `webrtc_host.py`, `webrtc_host_auth.py`, `session_quality_cache.py`), which the restart-race work of U-20260922-12/13 had not covered. Five confirmed, plus one found while fixing them.
- **Security**: (1) `_approve_pending_viewer` guarded with `not self._has_pending_viewer and self._authenticated`; for a peer that never sent a token both are false, so the public `approve_pending_viewer` / `MultiViewerHost.approve_pending_viewer(sid)` authenticated it, cancelled its deadline and let it send input. The public path now requires a pending viewer, and the internal auto-approve paths (trust list, IP whitelist, no prompt hook) — reached only after the token matched — call a separate `_grant()`. (2) The token was compared with `!=`; it is `hmac.compare_digest` now.
- **Lifecycle**: (3) the 5-second auth deadline tore down a viewer that had the right token and was waiting on the user's Accept/Reject; it spares a pending viewer now. (4) Sessions whose peer went `failed`/`closed` stayed in `MultiViewerHost._sessions` — the state wrapper was skipped when no listener subscribed — so `session_count` only grew and the shared screen source was never released; the wrapper always exists and prunes on those two states (not `disconnected`, which ICE can recover from), on a thread because `stop_session` blocks on the bridge. (5) A `create_offer` that raised left a session whose id the caller never received; it is stopped before re-raising. (6) The host-service daemon did the same after the offer existed: every answer that did not arrive within 300 s left a session holding a source subscription, so an idle daemon leaked one every five minutes; `_serve_one_viewer` stops it on any failure.
- **Tests**: `test_webrtc_host_auth.py` +3 (approval without a token, the deadline sparing a pending viewer, the constant-time comparison), `test_remote_desktop_multi_viewer.py` +4 and one rewritten (`test_no_state_callback_means_no_wrapper_is_installed` pinned the behaviour behind (4)), `test_host_service_session_cleanup.py` (new, 3).
- **Checked and not bugs**: read-only viewers cannot inject input or SAS; file, mic and USB channels check authentication plus their permission; empty tokens are refused by both constructors; `SessionQualityCache` is locked; permission updates are atomic swaps; input dispatch exceptions are contained. A Python-3.10-only `concurrent.futures.TimeoutError` escape from `WebRTCDesktopHost.stop()` was reported unverified and is not changed here.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/{webrtc_host_auth,multi_viewer,host_service}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_webrtc_host_auth,test_remote_desktop_multi_viewer,test_host_service_session_cleanup}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-15 · 2026-09-23 · USB passthrough audit: ACL bypasses, credit stall, claim leaks · #incident #security #usb

- **What**: A focused audit of `utils/usb/passthrough/` — a remote viewer asks this host to claim a local USB device, so the ACL is the security boundary. Six confirmed, each reproduced by the auditor with the fake backend or by reading the path end to end.
- **ACL bypasses**: (1) ids were compared as lowercased strings in `AclRule.matches` but parsed with `int(x, 16)` by the libusb backend, so `0x1050` / `01050` / `10_50` missed a `1050` deny rule, fell to a default "allow" and opened 0x1050. New `acl.normalize_usb_id` (four lowercase hex digits, optional `0x`, anything else a `ValueError` → "bad OPEN payload") is applied to the OPEN request and used by `matches`. (2) A request without a serial skipped rules scoped to one serial; `_handle_open` now denies it when the ACL holds a serial rule for that vid/pid. (3) `WinusbBackend.open` logged the requested serial and opened the first vid/pid match; it raises instead, as its own comment said it should. (4) `length` / `timeout_ms` came from the wire unbounded (a 1 GiB bulk read was allocated, base64-encoded and fragmented into ~87k frames); `_bounded` caps them at 0xFFFF for control, 1 MiB for bulk/interrupt, 60 s.
- **Lifecycle**: (5) each reply sends the viewer a CREDIT grant but the host never raised its own `inbound_credits`, so a claim stopped after its initial 16 transfers and each "credit exhausted" counted as a strike toward lockout; the host now adds the grant to its count, and a malformed transfer refunds the credit it consumed. (6) `close_all()` had exactly one caller (the loopback); `UsbChannelHost` now releases every claim when its channel closes, and `WebRTCDesktopHost` does so in `_async_stop` and before a new offer replaces the channel — previously a device stayed open with its kernel HID drivers detached after the viewer left. (7) The claim-id counter wrapped onto live claims after 65,534 opens and orphaned the handle; ids in use are skipped.
- **Tests**: `test_usb_passthrough_audit.py` (new, 23). `test_credit_exhaustion_returns_error` had asserted the stall itself — "credit exhausted" after two sequential transfers — and now reaches exhaustion the only way it can legitimately happen, a viewer sending more than it was granted. The new file does not import on the previous commit (`normalize_usb_id` did not exist), so its tests could not be run there one by one.
- **Checked and not bugs**: a prompt error or a missing prompt callback counts as deny; transfers are limited to the viewer's own claim table.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/usb/passthrough/{acl,session,winusb_backend,webrtc_channel}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/webrtc_host.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_usb_passthrough,test_usb_passthrough_audit}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-16 · 2026-09-23 · MCP/REST audit: cross-session confirm, browser CSRF, token crash · #incident #security #mcp

- **What**: A focused audit of the REST server, the MCP HTTP transport (sessions, SSE, the destructive-tool confirmation) and the legacy socket server, each finding reproduced against a throwaway server on 127.0.0.1. It confirms the open item from the 2026-07 round-4 audit ("MCP-HTTP confirm-gate session-identity regression").
- **Fixes**: (1) replies to server-sent requests were looked up by id alone in one server-wide table, and ids ran `srv-1`, `srv-2`…, so session B could POST `accept` for a prompt shown to session A and A's destructive tool ran. Each pending slot now records the connection that sent it and `_dispatch_outbound_response` ignores a reply from any other; ids are `secrets.token_urlsafe`. (2) `_maybe_confirm_destructive` returned — i.e. ran the tool — when a client advertised elicitation but had no stream open; it raises -32000 now. (3) With no token (the default) the server accepted cross-site browser requests: `Content-Type: text/plain` needs no CORS preflight, so any page could run `tools/call`. `_authorize` now refuses a non-loopback `Origin` (unless listed in the new `JE_AUTOCONTROL_MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`) and, when bound to loopback, a non-loopback `Host` — a DNS-rebinding page reaches 127.0.0.1 under its own name, and its same-origin GET carries no `Origin`. The MCP specification requires the `Origin` check. (4) `compare_digest` raised `TypeError` on a non-ASCII `str`, and http.server decodes headers as latin-1, so `Authorization: Bearer é` killed the request thread in both MCP and REST (`rest_auth.constant_time_equal`) without a reply or a counted failure; both compare UTF-8 bytes. (5) The socket server let a `UnicodeDecodeError` from a non-UTF-8 payload escape its handlers; it decodes with `errors="replace"` so the bad input is answered as bad JSON.
- **Unchanged on purpose**: a client that does *not* advertise elicitation still runs destructive tools without a prompt when the confirmation variable is set — that path logs it and is the documented behaviour; the reported bypass was the advertised-but-unreachable case.
- **Tests**: `test_mcp_http_sessions.py` +9 (a forged cross-session accept, a stream-less confirmation, foreign `Origin`, rebound `Host`, loopback and allow-listed origins, a non-ASCII token over HTTP, the REST comparison), `test_r3_net_socket_rest.py` +1. `test_http_content_length.py` sent `Host: x` in its raw request and is now `Host: 127.0.0.1`.
- **Checked and fine**: REST routes other than `/health` and the static dashboard need the token; the REST rate limit keys on the socket address, not `X-Forwarded-For`; bodies are capped; the dashboard's asset lookup resists traversal; MCP session ids are 32 random bytes and never client-chosen; SSE events stay per session.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/mcp_server/{_client_requests,http_transport}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/rest_api/rest_auth.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/socket_server/auto_control_socket_server.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_mcp_http_sessions,test_r3_net_socket_rest,test_http_content_length}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/mcp_server/mcp_server_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-17 · 2026-09-23 · Scheduler/trigger audit: cron semantics, leap-day refire, lost edges · #incident #scheduler #triggers

- **What**: An audit of `utils/scheduler` and `utils/triggers`; every finding reproduced before the fix.
- **Cron** (`cron.py`): (1) day-of-month and day-of-week were ANDed even when both were restricted, so `0 0 1 * 1` meant "a Monday that is the 1st" instead of Vixie cron's "the 1st, and every Monday"; they are ORed when both are restricted and ANDed when either is `*`. (2) `7` for Sunday was rejected as out of range; it is accepted and folded to 0. (3) `5/15` expanded to just `5`; it is `5-max/15` now. (4) `next_match` searched one year minute by minute, and `0 0 29 2 *` has no match inside a year whenever the next leap day is further away; it walks day by day over eight years, which always contain a 29 February.
- **Scheduler** (`scheduler.py`): a `ValueError` from `next_match` escaped `_fire` with `next_run_ts` left in the past, so the job fired on every tick; the job is now removed and logged. `max_runs=0` was accepted and meant "run once"; `add_job` / `add_cron_job` refuse anything below 1.
- **Triggers**: (1) `AllOfTrigger` checked its children in list order and `all()` stopped at the first false one, so an edge child (cron minute, file change, sequence step) listed before a false level child had already spent its event — "at 09:00, if the image is on screen" never fired when the image appeared a few seconds into the minute. Children declare `consumes_on_check` and edges are checked last (composites inherit it from their children; duck-typed children default to level). (2) The IMAP fetch used `(RFC822)`, which sets `\Seen` (RFC 3501), so mail was marked read with `mark_seen=False` and before the script had run; it fetches `(BODY.PEEK[])`. (3) The webhook server caught a fixed tuple of four exception types around the script run; a `TypeError` reached `finally` with the status still 200, the run was recorded as a success and the connection dropped with no reply. Any `Exception` is recorded as a 500.
- **Tests**: `test_scheduler_trigger_audit.py` (new, 12).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/scheduler/{cron,scheduler}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/triggers/{trigger_engine,email_trigger,webhook_server}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_scheduler_trigger_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-18 · 2026-09-23 · Action signing audit: enforcement on every run path, key files, salted passphrases · #incident #security #signing

- **What**: An audit of `utils/action_signing` and the paths that run action files from disk.
- **Fixes**: (1) `JE_AUTOCONTROL_REQUIRE_SIGNED_ACTIONS` was honoured by `execute_files` alone; `je_auto_control run`, `python -m je_auto_control -e`, the scheduler, the trigger engine, e-mail triggers, webhooks, hotkeys, the MCP `execute_action_file` tool and the GUI's Execute button all ran unsigned files with enforcement on. They now load through the new `json_file.read_executable_action_json`. (2) `execute_files` verified one read of the file and parsed a second, so a file swapped in between ran unverified; `signer.read_signed_action_bytes` reads once, verifies those bytes and hands the same bytes to the parser. (3) Key files were written with the default mode and chmod-ed afterwards, and two processes creating one at once could each write a different key; the new `_key_file.load_or_create_key_file` creates it with `O_CREAT | O_EXCL` and 0600 in one call, and the loser of a race reads the winner's key. (4) An empty key file — left by a crash between create and write — was used as an empty HMAC key that anyone can reproduce; a key file under 32 bytes (44 for the Fernet key) is refused with instructions, and an empty explicit key or passphrase is refused. (5) A passphrase became the Fernet key through one unsalted SHA-256, so one guess tested every file encrypted with any passphrase; new files are `ACENC1:` + a 16-byte salt + the token, keyed by scrypt (N=2^15, r=8, p=1). Files in the old format still decrypt.
- **Unchanged on purpose**: `read_action_json` stays unverified — the editors, the linter, `validate` and code generation read files they do not run.
- **Tests**: `test_action_signing_audit.py` (new, 18). It does not import on the previous commit (`_key_file` did not exist). Six existing tests patched `read_action_json` on the modules that now import `read_executable_action_json`; only the patched name changed.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/action_signing/{_key_file,signer,cipher,__init__}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/json/json_file.py`, `je_auto_control/{__init__,__main__,cli}.py`, `je_auto_control/gui/_script_tab.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/{scheduler/scheduler,triggers/trigger_engine,triggers/email_trigger,triggers/webhook_server,hotkey/hotkey_daemon,executor/action_executor,mcp_server/tools/_handlers_runs}.py`, six tests, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v4_features_doc.rst`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-19 · 2026-09-23 · Secret handling: masked passphrases in logs, atomic vault rekey, unique backups · #incident #security #secrets

- **What**: The rest of the secrets / configuration audit, after U-20260923-18.
- **Fixes**: (1) `Executor.execute_action` logged every action list and keyed its record by `str(action)`, so `AC_secret_init` / `AC_secret_unlock` passphrases and `AC_secret_set` values went into the log file and back to every REST, MCP, socket and run-history caller in plain text; the new `utils/executor/action_redaction.py` masks the arguments of every `AC_secret_*` command, nested ones included, in the log lines, the record keys, the dry-run keys and the "cannot execute" error. The socket server logged the raw command text too and now logs its length. (2) `SecretManager.change_passphrase` deleted the vault and re-added each secret with its own write, so an error or crash part-way lost every secret not yet re-added; the new vault is built in memory and written once, atomically. The vault's temporary file is created 0600 rather than chmod-ed after the rename. (3) Config-bundle backups were named `<file>.bak.<unix seconds>`, and a second import in the same second overwrote the first backup — the one holding the user's original file; `_unused_backup_path` appends `.N` until the name is free. (4) `SecretStoreError` derived from `RuntimeError` alone, outside the `AutoControlException` family the containment boundaries catch; it is now both.
- **Tests**: `test_secret_handling_audit.py` (new, 8). The redaction tests were checked to see this logger's records (a probe asserting an unmasked argument is captured), so they cannot pass on an empty log.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/executor/{action_redaction,action_executor}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/socket_server/auto_control_socket_server.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/secrets/secret_store.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/config_bundle/config_bundle.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_secret_handling_audit.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-20 · 2026-09-23 · CI actions moved off the deprecated Node 20 runtime · #ci #maintenance

- **What**: Every job logged "Node.js 20 is deprecated … forced to run on Node.js 24" for `actions/checkout@v4` and `actions/setup-python@v5`. Each action was bumped to the first major whose `action.yml` declares `node24` (read from the tag, not assumed): `checkout@v5`, `setup-python@v6`, `upload-artifact@v6`, `download-artifact@v7`, `dependency-review-action@v5`, `docker/build-push-action@v7`, `docker/setup-buildx-action@v4`.
- **Breaking notes checked**: all require runner ≥ 2.327.1 (GitHub-hosted runners are). `download-artifact@v5` changed the path of single downloads *by ID*; `release.yml` downloads by name. `build-push-action@v7` dropped `DOCKER_BUILD_NO_SUMMARY` / `DOCKER_BUILD_EXPORT_RETENTION_DAYS` and `setup-buildx-action@v4` deprecated inputs; neither is used. `pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish` stays pinned to its commit.
- **Files**: `.github/workflows/{action-json-lint,docker,platform-smoke,quality,release,stable}.yml`, `docs/source/getting_started/run_in_ci.rst`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-21 · 2026-09-23 · Report audit: control characters, silent write failures · #incident #reports

- **What**: An audit of `utils/generate_report`, the JUnit/Allure writers in `utils/test_suite` and the XML builder they use; every finding reproduced first.
- **Fixes**: (1) ElementTree writes the characters XML 1.0 forbids (`\x01`, `\x0b`, the ESC of an ANSI colour code) as they are. The XML report then raised `ExpatError` while pretty-printing — outside the `AutoControlException` family, so it aborted a script run with `raise_on_error=False` — and a JUnit file with a coloured failure message was rejected by every CI system. The new `xml_safe_text` in `change_xml_structure.py` replaces them with U+FFFD; `dict_to_elements_tree` applies it to every text and attribute, and `to_junit_xml` to every name and message. (2) The HTML, JSON and XML report writers logged an `OSError` and returned as on success, so scripts went on as if the report existed; they write atomically (`atomic_write_text`) and raise `AutoControlHTMLException` / `AutoControlGenerateJsonReportException` / `XMLException`. The per-call `Lock()` objects in the JSON and XML writers, which serialised nothing, are gone. (3) `record_action_to_list` applied `repr()` to exception text that callers had already `repr()`-ed, so every report showed `"ValueError('boom')"` in extra quotes; strings are stored as given.
- **Checked and fine**: the HTML report escapes every recorded field; success/failure is decided on `"None"`, which `repr(None)` still produces; JUnit counts come from the result properties; the compliance report escapes everything it interpolates.
- **Tests**: `test_report_audit.py` (new, 6; all 6 fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/generate_report/{generate_html_report,generate_json_report,generate_xml_report}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/xml/change_xml_structure/change_xml_structure.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/test_suite/reports.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/test_record/record_test_class.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_report_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-22 · 2026-09-23 · Data-command audit: escaping errors, redirect egress bypass, Windows shell quoting · #incident #security #executor

- **What**: An audit of the flow data commands (`flow_data_commands.py`), `utils/data_source`, `utils/sql`, `utils/http_client`, `utils/pdf`, the TOTP helper and `utils/script_vars`; every finding reproduced first.
- **Errors that escaped the executor**: `_run_one_action` contains the `AutoControlException` family plus `OSError`, `RuntimeError`, `AttributeError`, `TypeError`, `ValueError` and `LookupError`. Seven errors were none of those, so one bad step aborted the whole script under `raise_on_error=False`: `sqlite3.Error` from `AC_sql_to_var` / `AC_assert_db` (`sql_query.py`) and from a SQLite data source (`data_source.py`), `csv.Error` from a CSV field over 128 KiB, `re.error` from an invalid pattern in `AC_transform_var`, `pypdf`'s `PyPdfError` from a corrupt file, a `ZeroDivisionError` from `AC_otp_to_var` with `step=0`, and `http.client.HTTPException` from a malformed HTTP reply. The first five are re-raised as `AutoControlActionException`; TOTP now refuses `step <= 0` and `digits` outside 1..10 with `TOTPError`; the HTTP transport raises the malformed reply as `urllib.error.URLError`, the `OSError` every other transport failure already is.
- **Egress policy bypass**: `http_request` checked only the first URL against the egress policy, and urllib then followed any redirect — to another host, or to `ftp://`. A `_CheckedRedirectHandler` applies the scheme allow-list and the policy to every hop.
- **Windows shell strings**: `AC_shell_to_var` split a string command with `shlex` in non-POSIX mode, which keeps the quote characters in each token, and `subprocess` quoted them again, so a quoted argument reached the child as a string literal. On Windows the string now goes to `CreateProcess` unchanged (still without a shell). Output was always decoded as UTF-8, which garbles a console program's output in the locale code page; it is decoded with the new `encoding` argument, defaulting to the locale's encoding, as `subprocess` text mode does.
- **Checked and fine**: SQL is always parameterised and read-only; the shell never uses `shell=True`; `${...}` interpolation is a single pass, so a value holding `${secrets.X}` stays literal; HTTP always has a timeout and only http/https.
- **Not changed**: `AC_http_to_var` reads the whole body and `AC_read_file_to_var` has no size bound. Both are design choices rather than defects found here.
- **Tests**: `test_data_commands_audit.py` (new, 10; all 10 fail on the previous commit). `test_http_client.py` stubbed `urllib.request.urlopen`, which the transport no longer calls; it stubs the module opener (`_OPENER.open`) with the same fakes.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{sql/sql_query,data_source/data_source,http_client/http_client,pdf/pdf_reader,remote_desktop/totp,executor/flow_data_commands}.py`, `je_auto_control/gui/script_builder/command_schema.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v4_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_data_commands_audit,test_http_client}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-23 · 2026-09-23 · File transfer / admin audit: partial files, size limits, device names, poll crash · #incident #security #remote-desktop

- **What**: An audit of the remote-desktop file transfer (`file_transfer.py`, the WebRTC inbox in `webrtc_files.py`), `utils/admin` and `utils/package_manager`; every finding reproduced first.
- **Plain-socket receiver** (`FileReceiver`): (1) `FILE_BEGIN` opened the destination with `"wb"`, truncating an existing file before any data arrived, so a failed transfer destroyed the original and left a partial file. It writes `.<name>.<id>.part` and renames it into place only on success. (2) Chunks past the announced size were written and the transfer still succeeded; they now fail it. (3) A transfer whose sender reported success with fewer bytes than announced was a success; it fails with "received N of M bytes". (4) A second `FILE_BEGIN` with an active transfer id replaced the entry and leaked the first handle; it is ignored. (5) `mkdir` ran outside the `try`, and the viewer / host-client loops catch only `FileTransferError`, so a NUL in the name (`ValueError`) or a protected directory killed the receive thread — on the host without `stop()`; both now fail the one transfer.
- **WebRTC inbox** (`FileTransferReceiver`): (6) no check compared bytes written with the announced size in either direction; excess data and a short `file_end` are errors. (7) The target was opened `"wb"` at `file_begin`; a `.part` file replaces it only when complete, so a failed upload keeps the earlier copy. (8) `_safe_basename` accepted Windows device names and a trailing dot or space: `nul` went to the null device yet was reported and audited as received, and `report. ` overwrote `report`; those names and control characters are refused. (9) A non-object envelope or a non-numeric, null or infinite `size` raised `AttributeError` / `ValueError` / `TypeError` / `OverflowError` into aiortc's message callback; they are protocol errors.
- **Admin client**: (10) `http.client.HTTPException` (a garbage status line, a truncated body) is no `OSError`, so one misbehaving host escaped `pool.map` and failed `poll_all`, `fetch_thumbnails` and `broadcast_execute` for every host — and left the GUI tab waiting, since its worker emits neither signal then. It is caught with the rest. (11) `{"hosts": 5}` in the address book still crashed the constructor; a non-list is ignored with a warning.
- **Package manager**: (12) `find_spec` ran outside the `try`, and only `ModuleNotFoundError` was caught, so a missing parent package or a module with a syntax error escaped `check_package`; both are logged and return `None`.
- **Family**: both `FileTransferError` classes derived from `RuntimeError` alone; they are `AutoControlException`s too.
- **Not changed, recorded in `Progress.md`**: the viewer writes a host-pushed file wherever the host names (`send_file_to_viewers` is documented with an absolute path), so confining it changes a documented contract.
- **Checked and fine**: the admin address book is saved atomically and 0600; only http/https host URLs; the inbox strips directories and drive prefixes; one inbox transfer at a time, rate-limited, only for authenticated viewers with files allowed; package names are validated.
- **Tests**: `test_file_transfer_audit.py` (new, 24; 21 fail on the previous commit, the other 3 guard the success paths and a name Windows already refused).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/{file_transfer,webrtc_files}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/admin/admin_client.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/package_manager/package_manager_class.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_file_transfer_audit.py`, `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: the viewer-side path decision in `Progress.md`.

## U-20260923-24 · 2026-09-23 · Outbound audit: credentials across redirects, egress bypass, chat-ops screenshot path · #incident #security #chatops

- **What**: An audit of the outbound integrations (`utils/failure_hooks`, `utils/chatops`, `utils/notify_channels`, `utils/events`, `utils/email_send`, `utils/observability`); every finding reproduced against servers on 127.0.0.1.
- **Fixes**: (1) urllib copies every request header onto a redirect, so after U-20260923-22's redirect check a cross-host redirect still handed the `Authorization` header to the other host. `_CheckedRedirectHandler` now drops `Authorization`, `Cookie` and `Proxy-Authorization` when the host changes. (2) The Jira / Linear / GitHub backends (`failure_hooks/backends.py`) and the Slack bot (`chatops/slack_bot.py`) called `urllib.request.urlopen` themselves, so the egress policy did not apply to them. Both go through the new `http_client.perform_call`, the egress check plus transport that `http_request` now uses too, with redirects refused (`call["follow_redirects"] = False`): urllib turned a ticket POST's 302 into a GET that carried the credentials to the other host, and counted that reply as a created ticket. A non-2xx ticket reply is a failure with its status. (3) A reply that was valid JSON but not an object raised `AttributeError`: out of `FailureHookManager.fire()`, and out of `SlackBot.run_forever`, which catches only `SlackError`, ending the poll loop. Both treat it as a failed call; non-object message items are skipped. (4) `/screenshot <path>` wrote the PNG to any path in the chat message, and no default command requires a role. The argument now keeps only its file name, written into `context['screenshot_dir']` (default `je_auto_control_chatops` in the temp directory). (5) `SlackError` and `ChatOpsError` join the `AutoControlException` family, keeping their old bases.
- **Checked and fine**: `notify_channels` and `events.post_cloudevent` already used `http_request`; e-mail rejects CR/LF in headers and verifies STARTTLS; every direct call had a timeout; Slack backoff is capped; `/run` is held to `script_root`.
- **Tests**: `test_outbound_audit.py` (new, 9; 8 fail on the previous commit, the same-host redirect one is a guard). `test_failure_hooks.py` stubbed the global `urlopen`, which the backends no longer call; it stubs `http_client.urllib_transport`. `test_chatops_bot.py`'s screenshot test passes a name and a `screenshot_dir`.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/http_client/{http_client,__init__}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/failure_hooks/backends.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/chatops/{slack_bot,router,handlers}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_outbound_audit,test_failure_hooks,test_chatops_bot}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v2_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-25 · 2026-09-23 · SQLite store audit: stuck work items, dedupe race, leaked connections, audit chain · #incident #stores

- **What**: The SQLite half of an audit of the persistent stores (`work_queue`, `checkpoint`, `agent_memory`, `run_history`, the remote-desktop audit log); every finding reproduced with HOME pointed at a temp directory. The JSON-file stores are the next entry.
- **Work queue**: (1) a performer that crashed after `get_next` left its item `in_progress` for good, and since a live duplicate blocks `add`, it could not be enqueued again either — against the module's "resumable after a crash". `get_next(stale_after_s=...)` also claims an item left in progress that long; `AC_queue_next`, `ac_queue_next` and the Script Builder take the same optional argument. (2) The dedupe check and the insert in `add` ran in autocommit, so two dispatchers could both pass the check; they share one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. (3) `fail` on an unknown id returned `"new"` — requeued — having updated nothing; it raises.
- **Connections**: `WorkQueue`, `CheckpointStore` and `AgentMemory` used `with sqlite3.connect(...) as conn`, which commits but never closes, leaking a connection per call (17 `ResourceWarning`s in the reproduction). The three identical `_connect` methods now return the new `sqlite_support.autocommit_connection`, which closes.
- **Errors outside the family**: `sqlite3.Error` derives from `Exception` alone. From `HistoryStore` it escaped the Windows hotkey listener's thread and unregistered every hotkey (a corrupt file, or a lock held past the timeout); the new `sqlite_support.sqlite_errors_as` decorator re-raises it as `HistoryStoreError` from every store method. `AuditLog()` on a corrupt file raises `AuditLogError`.
- **Audit chain**: `AuditLog` cached the chain head per instance, so two writers on one `audit.db` (the host service and the GUI) linked rows to stale predecessors and `verify_chain` failed. `log` reads the head inside its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction.
- **Checked and fine**: `HistoryStore` locks and uses `check_same_thread=False`; ordering and limits are right; audit-log pruning keeps the chain verifiable; `get_next` claims atomically; `max_retries` gives the documented count.
- **Tests**: `test_sqlite_store_audit.py` (new, 8). It does not import on the previous commit (`HistoryStoreError` and `AuditLogError` are new).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/sqlite_support.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/{work_queue/work_queue,checkpoint/checkpoint,agent_memory/agent_memory,run_history/history_store,remote_desktop/audit_log}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/executor/action_executor.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/mcp_server/tools/{_handlers,_factories}.py`, `je_auto_control/gui/script_builder/command_schema.py`, `je_auto_control/actions.pyi` (regenerated), `test/unit_test/headless/test_sqlite_store_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-26 · 2026-09-23 · JSON store audit: in-place rewrites, constructor crashes, lost counts and log lines · #incident #stores

- **What**: The JSON-file half of the persistent-store audit (U-20260923-25 was the SQLite half).
- **Fixes**: (1) `QuarantineStore`, `TrustList`, `KnownHosts` and `UserStore` rewrote their file in place with `write_text` and chmod-ed it afterwards: in the reproduction 166 of 200 concurrent readers saw a partial quarantine file and 126 of 200 a partial trust list, and the file was briefly readable by others. They write with `atomic_write_text`, whose `mkstemp` file is 0600 from the start. (2) Their loaders caught only `JSONDecodeError`, so a file that is not UTF-8 raised `UnicodeDecodeError` from the constructor; they catch `ValueError`. The quarantine loader also crashed on a top-level list and now checks the shape. (3) `ABStore._load_if_needed` set `_loaded` before reading, so one transient `PermissionError` (antivirus, a file lock) left the cache empty and the next `record()` overwrote every stored count — 50 successes became 1. It is set only after a successful read. (4) The cost-telemetry and self-healing JSON-lines logs appended with `open("a")`: after a torn last line the next record joined it and was lost as well. The new `json_store.append_json_line` terminates a torn line first.
- **Not fixed here, recorded in `Progress.md`**: the governance approval gate, the asset store and the locator-repair store each write back the whole dict their instance loaded, so two processes overwrite each other's updates (two checkers deciding one request both succeed). That needs a cross-process lock.
- **Tests**: `test_json_store_audit.py` (new, 11). It does not import on the previous commit (`append_json_line` is new).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/json_store/json_store.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/{quarantine/store,remote_desktop/trust_list,remote_desktop/fingerprint,rbac/users,ab_locator/store,cost_telemetry/store,self_healing/heal_log}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_json_store_audit.py`, `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: the cross-process lock in `Progress.md`.

## U-20260923-27 · 2026-09-23 · CLI / hotkey / recording audit: exit codes, lost recordings, wrong virtual keys · #incident #cli #hotkey

- **What**: An audit of `cli.py`, the legacy `__main__.py`, the recording wrapper and recorder base, and `utils/hotkey`, with the platform input stubbed.
- **CLI**: (1) `run` returned 0 even when actions failed — the executor records a failure and goes on — so CI could not tell a failed run from a clean one. The executor now counts, per thread, the failures it records (`reset_recorded_failures` / `recorded_failures` in `action_executor.py`; failures that propagate, e.g. inside `AC_try`, are left to whoever catches them), and `run` exits 1 with "N action(s) failed". (2) `failure-bundle --context '[1]'` ended in a `TypeError` traceback; a non-object context is an error message. (3) A recording whose hook could not start still wrote `[]` over the output file and reported "Recorded 0 action(s)" with status 0. `record()` returns whether it started and `record_to_json` raises instead of writing; a negative `--duration` is refused.
- **Legacy entry point** (a cross-project contract; flags and the success exit code are unchanged): the `except` did not include `AutoControlException`, so a missing or invalid action file ended in a raw traceback — exit status 1 already, so only the output changes. A `-d` path that is not a directory ran nothing and exited 0; it is an error now. `architecture.md` §6 says both.
- **Recording**: `InputRecorder._start` replaced a running hook without stopping it, leaving the first low-level hook and its message pump installed for the process's life (`AC_record` twice, or `record_to_json` after a stray `record()`); it stops the old one.
- **Hotkeys**: (1) `_key_to_vk` used `ord()` for any single character, but a virtual-key code equals the code point only for A-Z and 0-9: `ctrl+.` registered VK_DELETE, `ctrl+[` VK_LWIN, `ctrl+,` VK_SNAPSHOT. The US-layout `VK_OEM_*` codes are mapped and other characters refused. (2) `bind` accepted combos `RegisterHotKey` cannot take (`ctrl+foo`); on Windows it validates with `parse_combo`. (3) The Windows backend retried a failed registration on every 50 ms tick, logging an error 20 times a second while another program owned the combo; a failed binding is retried only when its combo changes. (4) `_fire_binding` recorded the run in history outside its `try`, so a history failure ended the listener thread, and it caught a fixed tuple around an injected executor, so a `KeyError` did the same; history failures are logged and the executor call is a documented broad catch. `fired` is counted under the lock.
- **Checked and fine**: `run`, `validate`, `fmt` and `codegen` report bad files cleanly; the callback executor keeps trigger and callback errors apart; recorded actions validate and replay; timeline gaps are clamped; the recording editor's operations bound-check and do not mutate their input.
- **Tests**: `test_cli_hotkey_audit.py` (new, 21; 18 fail on the previous commit, the others guard a clean run and the A-Z / 0-9 mapping). `test_cli.py`'s record stub returns `True`, as `record()` now does.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/{cli,__main__}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/executor/action_executor.py`, `je_auto_control/wrapper/auto_control_record.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/input_macro/recorder_base.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/hotkey/{hotkey_daemon,backends/windows_backend}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_cli_hotkey_audit,test_cli}.py`, `README.md`, `README/README_zh-{TW,CN}.md`, `architecture.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-28 · 2026-09-23 · Shared JSON stores lock and re-read across processes · #done #stores

- **What**: The `Progress.md` item recorded in U-20260923-26. `ApprovalGate`, `AssetStore` and `RepairStore` loaded their JSON file once and wrote back their own copy on every change, so processes sharing the file overwrote each other: two checkers deciding one approval request were both told they had succeeded, and an asset or suggestion another process had added vanished on the next write — although the gate's docstring promises maker and checker may be separate processes.
- **How**: the new `json_store.SharedJsonDict` wraps the file. `update(mutate)` holds `<file>.lock` (created with `O_CREAT | O_EXCL`; a lock older than 30 s is a dead process's and is taken over; waiting over 10 s raises `TimeoutError`), re-reads the file, applies the change and writes it atomically; `read()` reads it afresh.
