The gate's decision checks `pending` on the fresh data inside the lock, so a request is decided once. Without a path the dict stays in memory, as before. On Windows a lock file being deleted is reported as `PermissionError`, which the loop treats as contention (found by the concurrency test, 19 of 20 writes before that).
- **Tests**: `test_shared_json_dict.py` (new, 8; 7 fail on the previous commit, the in-memory one is a guard). The three stores' existing tests pass unchanged.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/json_store/{json_store,__init__}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/{governance/governance,assets/assets,locator_repair/locator_repair}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_shared_json_dict.py`, `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-29 · 2026-09-23 · Codegen injection, USB/IP device scope, TLS key writes, signaling and relay limits · #incident #security

- **What**: The security findings of three audits run in parallel (logic engines, `usbip` / `tls_acme` / `config_sync` / `resilience`, remote-desktop signaling and relay); the robustness findings of the same audits are the next entries.
- **Codegen** (`codegen.py`): (1) parameter *names* from the action file went into `key=value` source text as written, so a key such as `x=1)\nimport os; os.system(...)` put statements into the generated test; only identifier, non-keyword keys become keyword arguments, anything else takes the `ac.execute_action([...])` fall-back where it is a string literal. (2) The Robot target embedded the actions in `r'''...'''`, which a value containing `'''` closed, running the rest inside `Evaluate`; the payload is base64, and the test name is collapsed onto one line. (3) `failure_bundle=True` imported `je_auto_control.api` *as* `ac`, and `api` has none of the facade functions the calls style emits, so every generated test failed with `AttributeError` while compiling cleanly — which is all its test checked; the test now also checks each `ac.<name>` exists.
- **USB/IP** (`usbip/server.py`): the devid in each `CMD_SUBMIT` reached the backend unchecked, and `LibUsbBackend` caches every enumerated device, so importing one device gave URB access to all of them. The URB loop is bound to the imported device's `(busnum << 16) | devnum` and answers any other with `-ENODEV`. Two tests sent the bare devnum (`2`), which the libusb backend never routed; they send the full devid.
- **TLS keys** (`tls_acme/keys.py`): `save_pem` wrote the private key with `write_bytes` (0644 under a 022 umask) and chmod-ed it afterwards, in place. The new `json_store.atomic_write_bytes` writes it through a 0600 temp file and a rename, byte for byte (the text variant would have turned the PEM's newlines into CRLF on Windows).
- **Signaling server**: the shared secret was compared with `!=`; it uses `hmac.compare_digest`. Any client allowed to post could create sessions without limit (200 held 48 MB for the TTL); new sessions beyond 1,024 get 503.
- **Relay**: a parked peer stayed in the pending table until its partner came or the relay stopped, so once `max_pending_sessions` peers had parked and left, every new session was refused for good. Before the capacity check, entries whose socket reports EOF or that waited over five minutes are dropped, and a departed partner is replaced rather than paired.
- **Tests**: `test_codegen_injection.py` (new, 4; all fail on the previous commit), `test_network_edge_audit.py` (new, 6; it does not import on the previous commit, `atomic_write_bytes` being new). `test_codegen.py`, `test_usbip.py` and `test_r3_rdusb_usbip.py` updated as described.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{codegen/codegen,usbip/server,tls_acme/keys,json_store/json_store,remote_desktop/signaling_server,remote_desktop/relay}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_codegen_injection,test_network_edge_audit,test_codegen,test_usbip,test_r3_rdusb_usbip}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-30 · 2026-09-23 · Remote-desktop robustness: dead receive threads, blocked accept thread, signaling errors · #incident #remote-desktop

- **What**: The robustness findings of the remote-desktop network audit (the security ones are U-20260923-29).
- **Receive threads**: (1) an INPUT message with a missing field (`KeyError`), an infinite or non-numeric coordinate (`OverflowError` / `ValueError` from `int()`), or an unknown key or button name (the wrappers' `AutoControlException`) escaped the host's catch; the receive thread died without `stop()`, so the handler still counted as an authenticated client, ignored all later input and — with `max_clients=1` — locked out every new viewer. `dispatch_input` turns conversion errors into `InputDispatchError`, the host catches the wrappers' framework errors, and `_recv_loop` calls `stop()` before letting anything else propagate. (2) `WsProtocolError` was a bare `RuntimeError`, so one TEXT or fragmented frame killed the WebSocket host's receive thread the same way and the viewer's silently (`on_error` never ran); it is a `ProtocolError`, which both loops already end a connection on.
- **Accept thread**: the TLS handshake, the WS upgrade and authentication ran on the one accept thread, each read bounded by a 60 s *per-read* timeout, so a peer sending a byte just inside each timeout kept everyone else from connecting (8.6 s and counting in the reproduction). Each connection is handshaken on its own `rd-handshake` thread, at most 32 at once; beyond that new connections are closed.
- **Signaling client**: a read timeout (`TimeoutError`) or a server hanging up (`RemoteDisconnected`) is no `URLError`, so they left `_request` as themselves while the GUI workers catch only `SignalingError`; they are wrapped too.
- **Tests**: `test_rd_robustness_audit.py` (new, 8; all fail on the previous commit). The 360 existing remote-desktop tests pass unchanged, including the two that pin the stop-during-handshake window.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/{host,host_client,input_dispatch,ws_protocol,signaling_client}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_rd_robustness_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-31 · 2026-09-23 · Logic-engine audit: DAG failures, FSM guards and timers, RRULE semantics, suite variables · #incident #executor

- **What**: The logic-engine findings of the parallel audit (codegen, the other finding of that audit, is U-20260923-29).
- **DAG** (`dag/runner.py`): (1) the default local runner called `execute_action` with the default `raise_on_error=False`, so a node whose actions all failed counted as succeeded and its dependants ran; it runs with `raise_on_error=True` (loading an action file through the verifying loader). (2) A runner error outside `AutoControlException` / `RuntimeError` / `OSError` / `ValueError` — a `KeyError`, a `TypeError` — escaped `run_dag` and left the node "running"; both catches use the executor's containment set. (3) The harvest waited on the oldest submitted node, so a node whose inputs a faster sibling had produced waited for an unrelated slow one; it takes whichever finishes first.
- **State machine** (`state_machine/engine.py`): (1) the docstring promised `if_var`, `if_image_found` and `if_pixel`, none implemented, so such a transition fired unconditionally. `if_image_found` is implemented (a template path or `{"image", "detect_threshold"}`, not-found meaning the guard does not fire); any other `if_*` key raises. (2) `after` was measured from the start of the run and evaluated once, so a state whose exit was a timer failed at once; it counts from state entry and the machine waits for the earliest timer — failing at once only when no timer can fire before `global_timeout_s`. (3) The step budget was checked before the final-state check, so reaching the final state on the last allowed step raised. The package docstring no longer promises a `retry` key that was never implemented.
- **Recurrence** (`recurrence/recurrence.py`): (1) YEARLY rules without BYMONTH were confined to dtstart's month: `BYMONTHDAY=1` gave one date a year and `BYDAY=-1FR` the last Friday of January. Per RFC 5545 they cover the whole year, and BYDAY ordinals without BYMONTH count within the year (`20MO` is the 20th Monday). (2) A rule that can never match (`BYMONTH=2;BYMONTHDAY=30`) scanned until the calendar overflowed, and the `OverflowError` escaped the executor; series stop 400 years past dtstart. (3) INTERVAL and COUNT below 1, BYMONTH outside 1..12, BYMONTHDAY outside ±1..31 and non-numeric parts raise `AutoControlException`; `count=0` yields nothing.
- **Suite runner**: a data-driven row variable stayed set after its case; the previous value is restored.
- **Executor**: `ArithmeticError` (`OverflowError`, `ZeroDivisionError`) joins the containment set, so such a failure is recorded like any other instead of aborting the script. `run_assertion_spec` reports an unknown spec key as `AutoControlAssertionException`, as it does an unknown kind.
- **Tests**: `test_logic_engine_audit.py` (new, 18; 16 of the 17 that predate `if_image_found` fail on the previous commit, the other guards the default-month case). The existing FSM test with a 999 s timer against a 300 s budget still fails at once.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{dag/runner,state_machine/engine,state_machine/__init__,recurrence/recurrence,test_suite/runner,assertion/combinators,executor/action_executor}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_logic_engine_audit.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v6_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-32 · 2026-09-23 · Circuit-breaker trials, config-sync and ACME error handling · #incident #resilience

- **What**: The remaining findings of the `usbip` / `tls_acme` / `config_sync` / `resilience` audit (the USB/IP and TLS-key ones are U-20260923-29).
- **Circuit breaker** (`resilience.py`): it had no lock and no trial token, so every caller that arrived while half-open ran — 20 concurrent trials in the reproduction, against the service the breaker exists to protect, and the shared breakers behind `AC_circuit_call` were affected. State is guarded by a lock and a half-open circuit admits one trial; others get `CircuitOpenError` until it ends. A failed trial reopens the circuit.
- **Config sync** (`config_sync/client.py`): (1) `ConfigBucket.from_dict` trusted the server's reply: a list where a section belongs, a string where an entry belongs, or a non-numeric `revision` / `last_modified` raised `AttributeError` or `ValueError` there or later in `merge_buckets`. Sections and entries must be mappings and stamps finite numbers, or it raises `ConfigSyncError`. (2) A server hanging up (`RemoteDisconnected`) or a stalled body (`TimeoutError` from `read()`) is no `URLError` and left the client as itself; wrapped too. (3) `upsert` keeps a `last_modified` already in the entry (a tested feature), so an entry read back from a bucket and edited keeps its old stamp and loses the merge; the docstring now says to drop it.
- **ACME**: `_http` did not catch transport failures, so an unreachable CA raised `URLError`; they are `AcmeError`. An unparsable CSR raised a plain `ValueError` from `cryptography`; it is `JwsError`. `renewal_due` raised `TypeError` for a naive `now`, which it now reads as UTC like the certificate.
- **Not fixed here, recorded in `Progress.md`**: config sync has no tombstones, so an entry removed locally returns on the next sync; fixing it changes the synced format.
- **Tests**: `test_resilience_sync_audit.py` (new, 11; 10 fail on the previous commit, the reopen-on-failed-trial one is a guard).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{resilience/resilience,config_sync/client,acme_v2/client,acme_v2/jws,tls_acme/renewal}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_resilience_sync_audit.py`, `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: config-sync tombstones in `Progress.md`.

## U-20260923-33 · 2026-09-23 · Agent audit: broken computer-use actions, unoffered tools, VLM errors · #incident #security #agent

- **What**: An audit of `utils/agent`, `utils/llm` and `utils/vision` with every model client stubbed; no network calls, no real input.
- **Computer-use translations** (`anthropic_computer_use.py`): the tool runner calls commands from `event_dict` by keyword, and most translations could not be called: every click passed a `repeat` argument `AC_click_mouse` does not take; `wait` named `AC_sleep` and `left_click_drag` `AC_drag`, neither in `event_dict`; `hold_key` passed `keycode` / `duration` where `AC_hold_key` takes `key` / `duration_s`. The existing tests passed because they compared translations with expected dicts and never bound them to the real commands. Single clicks call `AC_click_mouse` alone; multi-clicks, drags (press, move, release) and waits run as one `AC_execute_action` sequence with `raise_on_error=True`. The new test binds every translation — and every action inside a sequence — against the real signatures; the old dict-comparison tests were updated to the new shapes.
- **Unoffered tools**: both Messages-API backends passed whatever tool name the model returned to the executor, which runs any `AC_*` command, so offering one click tool did not stop a reply naming `AC_shell_command` (reproduced with the shell command stubbed). `base.require_offered` rejects a name that is not in the backend's own tool list; computer-use rejects any tool but `computer` instead of treating the turn as a successful final answer.
- **Malformed model output**: OpenAI arguments that did not parse became `{}` and the tool still ran (a truncated click became a click at the cursor), and valid JSON that was not an object crashed the run from `dict()`; both raise `AgentBackendError`. A computer-use coordinate that is not a finite number raises it instead of `ValueError` / `TypeError`, and coordinates are clamped to the declared display (`[-500, 99999]` on 100x100 was dispatched as given).
- **VLM**: both backends caught `(OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError)`, but the SDKs' own errors (rate limit, timeout, 5xx) derive from `anthropic.AnthropicError` / `openai.OpenAIError`, bare `Exception`s, so they escaped — the LLM backend already caught them. They are caught (looked up lazily, so an injected client without the SDK still works). A reply outside the requested region was translated and clicked (region 100x100, point at y=5100); it counts as not found.
- **Family**: `AgentBackendError`, `LLMNotAvailableError` and `VLMNotAvailableError` derived from `RuntimeError` alone; they are `AutoControlException`s too.
- **Recorded in `Progress.md`**: `AC_run_agent` offers the model every command, shell and process execution included; narrowing that default changes capability, so it is the maintainer's call.
- **Checked and fine**: step and wall-clock budgets, parallel tool calls disabled, truncated replies rejected, tool results capped, the LLM planner validates against an allowlist, no key reaches a log or message, VLM temp screenshots are deleted.
- **Tests**: `test_agent_audit.py` (new, 29; 20 fail on the previous commit, the other 9 guard translations that already worked). `test_anthropic_computer_use.py`, `test_r3_agent_computer_use.py`, `test_agent_backends.py` (the malformed-arguments test had asserted the `{}` fallback) and `test_r3_agent_openai_backend.py` (its fake tool list now names the tool its fake model calls) updated.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/agent/backends/{base,anthropic,openai,anthropic_computer_use}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/vision/{vlm_api,backends/anthropic_backend,backends/openai_backend,backends/base}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/llm/backends/base.py`, five test files, `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: the `AC_run_agent` default in `Progress.md`.

## U-20260923-34 · 2026-09-23 · Wrapper audit: template-match accuracy, drawn multi-match, multi-monitor scroll, ctypes truncation · #incident #wrapper #image

- **What**: An audit of `wrapper/` and the template matching behind it, run on synthetic frames and recording fakes (no real input or capture).
- **Template matching** (`cv2_utils/template_detection.py` now matches itself; `je_open_cv` only normalises the inputs): (1) the single-match path returned the first position over the threshold in scan order — the topmost-leftmost near miss — so centres were 1–5 px off the real match at thresholds below 1 and clicks landed beside the target; it takes the `minMaxLoc` peak. (2) `TM_CCOEFF_NORMED` is float32, so a pixel-identical template scores 0.99999905 and the default threshold of 1.0 never matched it on smooth images; thresholds are compared with a 1e-5 allowance. (3) With `draw_image=True` the multi path returned the frame first, and `locate_all_image` read the frame as `found` — a `ValueError` on every call; results are always `[found, boxes]` with the drawn frame appended. Multi-matches come best score first. (4) A missing template path failed as `'NoneType' object has no attribute 'shape'`, a threshold of -1 "matched" 154 places and 85 (a percentage) could only miss; both raise `ImageNotFoundException` saying what is wrong.
- **Mouse**: (5) `mouse_scroll(x, y)` clamped to the primary monitor while everything else uses virtual-desktop coordinates, so a point on a monitor left of the primary scrolled at the primary's edge; the clamp uses the virtual desktop where the platform reports it. (6) A coordinate past int32 was truncated by ctypes — `set_mouse_position(2**40, 5)` moved somewhere else and returned the requested point — and a non-numeric or infinite one raised `ValueError` / `OverflowError`; both are `AutoControlMouseException` before anything moves.
- **Keyboard**: (7) on Windows an integer keycode past 16 bits was truncated into the `wVk` field — 65601 pressed "A" — and is refused.
- **Checked and fine**: hotkeys and typed sequences release every pressed key on failure; the region origin offset and the logical grab are right; zero or negative screenshot regions are refused; float coordinates round.
- **Tests**: `test_wrapper_audit.py` (new, 15; all fail on the previous commit). `test_platform_backend_binding.py`'s scroll environment reports no virtual desktop, so its clamp still falls back to the patched 1920x1080 screen.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/cv2_utils/template_detection.py`, `je_auto_control/wrapper/{auto_control_mouse,auto_control_keyboard}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_wrapper_audit,test_platform_backend_binding}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-35 · 2026-09-23 · Android/iOS audit: adb shell injection, escaping SDK errors · #incident #security #mobile

- **What**: An audit of `android/` and `ios/` with `subprocess`, uiautomator2, adbutils and facebook-wda stubbed (their exception classes taken from the real wheels); no device was touched.
- **Shell injection**: `adb shell <command>` hands the command line to the device shell, which parses it again. (1) `AdbClient.text` / `AC_android_text` wrapped the text in double quotes after escaping only spaces, so `$(...)`, backticks and `$VAR` ran and a `"` closed the quote — typed text could run commands on the device. It is quoted with `shlex.quote`. (2) `key_event` / `AC_android_key` pasted the key in unquoted (`HOME; echo x` ran the second command); only letters, digits and underscores are accepted. `input text` itself turns every `%s` into a space with no escape, which the docstring now says.
- **SDK errors**: `adbutils.AdbError` (no device, or several without a serial), uiautomator2's errors and facebook-wda's `WDAError` (an invalid session, a crashed app) derive from `Exception` alone, so every `AC_android_find/click/dump` and `AC_ios_*` call aborted the rest of a script under `raise_on_error=False`. A `translate_device_errors` decorator in each client module re-raises them as `UIAutomatorUnavailableError` / `IOSUnavailableError` on every public device call, and the connect paths catch them too (the SDK error types are looked up lazily and star-unpacked into the `except`).
- **Checked and fine**: adb runs as an argv list with a timeout and passes `-s <serial>` on every call; adapters cache one client per serial; coordinates go through `int()`; nothing parses XML; `AC_android_shell` runs raw shell by design.
- **Tests**: `test_mobile_audit.py` (new, 14; 7 fail on the previous commit, the rest guard real key names and texts double quotes already handled). `test_android_adb.py`'s expected command for `"hello world"` is `input text hello%sworld` — `shlex.quote` leaves a string without metacharacters as it is.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/android/{adb_client,client,find}.py`, `je_auto_control/ios/{client,input,find,screen}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_mobile_audit,test_android_adb}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-36 · 2026-09-23 · Accessibility / OCR / window audit: blank matches, wrong windows, zero timeouts · #incident #accessibility #window

- **What**: An audit of `utils/accessibility`, `utils/ocr` and window management with every platform object faked (no real windows enumerated, focused or closed).
- **Matching the wrong thing**: (1) `element_matches(name="  ", contains=True)` matched every element — an empty needle is "in" every name — so `click_accessibility_element` clicked whatever came first; a blank needle matches nothing. (2) `find_window("")` matched every window and `close_window_by_title("")` closed the first listed (`None` raised `AttributeError`); every `*_by_title` function now refuses a blank or non-string title with `AutoControlActionException`. (3) On Linux, `_find` dropped `window_title`, so `get_value("Name", window_title="Firefox")` read gedit's field; the single-control lookup applies the same window scoping as the listing walk.
- **Layouts**: `save_window_layout` threw away the handle the lister gave it and looked each window up again by title substring, and restore did the same, so a title contained in another window's title, or two windows with one title, read and moved the first match. Saving reads geometry by handle; restoring gives each saved entry a different window with exactly that title. Snap, grid and cascade keep substring matching — their titles are typed by a user.
- **Windows specifics**: a window-scoped UIA search let a raw `COMError` from the first matching window escape and never tried the others; it skips a window that errors or returns a null element, as the unscoped walk does. `show_window` called `SetForegroundWindow` after every command but hide, so minimise and the no-activate commands pulled the window forward; only the activating commands do.
- **Waits and OCR text**: `wait_for_window` and `wait_for_text` with `timeout=0` never looked once; they look first and then check the clock. OCR text comparison only lowercased, so NFD vs NFC accents, full-width forms and `ß` never matched; it applies NFKC and `casefold()`.
- **Family**: `AccessibilityNotAvailableError` and `OCRBackendNotAvailableError` are `AutoControlException`s as well as `RuntimeError`s.
- **Checked and fine**: OCR boxes are offset by the region origin and rescaled to logical pixels; confidence thresholds point the right way; UIA and AT-SPI walks are bounded; the UIA focus handler is removed in `finally`; Win32 argtypes are explicit.
- **Tests**: `test_a11y_window_audit.py` (new, 19 on Windows; 15 fail on the previous commit, the rest guard the activating `show_window` commands) and one test added to `test_accessibility_linux.py` (fails on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/accessibility/{element,backends/linux_backend,backends/windows_query}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/ocr/{ocr_engine,text_span,backends/base}.py`, `je_auto_control/wrapper/auto_control_window.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/window_capture/window_capture.py`, `je_auto_control/windows/window/windows_window_manage.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_a11y_window_audit,test_accessibility_linux}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-37 · 2026-09-23 · Secret detection and failure-bundle audit: key words, free text, vault commands, image modes · #incident #security #redaction

- **What**: An audit of `utils/secrets_scan`, `utils/config_redaction`, `utils/failure_bundle` and the screenshot `utils/redaction` engine: what reaches a report, a bundle or a log in plain text.
- **Secret keys**: the key test was a substring regex, so `tokenizer`, `bypass_proxy` and `compass_heading` were secrets while `apiKey`, `sessionId`, `cookie`, `passphrase` and `Authorization` were not. `is_secret_key` splits snake/kebab/camel case into words and matches words and adjacent pairs against a fixed set.
- **What was never looked at**: list items lost their key (`"tokens": ["..."]` was checked as keyless), tuples were not walked at all, and a number under a secret key (`"password": 1234`) was skipped because only strings were checked. List and tuple items inherit the key; non-string scalars under a secret key are findings. New value shapes: JWTs, `scheme://user:password@` URLs, PGP `PRIVATE KEY BLOCK` headers, and a lower-case `bearer`.
- **Free text**: `redact_secret_text` required a word boundary before the key, so `db_password=hunter2` and `"client_secret": "..."` survived, and only `Authorization: Bearer` was handled. It now masks prefixed keys (quoted or not), `Authorization` with Basic, Token and Digest as well, and the password in a credentials URL. The URL value pattern skips a password that is already `***`, so a redacted URL keeps its host instead of collapsing to `***`.
- **Failure bundle**: (1) `AC_secret_set` / `AC_secret_unlock` arguments in `events` or `context` were written in plain text, because `redact_config` goes by key names and value shapes and an action list has neither; `redact_actions` runs first. (2) `failure_bundle_on_error` raised the bundle's own error (a bad path, a full disk) instead of the block's; the bundle error is logged and the original propagates. (3) A truncated log tail started mid-line, which can cut a secret so that no pattern matches the rest; the partial first line is dropped. `Mapping` events are copied into dicts so the walk enters them.
- **Screenshot redaction**: a `{"x", "y", "width", "height"}` box (the shape OCR and accessibility dumps use) collapsed to `(0, 0, 0, 0)`, so the password field it described was never blurred; that shape is read, and a dict with no known coordinate keys raises instead of blurring nothing. Blurring a palette or bilevel image raised `ValueError` and an RGB overlay on an `L` / `LA` image raised `TypeError`; the image is converted to RGB (RGBA when it has alpha or transparency) first.
- **Tests**: `test_redaction_audit.py` (new, 37; 35 fail on the previous commit, the other two guard the corner-key and list bbox shapes that already worked).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/secrets_scan/secrets_scan.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/config_redaction/config_redaction.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/failure_bundle/bundle.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/redaction/{rules,engine}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_redaction_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-38 · 2026-09-23 · MCP tool-safety audit: annotations, read-only writes, resource reads, undeclared arguments · #incident #security #mcp

- **What**: An audit of the MCP tool layer's safety annotations and argument handling, reproduced against the default registry with a probe command, a local HTTP server and a stubbed elicitation client.
- **Read-only mode ran action lists**: `ac_bulkhead_run` and `ac_run_chaos` call `executor.execute_action` but were annotated read-only, so with `JE_AUTOCONTROL_MCP_READONLY=1` (which removes `ac_execute_actions`) any `AC_*` command still ran through them. `ac_export_sarif`, `ac_compliance_report` and `ac_assert_visual` wrote files at caller-chosen paths while read-only. `ac_assert_http` was read-only and sent any method, including DELETE; its schema now allows only GET and HEAD.
- **The confirmation gate never asked**: tools that run action lists or code (`ac_run_saga`, `ac_skill_run`, `ac_run_suite`, `ac_replay_trace`, `ac_load_plugins`, `ac_open_path`, `ac_scheduler_add_job`, `ac_trigger_add`, `ac_hotkey_bind`...), tools that send input (`ac_input_sequence`, `ac_element_click`, `ac_set_field_text`, `ac_type_unicode`...), and tools that delete, send data off the machine or loosen a control (`ac_s3_delete`, `ac_http_request`, `ac_send_email`, `ac_egress_allow`, `ac_lease_secret`, `ac_remote_host_start`, `ac_usb_acl_set_default`...) were `destructiveHint: false`, so `JE_AUTOCONTROL_MCP_CONFIRM_DESTRUCTIVE=1` let them through; a declined prompt on `ac_execute_actions` could be sidestepped with `ac_run_saga`. 52 tools are now destructive, 3 file writers are no longer read-only; the rule is written in the MCP server doc.
- **Read-only tools created databases**: `ac_queue_stats`, `ac_memory_recall` / `_recent` / `_stats` and `ac_checkpoint_status` created an empty SQLite file at any `db` path. They return an empty result for a missing file, as the other read-only `db` tools already did.
- **Resources**: `resources/read` on `autocontrol://files/<name>` served any file in the root (`creds.txt`, labelled JSON) and NTFS stream names (`a.json::$DATA`), though `resources/list` shows only `*.json`. Only a plain `*.json` name is read; `\` and `:` are refused with `/`.
- **Undeclared arguments**: an argument the schema does not declare (including `ctx`) reached the handler as a keyword and came back as a `TypeError` tool error (and an error screenshot when enabled). `undeclared_arguments` refuses it with `-32602` before the tool runs.
- **Checked and fine**: all 676 tool schemas agree with their handler signatures; the read-only filter runs before aliases are added; `resources/read` path traversal is refused; SQL tools are read-only; malformed requests are contained.
- **Tests**: `test_mcp_tool_safety_audit.py` (new, 44; 42 fail on the previous commit, the other two guard declared arguments and the read-only registry's contents).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/mcp_server/{server,resources}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/mcp_server/tools/{_factories,_handlers,_handlers_operations,_validation}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/mcp_server/mcp_server_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_mcp_tool_safety_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-39 · 2026-09-23 · JSON Patch, JSONPath and unified-diff audit: bounds, shared values, silent wildcards, -U0 hunks · #incident #data

- **What**: An audit of the pure-logic data modules behind `ac_apply_json_patch`, `ac_json_query` and `ac_apply_unified` / `ac_three_way_merge`, each finding reproduced against RFC 6902, RFC 9535 or the module's own round trip.
- **JSON Patch**: `add` at an index equal to the array length (RFC 6902 4.1 allows it) was refused; `add` / `replace` put the op's `value` object into the document, so a later op edited the caller's patch too; `move` with `from == path` returned without checking that `from` exists; `str.isdigit()` accepted `²` (an uncaught `ValueError`) and Arabic-Indic digits (which removed a real index). All fixed.
- **JSONPath**: a filter it could not parse (`@.a.b == 1`, `@.n`) became a wildcard and returned every element -- an assertion built on it passed on anything. Nested fields and existence tests are supported, anything else raises `ValueError`. `==` matched `true` against `1`; values of different types no longer compare equal. `$.foo-bar` was read as `$.foo.bar`; `-` is part of a bare key and any other stray character raises. An unterminated `[` was dropped and `['a]b']` cut at the inner bracket; both are handled.
- **Unified diff**: a pure-insertion hunk (`-N,0`, what `unified_diff(context=0)` and `git diff -U0` produce) was applied one line early -- half of 3,000 random zero-context round trips were wrong. Body lines starting with `---` / `+++` were dropped as file headers, and a `\ No newline at end of file` marker was read as a context line. Hunk bodies are now read by the header's line counts, and markers are skipped; zero-context round trips are exact.
- **Three-way merge**: two different insertions at the same point merged "clean" with both kept (diff3 reports a conflict), and an identical change on both sides was applied twice or reported as a conflict. Same-point insertions conflict; identical changes are applied once.
- **Checked and fine**: pointer unescaping order, `-` append, leading-zero indexes, move into own child, deep copies on `copy`, `test` equality (1 == 1.0, 1 != true); negative JSONPath indexes, `..`, missing keys.
- **Tests**: `test_patch_query_audit.py` (new, 20; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{json_patch/json_patch,jsonpath/jsonpath,text_diff/text_diff}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v51_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_patch_query_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-40 · 2026-09-23 · dotenv, VEX, data-source and HTTP-header parsing audit · #incident #data #security

- **What**: The second half of the data-module audit (`ac_load_dotenv`, `ac_apply_vex`, `ac_load_data`, `ac_parse_set_cookie`, `ac_store_validators`, `ac_parse_sse`, `ac_next_url`), each finding reproduced.
- **VEX (security)**: products were matched by substring, so a `not_affected` statement for `pkg:pypi/requests-toolbelt` suppressed a finding in `requests`; the purl's package name is compared instead. A statement's `vulnerability.aliases` were ignored, and the first matching statement won although later ones supersede earlier ones in OpenVEX; the last match now decides.
- **dotenv**: `A="quoted" # comment` kept its quotes, `A= # comment` became the comment text, and a quoted value spanning lines was cut at the first line. A quoted value now ends at its closing quote (a `\"` does not close it) and may span lines; an unclosed quote is read as its own line, as before. `dump_dotenv` left `'x'` unquoted (read back as `x`) and did not escape `\r` (the parser splits lines on it); both round-trip.
- **Data sources**: CSV and JSON files default to `utf-8-sig`, so the BOM Excel writes no longer turns the first header into `\ufeffuser` (and `${row.user}` unresolved) or fails JSON parsing.
- **HTTP headers**: a `Set-Cookie` with an empty name was stored (RFC 6265 says ignore it) and a past `Expires` -- how servers delete a cookie -- left the cookie in the jar; `Max-Age` still wins. `Cache-Control` and `Link` parameters were split inside quoted strings (`private="Set-Cookie, X-Foo"`, `title="x;y"`); both use one quote-aware splitter. `rel="NEXT"` was not found; relation types are compared case-insensitively.
- **SSE**: a `\r\n` split across two `feed` chunks counted as two line breaks and dispatched the event early, and a leading BOM hid the first field; both handled as WHATWG specifies.
- **Checked and fine**: dotenv `export`, `=` in values, `abc#x`; cookie attribute case and duplicate attributes; `Max-Age=0`; SQLite sources are read-only; cron day-of-week 7 and the DOM/DOW OR rule.
- **Tests**: `test_header_env_audit.py` (new, 24; 19 fail on the previous commit, the other five guard dump cases and `Max-Age` precedence that already worked).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{dotenv/dotenv,vex/vex,data_source/data_source,cookie_jar/cookie_jar,http_conditional/http_conditional,sse_client/sse_client,link_header/link_header}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v79_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_header_env_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-41 · 2026-09-23 · Executor flow-control audit: strictness through nested bodies, try/retry catch sets, double expansion · #incident #executor #security

- **What**: An audit of the executor's block commands and `${...}` substitution, reproduced with a probe command registered on a private `Executor` (no real input).
- **Strictness stopped at the first block**: `_run_branch`, `_run_loop_body` and `AC_assert_duration` ran nested bodies with the default `raise_on_error=False`, so a failure inside an `AC_loop`, an `AC_if_*` branch or a macro was recorded and swallowed. `AC_try` never ran `catch` for it, `AC_retry` reported success after one attempt, and `execute_action(..., raise_on_error=True)` did not raise. `execute_action` now keeps the running list's strictness in a thread-local that nested bodies (`_validated=True`) inherit; a lenient run is unchanged, and `AC_parallel` branches (own threads and executors) are unaffected.
- **Catch sets**: `AC_try`'s tuple, commented as aligned with the executor's, lacked `ArithmeticError` (an `OverflowError` from `AC_loop` with `times=inf` skipped `catch`); `AC_retry` also lacked `LookupError`. Both share one tuple now. Both caught `MacroDepthExceeded`, which is documented to unwind to the top-level record; they re-raise it.
- **Double expansion (security)**: `AC_execute_action`'s `action_list` was expanded at dispatch and again when the nested list ran, so a variable whose *value* was `${secrets.NAME}` (text read from OCR, HTTP or a file) was resolved from the vault. Every command that runs an action list under a non-block key (`action_list`, `actions`, `action`, `steps`, `spec`, `trace` -- declared per command, since the same keys are plain data elsewhere) defers it, so each placeholder is expanded once, when the nested action runs. This also keeps `AC_skill_save` from writing resolved secrets into a skill file and `AC_admin_broadcast_execute` from sending them to remote hosts.
- **Planner**: `run_from_description` ran the model's plan with `_validated=True`, which the executor reads as "nested body", so a stray `AC_break` escaped as a raw `LoopBreak`; it runs as a top-level list.
- **Not changed**: macro parameters are bound into the caller's scope, as the macro docstring says, so a nested call can overwrite an outer parameter; changing that is a behaviour change, not a fix.
- **Checked and fine**: `AC_break` / `AC_continue` scoping, `finally` on break / continue / re-raise, assertion propagation through lenient runs, loop-count coercion, `AC_for_each` input checks, the macro depth limit, type-preserving lone placeholders, undefined-variable errors.
- **Tests**: `test_flow_strictness_audit.py` (new, 12; 10 fail on the previous commit, the other two guard lenient runs and single expansion).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/executor/{action_executor,flow_control}.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/llm/planner.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/new_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_flow_strictness_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-42 · 2026-09-23 · QA subsystem audit: setup failures in reports, soft asserts, dead servers, malformed cases · #incident #qa

- **What**: An audit of the suite runner, JUnit / Allure writers, quarantine, run history, soft assertions and assertion combinators, reproduced with probe commands on a private executor.
- **Reports**: a setup failure is written to JUnit as a `<setup>` testcase with an error, but the `<testsuite>` counts left it out (`tests="0" errors="0"`), so readers that trust the attributes saw an empty, green suite; Allure got no result for it at all. Both now report it; JUnit also carries the `timestamp` the Ant schema requires. A lone surrogate in a case name or message raised `UnicodeEncodeError` part-way through writing Allure files; strings are made UTF-8-safe first.
- **Soft assertions**: `assert_all` raised `AutoControlActionException`, so a suite scored a failed batch as *error* and a lenient run swallowed it, unlike every `AC_assert_*`. It raises `SoftAssertionsFailed`, which derives from both `AutoControlAssertionException` and `AutoControlActionException`, so existing `except` clauses still match.
- **`assert_http`**: only `URLError` (connect-phase) counted as a failed assertion; a read timeout or a server closing without a reply raised `TimeoutError` / `RemoteDisconnected` and crashed the script, or ended an `assert_eventually` poll on its first attempt. Every `OSError` / `HTTPException` is a failed assertion.
- **`assert_eventually`**: a NaN `timeout` (valid in action JSON) passed the `< 0` check and never reached its deadline -- an endless loop; a NaN `interval` raised a bare `ValueError`. Both are refused.
- **Runner**: a case with `"tags": 5` raised `TypeError` out of `run_suite` and lost every result, and a list as a case name raised on the quarantine lookup; the case scores *error* instead. `"tags": "smoke"` (and `tags="smoke"` on the call) was matched letter by letter, so `tags=["s"]` selected it; a string is one tag.
- **Quarantine / history / timeline**: one malformed quarantine entry (`"added_at": null`, a list as the name) crashed the store and every suite run that consults it; bad entries are skipped. Runs with equal `started_at` came back oldest-first and `prune(keep_latest=1)` kept the oldest; `id` breaks ties. `critical_steps(top=0)` returned one step.
- **Checked and fine**: teardown always runs, quarantined data rows, JUnit escaping of `]]>` and control characters, `failure` vs `error` mapping, `assert_eventually` with `timeout=0`, `assert_all` / `assert_any` semantics, flip-rate maths, timeline offsets.
- **Tests**: `test_qa_suite_audit.py` (new, 15; 13 fail on the previous commit and the NaN-timeout test hangs there, the other one guards NaN intervals' error type).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{test_suite/reports,test_suite/runner,soft_assert/soft_assert,soft_assert/__init__,assertion/assertions,assertion/combinators,quarantine/store,run_history/history_store,step_timeline/step_timeline}.py`, `je_auto_control/__init__.py` (`SoftAssertionsFailed`), `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v148_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_qa_suite_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures, public-name count).

## U-20260923-43 · 2026-09-23 · Image-utility audit: BGR/RGB grayscale, red colour matching, non-ASCII paths, approval extension · #incident #vision #security

- **What**: An audit of the image utilities behind `ac_preprocess_image`, `ac_match_color`, `ac_find_color_region`, `ac_assert_visual`, `ac_image_hash`, `ac_mark_screen` and `ac_screen_changed`, plus approval testing, reproduced on in-memory images with the screen grabber patched.
- **Preprocessing**: every grayscale step used the BGR conversion on PIL images and screen grabs, which are RGB, so pure red read as 29 and pure blue as 76 -- `grayscale`, `binarize`, `denoise`, `contrast` and `deskew` all saw swapped weights. PIL input and screen grabs are converted to BGR, and palette / `LA` / `1` images are converted instead of being read as palette indices (all-white palette image: gray 0). Deskew measured the whole frame on light text over a dark background (+5 degrees read as 0); the mask is inverted when it is mostly foreground. `upscale` with a scale of 0 or below returned a 1x1 image; it raises. `block_size` / `c` reached no step -- `binarize` is Otsu -- so `adaptive_mean` / `adaptive_gaussian` steps take them.
- **Non-ASCII paths**: `cv2.imread` failed ("could not read image") and `cv2.imwrite` wrote a mangled file name while reporting success for any path with non-ASCII characters. The new `cv2_utils/image_file.py` (`read_image` / `write_image`) encodes and decodes the bytes itself; preprocessing, colour regions, SSIM and `AC_preprocess_image` use it.
- **Colour matching**: `TM_SQDIFF_NORMED` divides by the template's energy; a red glyph on white has hue 0 everywhere, so the score was 0 / 0 and even an exact copy lost -- red, the most common status colour, could never match. Distances are now plain squared distance scaled to each channel's range, hue compared the short way round the wheel (hue 1 and 179 are both red), and the score is `1 -` the RMS distance, so a hue 30-60 degrees off no longer passes. `find_color_regions` crashed on RGBA and `L` PIL images; they are converted to RGB.
- **Visual regression**: a golden of another size raised `ValueError` even with `raise_on_fail=False`, outside the framework's exception family; it is a mismatch at 100%.
- **Approvals (security)**: only `name` was checked, so `extension="x/../../outside/pwned"` wrote outside `approvals_dir`; the extension must be dot-separated letters and digits.
- **Smaller**: zero-area boxes (the `(0, 0, 0, 0)` a backend reports for an element without a position) were given a mark, so `mark_click` clicked (0, 0); they are skipped. `AC_image_hash` fell back to the average hash for any other name (`phash`, `DHASH`); unknown names raise. `hamming_distance` compared hashes of different sizes; it raises. `diff_snapshots` keyed elements by `(role, name)` alone, so a second unnamed row was reported as `moved` and one of two `OK` buttons vanishing as `moved`; same-key items are paired by unchanged box, then nearest centre. `screen_changed`'s docstring now says it replaces the baseline on each call.
- **Checked and fine**: aHash / dHash definitions and bit order, per-pixel threshold and tolerance semantics, RGBA and 16-bit goldens, deskew direction on light themes, set-of-marks numbering and centres, the pixelmatch YIQ constants.
- **Tests**: `test_image_utils_audit.py` (new, 21; 19 fail on the previous commit, the other two guard light-theme deskew and a NaN scale).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/cv2_utils/image_file.py` (new), `je_auto_control/utils/{preprocess/preprocess,color_match/color_match,color_region/color_region,visual_regression/compare,set_of_marks/set_of_marks,image_dedup/perceptual_hash,approval/approval_test,screen_state/screen_state,ssim/ssim,executor/action_executor}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/{v135,v179}_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_image_utils_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-44 · 2026-09-23 · Text and clipboard-format audit: fuzzy autojunk, RTF Unicode, drop lists, pseudo-localization, slugify · #incident #text #clipboard

- **What**: An audit of the pure text and clipboard-format helpers (CF_HTML, RTF, CSV, CF_HDROP, fuzzy matching, pseudo-localization, slugify, bidi stripping), without touching the real clipboard.
- **Fuzzy matching**: with the difflib fallback (rapidfuzz absent), strings of 200+ characters that differed by one letter scored ~0.14 -- difflib's autojunk heuristic drops common characters -- so `fuzzy_best_match` / `fuzzy_dedupe` missed near-duplicates. `autojunk=False` gives 0.996.
- **RTF**: `build_rtf` wrote `\uN` unsigned (`\u44032?` for U+AC00, where RTF takes a signed 16-bit N) and as one code point for characters beyond the BMP, so an emoji came back as U+F600; it writes each UTF-16 unit, signed. A lone CR was dropped; all line breaks become `\par`. `rtf_to_text` skipped the fallback as one character instead of one token (Word's `\u8220\'93` left `'93`), ignored `\ucN` (so `\uc0` ate the next control word's backslash or the next letter) and left surrogate halves; it is now a small reader that tracks `\ucN` per group, ends a fallback at a control word and joins surrogate pairs.
- **CF_HDROP**: an empty path in `build_dropfiles` wrote the list terminator early, so Windows saw only the files before it; empty paths and paths with NUL are refused. `parse_dropfiles` read past the terminator (a clipboard block may be larger than what was written) and returned an empty list for an offset past the data; it stops at the terminator and raises for a bad offset.
- **Pseudo-localization**: only `{..}` and `%s` / `%d` were protected, so `%(user)s`, `%1$s`, `%i`, HTML attributes and ICU `plural` keywords were accented (`%(ùśèr)ś`, `òthèr`) while the ICU case text stayed untouched; `check_catalog` shared the pattern and missed a renamed `%(user)s`. A segmenter keeps printf conversions, tags and ICU structure (and `#` in plurals) and localizes the case text; padding is counted on visible text (a lone long placeholder got padding for marker characters). `check_catalog` compares printf conversions and argument names, so languages with different plural categories are not flagged. The documented example output is corrected.
- **Smaller**: `slugify` passed `sep` to `re.sub` as a template (`sep="\"` raised, `\g<0>` was expanded) and stripped by it as a character set, eating real letters; `rows_to_csv` wrote `None` for a null cell.
- **Checked and fine**: CF_HTML byte offsets with CJK text, CSV quoting per RFC 4180, the 12 Bidi_Control characters removed by `bidi_strip`, Jaro-Winkler reference values, the clipboard history ring buffer.
- **Tests**: `test_text_clipboard_audit.py` (new, 31; 25 fail on the previous commit, the other six guard round trips that already worked). RTF sources in it are built from `chr(92)` so the escapes stay escapes in the file.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{fuzzy/fuzzy_match,clipboard_rich_formats/clipboard_rich_formats,clipboard_files/clipboard_files,i18n_test/i18n_test,text_normalize/text_normalize}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/{v20,v185}_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_text_clipboard_audit,test_patch_query_audit}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-45 · 2026-09-23 · Resilience-primitive audit: backoff caps, rate limits, Retry-After, loop guard, leases, approvals, CAS, breaker · #incident #resilience #security

- **What**: An audit of the retry, rate-limit, circuit-breaker, bulkhead, idempotency, CAS, loop-guard, lease and approval primitives, reproduced with fake clocks.
- **Backoff**: `backoff_delay` raised `OverflowError` for a large attempt number (from 1026 with base 0.1) instead of returning the cap; `RetryPolicy` slept the full `backoff` before applying `max_backoff` (sleeps `[10, 1]` for a cap of 1).
- **Rate limits**: `TokenBucket` accepted a negative `n`, minting tokens past capacity, and `acquire(n)` with `n` above capacity and no timeout waited forever; both refuse such requests. `SlidingWindowLimiter.time_until_available` answered "the rest of this window" although the current count carries over at full weight after the roll (advised 5 s, needed 10 s); the wait is solved from the weighted estimate.
- **`Retry-After`**: an asctime or `-0000` date came back naive and was read as local time (0 s instead of 3600 on a UTC+8 machine); HTTP-dates are GMT. `"²"` passed `isdigit()` and crashed `float()`.
- **Loop guard**: the first pattern past its minimum won, so one repeated step after a 15-step no-op run reported `repeat` / `ok` -- a stuck agent read as fine. The longest qualifying run decides.
- **Leases and approvals (security)**: a `ttl` of NaN never compared as expired (a permanent lease, while `active()` dropped it -- two views disagreeing) and `inf` was accepted; `ttl` must be finite and positive. The approval commands built a fresh in-memory gate per call, so without `db` a token was unknown to every later command; they share `approval_gate()`. An empty approver skipped the maker-checker test (anonymous approved anonymous); a named approver other than the requester is required, spaces ignored.
- **CAS / breaker / idempotency**: re-creating a deleted key restarted at version 1, so a stale writer holding the old v1 overwrote the new record (ABA); versions only increase. A `KeyboardInterrupt` during the half-open trial left the trial flag set and every later call refused for good; the slot is freed. `IdempotencyStore.begin` was not atomic (two duplicates could both see `new`) and the TTL ran from `begin`, so long work lost its stored response; a lock covers claim and completion, and the TTL starts at completion.
- **Not changed**: the outbox redelivers a failed event after later ones succeed; its docstring promises at-least-once delivery, and strict order would block every event behind one bad one.
- **Checked and fine**: breaker state transitions and single half-open trial, jitter bounds, `run_with_budget` limits, bulkhead permit release on exceptions, IMF-fixdate / RFC 850 parsing, dedup windows, sequence tracking, file-backed approval rules.
- **Tests**: `test_resilience_audit.py` (new, 22; 21 fail on the previous commit, the other guards IMF-fixdate parsing).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{retry_budget/retry_budget,resilience/resilience,rate_limit/rate_limit,bulkhead/bulkhead,loop_guard/loop_guard,governance/credential_broker,governance/governance,governance/__init__,optimistic/optimistic,idempotency/idempotency,executor/action_executor,mcp_server/tools/_handlers_operations}.py`, `je_auto_control/__init__.py` (`approval_gate`), `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v32_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_resilience_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures, public-name count).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-46 · 2026-09-23 · Plugin-loading audit: broken files, dataclass plugins, built-in overrides, watcher ownership · #incident #plugins #security

- **What**: The plugin half of an audit of the plugin and file-loading subsystems (path loader, entry-point SDK, MCP plugin watcher), reproduced with plugin files and fake entry points.
- **Directory loading**: `load_plugin_directory` caught only `OSError` / `ImportError` / `SyntaxError`, so one file raising anything else on import (a `RuntimeError`) aborted the load and every other plugin in the directory -- including from the GUI Plugins tab. Any exception now skips that file with a log line, as the watcher and the SDK already did.
- **`@dataclass` plugins**: the isolated import ran the module without registering it in `sys.modules`, and `dataclasses` (with postponed annotations) looks the module up there, so such a plugin raised `AttributeError` and could never load. The module is registered under its unique name while it executes.
- **Entry-point plugins**: a value that was not a function (a `functools.partial`) made `add_command_to_executor` raise part-way through, leaving the plugins before it registered and the rest not, although the docs promise broken plugins are skipped. Registration now goes through `register_plugin_commands`, which skips and logs such entries.
- **Built-in overrides (security)**: a plugin defining `AC_click_mouse` (or any existing command) silently replaced it, both through the path loader and the SDK. A name that belongs to a built-in or user command is skipped unless the caller passes `allow_override=True`; a plugin may still re-register its own commands on reload.
- **MCP watcher**: tools were tracked per file only, so deleting one of two files that both defined `AC_shared` removed `plugin_ac_shared` although the other file still defined it. The watcher records each file's definitions and hands a tool to the remaining definition instead of unregistering it.
- **Tests**: `test_plugin_loading_audit.py` (new, 6; all fail on the previous commit). They run against a copy of the executor's command table.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{plugin_loader/plugin_loader,plugin_sdk/plugin_sdk,mcp_server/plugin_watcher}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/{new_features,v31_features}_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_plugin_loading_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none; the file-store findings of the same audit are the next entry.

## U-20260923-47 · 2026-09-23 · File-store audit: lost saves, assets without db, bundle entries, permissions, non-ASCII recall · #incident #storage #security

- **What**: The file-store half of the plugin / loader audit (U-20260923-46): skill library, element repository, asset store, config bundles, agent memory and action-file reading.
- **Lost saves**: `SkillLibrary` and `ElementRepository` loaded the file once and wrote their whole copy back (non-atomically) on every change, so two instances on one file -- the executor, socket and MCP servers each build their own -- dropped each other's saves. Both now use `SharedJsonDict`, which re-reads under a lock and writes atomically. `SharedJsonDict` gains a `strict` mode in which an unreadable file raises instead of reading as empty, so a corrupt skill or locator file still raises instead of being erased on the next save.
- **Assets**: without `db` each command built a fresh in-memory store, so `AC_set_asset` followed by `AC_get_asset` found nothing; the commands share `asset_store()`. `set` stored an unknown type or a value its type could not read (`"eighty"` as `int`), after which every read raised; both are refused at write time.
- **Config bundles (security)**: an entry without `content` was imported as `null`, emptying the file (`admin_hosts.json` then loaded no hosts); a missing or non-object content is skipped. Imported files and the CLI's exported bundle were written with `write_text`, so they took the umask's default permissions although `admin_hosts.json` holds REST tokens and is documented as 0600; they are written with `atomic_write_text` (0600, atomic).
- **Smaller**: agent memory tokenised with `[a-z0-9]+`, so `recall` never matched Chinese or accented words; it uses `\w+`. A tag passed as a string was stored letter by letter by `AC_skill_save` and `remember`; a string is one tag. `read_action_json` rejected a UTF-8 BOM (as Notepad writes) and let a non-UTF-8 file escape as a raw `UnicodeDecodeError`; it reads `utf-8-sig` and wraps decode errors in its own exception.
- **Not changed**: `ac_write_action_file` stays `SIDE_EFFECT_ONLY` like the other file writers (U-20260923-38); the MCP tools' file paths remain unconfined, recorded as a Progress.md decision.
- **Checked and fine**: bundle allowlist and traversal checks, version checks, backup naming, config-sync `from_dict` validation, parameterised agent-memory queries, atomic action-file writes.
- **Tests**: `test_file_store_audit.py` (new, 14; 11 fail on the previous commit, two guard that a corrupt store raises and is left intact, and the POSIX permission test runs on Linux CI).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{json_store/json_store,skill_library/skill_library,element_repository/element_repository,assets/assets,config_bundle/config_bundle,config_bundle/__main__,agent_memory/agent_memory,json/json_file}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/v48_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_file_store_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-48 · 2026-09-23 · Observability and report-output audit: OTLP spans, Prometheus, SARIF, purls, version ordering, step videos · #incident #observability

- **What**: An audit of trace export, Prometheus metrics, SBOM / SARIF / vulnerability-scan output, W3C trace context, step videos, statistics and cost telemetry against their specs (OTLP, Prometheus text format, purl, SARIF 2.1.0, PEP 440, SemVer, W3C Trace Context).
- **Trace export**: `AgentTrace.to_otel` was documented as dropping straight into an OTLP exporter but had no ids, no wall-clock times (a monotonic clock), string enums and a flat attribute dict. Each span now carries the run's `traceId`, its own `spanId`, nanosecond start/end times (strings), integer `kind` / `status.code` and typed attributes (encoded by `otlp_export.attributes_to_otlp`); the provider is also written as the current `gen_ai.provider.name`.
- **Prometheus**: labelled counters and gauges rendered a bogus unlabelled zero sample next to their series (the executor's `autocontrol_action_calls_total` among them); a partial label set forked a new series; names were checked with `str.isalnum` (accepting `é`, refusing the valid `:`); `le` was accepted as a histogram label; HELP text was not escaped, so a newline broke the exposition. All fixed.
- **SARIF / SBOM**: levels were passed through (`high`, `critical`) although SARIF allows only none/note/warning/error; lint issues were one line early (0-based index as `startLine`, and index 0 got no region); a negative line was emitted; OS paths were written as `artifactLocation.uri`; a numeric rule id disagreed with its rule. SBOM purls kept `PySide6_Essentials` and a raw `+` instead of `pyside6-essentials` and `%2B`.
- **Vulnerability versions**: `2.0.0rc1` and `2.0.0.dev1` sorted after `2.0.0` (so were reported not affected), `2.0` sorted before `2.0.0`, and SemVer `alpha.2` after `alpha.10`. `version_key` now ignores trailing zeros and local/build metadata, ranks PEP 440 dev < pre < final < post, and compares SemVer identifiers numerically.
- **Trace context**: W3C multi-tenant keys (`tenant@system`) were dropped from `tracestate`, and duplicated keys kept; the key grammar follows the spec and a duplicate invalidates the header.
- **Step videos**: frames of another size were silently dropped by OpenCV while counted, and an unwritable path reported frames without a file; frames are resized to the video size and an unopened writer raises. A caller's array had every caption drawn onto it; frames are copied.
- **Smaller**: `welch_t_test` with no variance and different means returned p = 1 and a [0, 0] interval around a non-zero difference; negative token counts were stored and subtracted from the totals; both fixed.
- **Checked and fine**: label-value escaping, histogram cumulativeness and `+Inf`, exposition content type, traceparent validation, profiler maths, percentile and chi-square maths, cost rounding, compliance-report escaping, CycloneDX required fields, OSV range events.
- **Tests**: `test_observability_audit.py` (new, 25; 24 fail on the previous commit). `test_agent_trace_batch.py` and `test_sarif_batch.py` updated to the OTLP shape and 1-based lint lines.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{agent_trace/agent_trace,observability/metrics,sarif/sarif,sbom/sbom,vuln_scan/vuln_scan,trace_context/trace_context,video_report/video_report,stats/stats,cost_telemetry/store}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/{v38,v94}_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_observability_audit,test_agent_trace_batch,test_sarif_batch}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-49 · 2026-09-23 · Orchestration-runner audit: sagas that never rolled back, device matrix, DAG, work queue, observer · #incident #orchestration

- **What**: An audit of the DAG runner, sagas, chaos experiments, work queue, device matrix and observer / watchdog, reproduced with probe commands on the executor.
- **Sagas never rolled back**: `run_saga` (and `AC_run_saga`) ran each step with the lenient module-level `execute_action`, which records a failed action instead of raising, so `Saga.run` never saw a failing step: it reported `ok`, ran the later steps and compensated nothing. Steps now run with `raise_on_error=True`, as the DAG runner already did. A compensation that raised was still listed in `compensated`; it goes to the new `compensation_errors` instead.
- **Device matrix**: a device failing with anything outside a six-type list (`ImageNotFoundException`, `KeyError`, ...) raised out of `run_on_devices`, losing every other device's result; it uses the executor's containment set.
- **DAG**: a runner raising a plain `Exception` subclass escaped `run_dag` and returned no result; any node failure now fails that node and skips its dependents.
- **Work queue**: `complete` / `fail` updated by id alone, so failing an item that had already succeeded requeued it for a second run, a never-claimed item could be completed, and a performer whose item had been re-claimed as stale could overwrite the new performer's outcome. Both now settle only an `in_progress` item and raise otherwise.
- **Observer**: a rule removed while a poll was running still fired in that pass, after `remove()` had returned `True`; the rule is re-checked before firing.
- **Checked and fine**: topological order, cycle detection and skip cascades, `max_parallel`; LIFO compensation over completed steps; chaos steady-state and rollback semantics; concurrent queue claims; per-device variable isolation; observer transitions; watchdog containment.
- **Tests**: `test_orchestration_audit.py` (new, 7; all fail on the previous commit); `test_saga_batch.py` updated -- it asserted the failing compensation was listed as compensated.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{saga/saga,device_matrix/matrix,dag/runner,work_queue/work_queue,observer/observer,executor/action_executor}.py`, `docs/source/{Eng,Zh}/doc/new_features/{v10,v52}_features_doc.rst`, `test/unit_test/headless/{test_orchestration_audit,test_saga_batch}.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-50 · 2026-09-23 · Record / replay and input-helper audit: positions, held keys, speed, unknown ops, key names · #incident #input

- **What**: An audit of recording, timeline replay and the composite input helpers, run entirely against recording sinks and patched wrapper functions (every real `SendInput` / `SetCursorPos` was replaced by a raising stub).
- **Replay positions**: the Windows hook records no movement and the Windows / X11 backends press and scroll wherever the cursor is, so replaying a recorded drag pressed and released in place, and a scroll lost its position. `mouse_down` / `mouse_up` / `scroll` events move to their recorded x/y first, as `click` already did.
- **Timing**: `timeline` truncated each gap to whole milliseconds, so 1,001 events 7.9 ms apart replayed 11% fast; gaps come from cumulative rounded timestamps. `speed` 0 or negative slept 1e8 s per gap and NaN dropped all timing; `replay_timeline` refuses a speed that is not a positive number.
- **Unknown ops**: the default sink skipped an unknown op (`presss`, `tpyo`) while `replay_timeline` counted it as played and `run_sequence` logged it; it raises `ValueError`.
- **Held inputs**: a failing step in `run_sequence` or `replay_timeline` left keys and mouse buttons held (Shift stayed down); both track what is held and release it, newest first, before the error propagates. `tween_drag` and `drag_path` release the button in `finally`.
- **Names**: the Windows key table had `control` but not `ctrl`, which Linux and macOS have and which `type_unicode` (after overwriting the clipboard), `set_field_text`, `with_modifiers` and the documented `run_sequence` example use; `ctrl` is an alias there. The mouse tables knew `mouse_left` but not `left`, the default of `AC_act_with_mode` / `ac_act_in_view`, which failed without clicking; `left` / `right` / `middle` are aliases in the mouse wrapper.
- **Not changed**: the accessibility recorder documented that it follows the focused element but observes the first element found, because no backend exposes focus yet; its docstring now says so and the backend work is a Progress.md entry. Nesting `hold_modifiers` with the same key releases it when the inner block exits (unchanged).
- **Checked and fine**: `hold_modifiers` / `hold_key` release in `finally`, easing end points, step clamping, surrogate-pair units, `act_with_mode` trial mode, `mark_click`, recorder start/stop lifecycle.
- **Tests**: `test_input_replay_audit.py` (new, 15; 14 fail on the previous commit, one guards the sink table).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/{input_macro/input_macro,input_macro/recorder_base,tween_drag/tween_drag,mouse_path/mouse_path,accessibility/recorder}.py`, `je_auto_control/wrapper/{_platform_windows,auto_control_mouse}.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_input_replay_audit.py`, `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: accessibility focus tracking (Progress.md).
