## U-20260923-52 · 2026-09-23 · Server-surface audit: signaling secret checked before the body, bearer scheme case · #incident #security #remote_desktop

- **What**: An audit of the REST API, socket server, webhook receiver, signaling server and MCP HTTP transport, run against 127.0.0.1 servers with probe commands only.
- **Signaling server (security)**: the `X-Signaling-Secret` check was a route dependency, and FastAPI reads and parses the request body before resolving dependencies, so a client without the secret made the server buffer and parse a body of any size (a 30 MB body cost ~95 MB before the 401) and got JSON validation details for malformed input. An HTTP middleware now refuses `/sessions/*` requests with a wrong secret (401), a POST without `Content-Length` (411) or with a body over the SDP cap plus its JSON wrapper (413) before the body is read; the route dependency stays as a second check. It is registered inside CORS so browsers still see the status.
- **MCP HTTP transport**: `Authorization: bearer tok` was refused as "missing bearer token" although auth schemes are case-insensitive (RFC 7235) and the REST gate accepts it; the scheme is compared case-insensitively.
- **Checked and fine**: REST token handling (constant-time bytes compare, no query-string tokens, lockout, 1 MB body cap, dashboard traversal, 12 malformed `/execute` shapes), socket-server framing and malformed input, webhook auth before body read with a drain cap, MCP Origin / Host / token checks, the ACME challenge responder and the Prometheus exporter.
- **Tests**: `test_server_surface_audit.py` (new, 5; 3 fail on the previous commit, two guard a normal offer and the unauthenticated health / preflight routes).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/signaling_server.py`, `je_auto_control/utils/mcp_server/http_transport.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_server_surface_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-53 · 2026-09-23 · Signaling server serves the config-sync bucket routes · #feature #remote_desktop #config_sync

- **What**: `config_sync` documented that its `ConfigSyncClient` talks to matching `GET` / `PUT /config/{user_id}` routes on the signaling server, but the server had no such routes, so every `fetch` / `push` / `sync` got a 404 or 405 and cross-machine sync could not work against anything in this repository.
- **Added**: `GET /config/{user_id}` returns the stored bucket (404 when none) and `PUT /config/{user_id}` replaces it. Buckets are stored as sent -- the merge stays client-side -- in memory, like the server's sessions, so a restart loses them. A bucket whose own `user_id` differs from the path is refused (400); ids are single printable path segments of up to 128 characters.
- **Limits**: the routes sit behind the same pre-body guard as `/sessions` (U-20260923-52): a wrong `X-Signaling-Secret` is refused before the body is read, and a `PUT` needs a `Content-Length` of at most 1 MiB; at most 1,024 users may hold a bucket (503 beyond that).
- **Tests**: `test_signaling_config_routes.py` (new, 6), including an end-to-end `ConfigSyncClient` fetch / push against the server under uvicorn on 127.0.0.1.
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/signaling_server.py`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_signaling_config_routes.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none; client-side tombstones for deletions are a separate change.

## U-20260923-54 · 2026-09-23 · Signaling guard keyed on the routed path (Starlette BadHost, CVE-2026-48710) · #security #remote_desktop #deps

- **What**: Starlette up to 1.0.0 rebuilds `request.url` from the `Host` header, so a crafted Host (`example.com?`) shows middleware a different path from the one the router dispatches (CVE-2026-48710, "BadHost", in CISA's KEV catalogue since 2026-09-02). The signaling server's new pre-body guard (U-20260923-52) decided by `request.url.path`, and this development environment had Starlette 1.0.0: with `Host: example.com?` the guard was skipped and a 2 MB body was read and parsed (the handler answered 400 instead of the guard's 413). The secret itself still held, because the route dependency checks it as well.
- **Fix**: the guard (and the request log) use `request.scope["path"]`, the path the router dispatches on, so it holds whatever Starlette version is installed; the `signaling` extra requires `starlette>=1.0.1`, and `uv.lock` records the bound (it already resolved 1.3.1).
- **Checked**: no other code in the package decides on `request.url`; the REST API, webhook and MCP HTTP servers use `http.server`, not Starlette.
- **Tests**: `test_signaling_badhost.py` (new, 2; the body-limit test fails with the previous guard on Starlette 1.0.0, the secret test guards the route dependency).
- **Files**: `je_auto_control/utils/remote_desktop/signaling_server.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_signaling_badhost.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (figures).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-01 · 2026-09-24 · Security floors: zeroconf 0.149.16 for discovery, and a test pinning every floor · #security #deps

- **What**: `pip-audit` over the lock file was clean, but over this development environment it found Starlette 1.0.0 (CVE-2026-48710, fixed by U-20260923-54), urllib3 2.6.3 and zeroconf 0.148.0. The lock already resolved fixed versions of all three, so the environment was upgraded to them (starlette 1.3.1, urllib3 2.7.0, zeroconf 0.149.16).
- **Floor**: the `discovery` extra still allowed `zeroconf>=0.130`, which admits five 2026 advisories fixed between 0.149.5 and 0.149.16 -- unbounded cache and log growth, recursion on chained compression pointers, retained truncated queries and unchecked string reads, all reachable by any host on the local link over UDP 5353, where `discovery` listens. The floor is `zeroconf>=0.149.16`. urllib3 is only transitive and the lock pins a fixed version, so it gets no floor of its own.
- **Guard**: `test_dependency_security_floors.py` parses `pyproject.toml` and fails when the `cryptography`, `starlette` or `zeroconf` lower bound drops below its security floor, naming the advisory.
- **Tests**: `test_dependency_security_floors.py` (new, 3; the zeroconf case fails on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `test/unit_test/headless/test_dependency_security_floors.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-02 · 2026-09-24 · System utilities: Windows shell quoting, keep-awake, clipboard leaks, file triggers, waits, dotenv · #bugfix #windows #audit

- **Shell quoting (Windows)**: `AC_shell_command`, `AC_exec_shell_to_var` and MCP `ac_shell` split a command string with non-POSIX `shlex`, which keeps the quote characters in each token; `subprocess` then quoted them again, so `"C:\Program Files\x.exe"` was not found and `"a b"` reached the child with its quotes. New `command_args()` in `shell_exec.py` passes a string to `CreateProcess` unchanged on native Windows and splits it with POSIX rules elsewhere (Cygwin and MSYS included); a list stays the argv. Still never a shell.
- **`command` keyword**: the Script Builder and docs name the argument `command`, but `ShellManager.exec_shell` only accepted `shell_command`; both are accepted now.
- **Keep-awake**: `SetThreadExecutionState` releases were hard-coded to `ES_CONTINUOUS`, so a second `keep_awake_on()` cancelled itself when its predecessor was released afterwards, and leaving a nested `keep_awake()` block let the machine sleep inside the outer one. A request now restores the state it replaced, and `keep_awake_on` releases the previous request before taking the new one.
- **Clipboard**: `set_clipboard_format` leaked its `GlobalAlloc` handle whenever locking, copying or `SetClipboardData` failed, and the text and image setters duplicated that code. The handle is freed on every failure (ownership passes to the system only on success) and both setters go through the one helper.
- **File trigger**: `FilePathTrigger` never fired for a file created after it started watching, and ignored a replacement whose mtime was older (a copied-in file keeps its source's timestamp). The first poll records a baseline; creation and any mtime change fire.
- **Waits**: `wait_until_file` treated a directory as a finished download and every wait accepted a NaN timeout or poll interval (`NaN <= 0` is false), which never ends. Directories count as not ready; non-positive and NaN values raise `ValueError`.
- **psutil**: without psutil, `AC_assert_process_running` raised `AutoControlAssertionException`, which escapes `raise_on_error=False` as a failed assertion. It raises `AutoControlException` now.
- **Kill race**: `ac_kill_process` raised `NoSuchProcess` when the process exited between lookup and terminate/kill; it reports `terminated` / `killed`.
- **dotenv**: `parse_dotenv` split with `str.splitlines()`, which also breaks on U+2028, U+0085, `\v` and `\f`, so a value containing one was cut. Only CR, LF and CRLF end a line now.
- **Tests**: `test_system_utils_audit.py` (new, 16; 15 run on Windows and all 15 fail on the previous commit, the POSIX-splitting case skips there).
- **Files**: `shell_process/shell_exec.py`, `mcp_server/tools/_handlers_system.py`, `executor/flow_data_commands.py`, `idle_keepawake/idle_keepawake.py`, `clipboard/win32_clipboard_api.py`, `clipboard/clipboard.py`, `triggers/trigger_engine.py`, `smart_waits/waits.py`, `assertion/assertions.py`, `dotenv/dotenv.py`, `test_adapter_registry_sweep.py` (`ac_shell` joins the documented exceptions: it now runs a real child from `command_args`), both `mcp_server_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-03 · 2026-09-24 · A NaN timeout is refused instead of polling forever · #bugfix #audit

- **What**: Python's `json` accepts `NaN`, so an action file or MCP call can pass one as a timeout. `start + NaN` is NaN and `clock() >= NaN` is never true, so every `while True` poll loop that checks its deadline that way ran forever: `AC_wait_image`, `AC_wait_pixel`, `expect_poll` / `AC_expect_poll`, `wait_until_app_idle`, `wait_for_composition_commit`, `wait_for_unlock` / `wait_for_lock`, OCR `wait_for_text` and `wait_for_window`. The MCP `wait_for_image` / `wait_for_pixel` handlers and the pytest keywords loop `while clock() < deadline` and instead gave up at once, reporting a timeout that never elapsed.
- **Fix**: new `utils/timeouts.py` with `deadline_after(start, timeout, name)`, which raises `ValueError` for NaN and otherwise returns `start + float(timeout)`. Negative (look once) and infinite (wait forever) timeouts keep their meaning. All eleven deadline sites above use it. `stable_for_s` / `gone_for_s` in the smart waits refuse NaN as well (`not x >= 0`).
- **Left alone**: the D-Bus client (`socket.settimeout(NaN)` already raises) and internal timeouts no caller supplies (signaling client, MCP server shutdown).
- **Tests**: `test_nan_timeouts.py` (new, 7; all fail on the previous commit, four of them by hanging, which a bounded thread turns into a failure).
- **Files**: `utils/timeouts.py` (new), `app_idle/app_idle.py`, `executor/flow_control.py`, `expect_poll/expect_poll.py`, `ime_state/ime_state.py`, `lock_session/lock_session.py`, `mcp_server/tools/_handlers_screen.py`, `ocr/ocr_engine.py`, `pytest_plugin/keywords.py`, `smart_waits/waits.py`, `wrapper/auto_control_window.py`, `architecture_explore.md` (§5.1 row, line counts), `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-04 · 2026-09-24 · Action and .env files saved with a UTF-8 BOM run, lint and load · #bugfix #windows

- **What**: `read_action_json` -- and with it `je_auto_control validate` -- decodes action files as `utf-8-sig`, so a file saved from Notepad with a BOM validated. `read_executable_action_json`, which every runner uses (`run`, `-e` / `-d`, the scheduler, file / email / webhook triggers, hotkeys, MCP `ac_execute_action_file`, the DAG runner, the Script tab), decoded plain `utf-8` and refused the same file with "Unexpected UTF-8 BOM". The action linter (`python -m je_auto_control.utils.action_lint`) refused it too, and let a non-UTF-8 file escape as a raw `UnicodeDecodeError`. `dotenv_values` / `load_dotenv` read the first key as U+FEFF + name, rejected it as invalid and dropped it silently.
- **Fix**: all three decode `utf-8-sig`, which reads plain UTF-8 unchanged. The signature check still runs over the raw bytes, so signed files verify as before. The linter catches `ValueError`, reporting an undecodable file like any other unreadable one.
- **Tests**: `test_utf8_bom_inputs.py` (new, 4; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `json/json_file.py`, `action_lint/linter.py`, `dotenv/dotenv.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-05 · 2026-09-24 · JSON Schema validator: invalid regex, sub-schema $ref cycles, $ref siblings, nested const, exact multipleOf · #bugfix #audit

- **Invalid regex**: `pattern` / `patternProperties` called `re.search` directly. `re.error` derives from `Exception`, outside the executor's containment, so `AC_validate_json` with a bad pattern aborted the whole script even under `raise_on_error=False`. It raises `AutoControlJsonException` now.
- **Cycles**: cycle detection only followed a chain of `$ref`s, so `{"allOf": [{"$ref": "#"}]}` recursed until `RecursionError`. The validator now tracks the (schema, instance) checks in progress: meeting one again can only recurse forever, and is reported as a `$ref` error. Recursive schemas over nested data validate as before.
- **`$ref` siblings**: keywords next to a `$ref` were dropped, although Draft 2020-12 (which the module claims) applies them; `{"$ref": "#/$defs/s", "maxLength": 3}` accepted any string.
- **Equality**: `const` / `enum` kept `true` and `1` apart only at the top level; inside objects and arrays Python's `==` made them equal. Comparison is recursive now.
- **Numbers**: `multipleOf` used float division, so `10**17 + 1` was a multiple of 2; integers are checked exactly. `uniqueItems` compared JSON text, so `[1, 1.0]` counted as unique; integral floats are normalised first.
- **Tests**: `test_json_schema_audit.py` (new, 12; 10 fail on the previous commit, the other 2 guard recursive schemas and equal containers).
- **Files**: `json_schema/json_schema.py`, both `v57_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-06 · 2026-09-24 · Scheduler: cron jobs no longer refire every tick in the repeated DST hour; a removed job's run leaves its successor alone · #bugfix #audit

- **DST fall-back**: `next_match` works in naive local time, and during the hour repeated when clocks fall back a wall time names two instants. `timestamp()` takes the first, so while the clock was in the second pass every next slot was an hour in the past and a cron job fired on every scheduler tick -- measured 20 fires in 10 s for `*/15 * * * *`, about 7,200 over the hour. `_next_cron_ts` takes a slot at its second instant (`fold=1`) when the first is past and skips a slot past in both, so wildcard jobs keep their pace through the repeated hour and a fixed-time job (`30 1 * * *`) still runs once. `add_cron_job` uses the same helper.
- **Removed while running**: the post-run bookkeeping looked the job up by id only. A job removed while in flight and re-registered under the same id had the old run counted against it (`runs=1`), and with `max_runs=1` it was deleted without ever running. The bookkeeping now applies only to the job object that ran.
- **Tests**: `test_scheduler_audit.py` (new, 3; all fail on the previous commit). The DST case uses a PEP 495 `tzinfo` for the 2026 US fall-back, so it needs no time-zone database and runs the same on every platform.
- **Files**: `scheduler/scheduler.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-07 · 2026-09-24 · Rate limiters and retry budgets refuse NaN; LoopGuard is thread-safe; no repair tactics for a negative budget · #bugfix #audit

- **Rate limiters**: `TokenBucket` and `SlidingWindowLimiter` validated with `<= 0`, which NaN passes. A NaN rate or window let every request through (100 of 100 in 0.1 s), and a NaN capacity made `acquire()` spin at 100% CPU, because the wait it computed was NaN and it slept `0.0` each pass. The values arrive from JSON through `AC_rate_limit`. Rate, capacity and window must be finite and positive; infinity is refused too, since an infinite rate turns the refill into `inf * 0 = NaN`.
- **Retry budget**: `RetryBudget(deadline_s=NaN)` passed the `remaining <= 0` stop and `min()` then ignored it, so the budget retried forever; the dataclass refuses a NaN deadline at construction.
- **LoopGuard**: `observe` appended and then iterated the deque without a lock, and `default_loop_guard` is shared by every executor thread. Four concurrent observers killed three threads with `RuntimeError: deque mutated during iteration`. `observe` and `reset` now hold a lock.
- **Step repair**: `plan_repair` sliced `[:max_attempts]`, so `max_attempts=-1` returned every tactic but the last, reachable through `AC_plan_repair`; a non-positive budget plans nothing. The `run_with_repair` docstring said `sleep` backs off, but it is called with `0` between attempts; the docstring now says so.
- **Tests**: `test_limits_and_guards_audit.py` (new, 15; 10 fail on the previous commit, the other 5 guard values that must stay valid).
- **Files**: `rate_limit/rate_limit.py`, `retry_budget/retry_budget.py`, `loop_guard/loop_guard.py`, `step_repair/step_repair.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-08 · 2026-09-24 · Codegen: every name compiles, NaN/Infinity survive, execute_action keeps its arguments, wrapped files and Robot names · #bugfix #audit #codegen

- **Names**: `_slug` replaced `\W+`, but `\w` also matches characters no identifier may hold (superscript two, vulgar fractions), and a Python keyword passed through untouched, so `name="import"` gave `def import():` and the file did not compile. Characters that cannot continue an identifier become `_`, and a slug that is still not an identifier, or is a keyword, gets the `flow_` prefix. Unicode letters (a Chinese flow name) are kept.
- **NaN / Infinity**: `json` reads them and `repr` writes the bare names `nan` / `inf`, so the generated code raised `NameError`. When the actions hold a non-finite float, the file starts with `from math import inf, nan`; both the `calls` and the `actions` style are covered.
- **`AC_execute_action`**: it maps to the executor's own method, whose `__name__` the facade also exports for a function that takes only `action_list`, so `raise_on_error=True` became a `TypeError`. A command is rendered as a direct `ac.<name>(...)` call only when the facade object is the executor's callable or has the same signature; the other 45 direct commands are unchanged.
- **Wrapped files**: `{"auto_control": [...]}` action files, which the executor runs, were refused with `ValueError: actions must be a non-empty list`; `generate_code` unwraps them, so the CLI `codegen`, `AC_generate_code` and `generate_code_file` all accept them. `generate_code_file` now writes atomically.
- **Robot names**: a leading `*` turned the test-case line into a section header, `#` into a comment and `|` into a pipe-separated row; `${...}` was substituted and `\` began an escape. Those are stripped from the name.
- **Tests**: `test_codegen_audit.py` (new, 22; 14 fail on the previous commit, the other 8 guard names and output that must not change).
- **Files**: `codegen/codegen.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-09 · 2026-09-24 · Step videos from a generator, one-string trajectory rubrics, and the error type in failure bundles · #bugfix #audit

- **Step video**: `write_step_video` read `steps` twice -- once for the overlay plan, once to load the frames -- so a generator was spent by the first pass and the video came out with `frame_count: 0` and no error. The steps are materialised once.
- **Trajectory rubric**: `required_actions` / `forbidden_actions` given as one string (a natural slip in JSON or MCP input) were iterated letter by letter: `"AC"` passed for actions `A` and `C`, and `"click"` never matched the action `click`. A string is one action name.
- **Failure bundle**: the manifest held only `str(error)`, which is empty for `TimeoutError()` and `'k'` for `KeyError("k")`, so the bundle could not say what failed. It gains an additive `error_type` field; `error` is unchanged for existing readers.
- **Tests**: `test_report_helpers_audit.py` (new, 3; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `video_report/video_report.py`, `trajectory_eval/trajectory_eval.py`, `failure_bundle/bundle.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-10 · 2026-09-24 · Email triggers: a failing script fires once, IMAP connections time out, unknown charsets keep their body · #bugfix #audit

- **Re-fire loop**: `_fire_for_uid` and `_execute_with_history` caught `(OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError, AutoControlException)`. Anything else a script or custom executor raised (`KeyError`, `TypeError`) skipped marking the UID seen and the `\Seen` STORE, so the same message ran its script on every poll, each run recorded as `ok` by the `finally` block, and `poll_once()` raised to its caller. Any exception is now recorded as an error run and the UID is still marked processed.
- **Timeout**: `IMAP4_SSL` / `IMAP4` were opened without `timeout=`. A server that accepted and never sent a greeting hung the watcher thread for good; `stop()` gave up on it, `is_running` read `False` and `start()` added a second hung thread. Every connect and command is bounded at 30 s (`_IMAP_TIMEOUT_S`), and the failure is recorded like any other.
- **Unknown charsets**: `get_content()` raises `LookupError` for charsets such as `unknown-8bit` or `x-user-defined`, and the body became `""`. The raw bytes are decoded as UTF-8 with replacement instead.
- **Tests**: `test_email_trigger_audit.py` (new, 4; all fail on the previous commit). They fake the history store and the error snapshot, so no row reaches the real run-history database and no screenshot is taken. `test_email_trigger.py`'s fake IMAP accepts the new `timeout=`.
- **Files**: `triggers/email_trigger.py`, `test_email_trigger.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-11 · 2026-09-24 · Trigger engine skips triggers removed mid-pass; poll threads survive infinite intervals and any rule error; callback executor returns its documented None · #bugfix #audit

- **Removed mid-pass**: `TriggerEngine._poll_once` fired from a snapshot of candidates, so a trigger that an earlier trigger's script removed or disabled in the same pass still ran its script, although `remove()` had returned `True`. Each candidate is re-checked under the lock (`_still_armed`) right before it fires.
- **Infinite interval**: the trigger engine and the screen observer clamped their interval with `max(0.05, x)`, which lets infinity through, and `Event.wait(inf)` raises `OverflowError` on Windows, killing the poll thread after one pass. New `clamp_poll_interval()` in `utils/timeouts.py` bounds it to 0.05 s to one hour (NaN gives the floor); both loops use it.
- **Observer rules**: `_evaluate` caught a fixed tuple around the predicate only, and `_transition` ran outside it. A predicate raising `NameError`, a handler raising `AssertionError`, or a value whose truth test raises (a numpy array) killed the daemon thread and silently stopped every rule. Each rule is guarded as a whole and logged; the others keep firing.
- **Callback executor**: `callback_function` documents `None` when the trigger fails and keeps the trigger's result when the callback fails, but caught only `(AutoControlException, TypeError, ValueError, RuntimeError)`, so an `OSError` from `AC_create_project` escaped and a callback's `OSError` discarded the result. Both scopes catch any exception and log it.
- **Tests**: `test_trigger_observer_audit.py` (new, 5; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `utils/timeouts.py`, `triggers/trigger_engine.py`, `observer/observer.py`, `callback/callback_function_executor.py`, `architecture_explore.md` (`utils/timeouts.py` row, line counts), `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-12 · 2026-09-24 · Webhook server: chunked bodies, case-insensitive Bearer, and an answer when run history fails · #bugfix #audit #security

- **Chunked bodies**: a request sent with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` has no Content-Length, so the body read as `""`: the bound script ran with an empty `webhook.body` / `webhook.json` and the client got 200 for data nobody read. New `read_chunked_body()` in `utils/http_headers.py` decodes the stream under the same 1 MiB cap (chunk extensions and trailers are accepted and discarded); a malformed or truncated stream gets 400, an oversized one 413, and the connection is closed either way. `_read_body` returns `None` once it has answered an error, replacing the old `""`-plus-Content-Length test that could not tell an empty chunked body from a failure.
- **Bearer scheme**: `authorize` compared the whole header to `Bearer <token>`, so `bearer <token>` got 401 although RFC 7235 makes the scheme case-insensitive. The scheme is matched case-insensitively and only the token is compared, still with `hmac.compare_digest`.
- **History failure**: `fire()` calls `start_run` before its own try, so a `HistoryStoreError` (a locked database) escaped the handler, the client saw the connection drop and a traceback reached stderr. `_dispatch` answers 500 `{"fired": false}` instead.
- **Not changed**: the REST API and the MCP HTTP transport already refuse a chunked body with 400 rather than accepting it silently.
- **Tests**: `test_webhook_audit.py` (new, 10; the three server cases fail on the previous commit, the seven decoder cases test the new helper).
- **Files**: `utils/http_headers.py`, `triggers/webhook_server.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-13 · 2026-09-24 · macOS and Linux hotkeys stop retrying a failed combo every tick; bind() validates on macOS · #bugfix #audit #macos #linux

- **What**: the macOS and Linux backends re-sync their bindings about ten times a second, and a combo that could not be parsed (`ctrl+.` has no key code in either table) or, on Linux, grabbed (another client holds it) was retried and logged on every sync for as long as the daemon ran. The Windows backend already remembered failures; the other two never got it. `HotkeyDaemon.bind()` only validated the key on Windows, so macOS accepted `ctrl+.` and failed later, every tick.
- **Fix**: new `FailedCombos` in `hotkey/backends/base.py` records a binding whose current combo failed and skips it until the combo changes; failures of removed bindings are forgotten. The macOS and Linux backends use it for parse failures, and Linux for grab failures too. On macOS a changed combo now also drops the old registration even when the new combo fails, so the old keys stop firing the binding. `bind()` checks the key with the macOS parser on darwin; Linux cannot check without a live X display, so there the memo is what stops the loop.
- **Not changed**: the Windows backend keeps its own equivalent dict; moving it onto `FailedCombos` would be a separate refactor.
- **Tests**: `test_hotkey_failed_combos.py` (new, 6; 5 fail on the previous commit, the sixth tests the new class). They drive `_sync` / `_sync_one` with fakes, so no display, event tap or real hotkey is involved.
- **Files**: `hotkey/backends/base.py`, `hotkey/backends/macos_backend.py`, `hotkey/backends/linux_backend.py`, `hotkey/hotkey_daemon.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-14 · 2026-09-24 · Replay scrolls the recorded way on X11/Wayland, releases where the button went down; paths round; NaN holds refused · #bugfix #audit

- **Replay scroll**: `_sink_scroll` called `mouse_scroll(value)`, whose `scroll_direction` defaults to `scroll_down` -- read only by X11 and Wayland -- while the recorders and the Windows / macOS backends treat a positive value as up. A wheel-up recorded on Windows replayed as wheel-down on X11. The sink names `scroll_up`, so a positive value means up everywhere.
- **Cleanup release**: after a failed step `replay_timeline` releases every held button with an event that carries no point, and `_sink_mouse_up` filled in `(0, 0)`; on macOS the release was posted in the top-left corner, where a hot corner can fire. A missing point is passed as `None`, which means the current cursor position.
- **Waypoints**: `plan_path` truncated with `int()`, so `-0.6` (a point on a monitor left of the primary one) became `0` and `100.7` became `100`, while `tween_points` rounds. Waypoints are rounded to the nearest pixel.
- **Key hold**: `plan_key_hold` validated with `<= 0`, which NaN passes, so the real key went down before `sleep(NaN)` raised. Non-finite durations and rates are refused up front.
- **Deferred**: the audit's findings in `wrapper/auto_control_keyboard.py` and `wrapper/auto_control_mouse.py` (upper-case letters and `is_shift` on Windows / X11, `\r\n` typing two Enters, the X11 scroll default, NaN in `mouse_scroll`, truncated coordinates) change what the Jeffrey_RPA batch types and scrolls, and that batch is running from this working tree; they are recorded in `Progress.md` until it is idle.
- **Tests**: `test_input_helpers_audit.py` (new, 7; 6 fail on the previous commit, the NaN-rate case already failed there through `round(NaN)`). All input goes to fakes.
- **Files**: `input_macro/input_macro.py`, `mouse_path/mouse_path.py`, `key_hold/key_hold.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the wrapper findings above (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-15 · 2026-09-24 · Signing, encryption and JWT keys, passwords and tokens are masked in the executor log, its record and the MCP audit file · #security #audit

- **Executor log and record**: `redact_actions` masked only `AC_secret_*` arguments. The key given to `AC_sign_action_file`, `AC_verify_action_file`, `AC_encrypt_action_file`, `AC_decrypt_action_file`, `AC_jwt_encode` and `AC_jwt_decode` -- and every `password` / `token` argument (`AC_email_trigger_add`, `AC_webhook_add`, `AC_remote_connect`...) -- was written to the `autocontrol_logger` output and used as the result record's key, which REST, MCP, the socket server and run history return. Arguments whose name marks a secret (`SENSITIVE_ARGUMENT_NAMES`: password, passphrase, token, secret, api_key, private_key, client_secret, authorization, access/refresh token) are masked for every command, and `key` for the six keyed commands; `key` elsewhere is a keyboard key and stays readable. Nested commands are masked with their parent, as before.
- **MCP audit file**: `_sanitise` checked top-level argument names only, against a list without `key` or `passphrase`, so `ac_execute_actions` with a nested `AC_secret_unlock` passphrase and `ac_jwt_encode`'s key reached the JSONL file. It walks every dict and list, masks the same names plus `key` (conservative: an audit file has no keyboard keys worth keeping), and runs action lists through `redact_actions`. `REDACTED_KEYS` is now `SENSITIVE_ARGUMENT_NAMES`.
- **Tests**: `test_secret_redaction_audit.py` (new, 11; 10 fail on the previous commit, the eleventh guards readable non-secret arguments). Fake values only.
- **Also in this push**: `test_codegen_audit.py` checks the generated `nan` / `inf` names with `ast` instead of `exec`, which Codacy flagged.
- **Files**: `executor/action_redaction.py`, `mcp_server/audit.py`, both `mcp_server_doc.rst`, `test_codegen_audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-16 · 2026-09-24 · Signed-action enforcement covers remote DAG nodes; UserAuthError and CredentialBrokerError join the framework family · #security #audit

- **Remote DAG nodes**: every local execution path reads action files through `read_executable_action_json`, which enforces `JE_AUTOCONTROL_REQUIRE_SIGNED_ACTIONS`, but `_resolve_remote_actions` loaded a remote node's `action_file` with a plain `json.load` and dispatched it through the admin console. With enforcement on, an unsigned file ran on the remote host. It goes through `read_executable_action_json` now (which also accepts a UTF-8 BOM, as the local paths do); inline `actions` on a node are unchanged.
- **Exception family**: `UserAuthError` (RBAC) and `CredentialBrokerError` (governance) derived from `RuntimeError` only, which `CLAUDE.md` forbids: they escaped every `except AutoControlException` containment boundary. Both derive from `(AutoControlException, RuntimeError)`, as `SecretStoreError` already does; nothing catches them by name, so no handler changes behaviour.
- **Tests**: `test_signing_and_error_family_audit.py` (new, 4; 3 fail on the previous commit, the fourth guards loading without enforcement).
- **Files**: `dag/runner.py`, `rbac/users.py`, `governance/credential_broker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-17 · 2026-09-24 · Audit hash chain: no re-blessing of cleared hashes, deletions from the top caught, clear() leaves a record · #security #audit

- **Re-blessing**: `_backfill_chain_locked` ran on every open and re-hashed any row whose `row_hash` was NULL. Editing a row and clearing its hash made the next open chain the forgery, and `verify_chain()` reported `ok`. The backfill is now a one-off migration marked with `PRAGMA user_version = 1`; after it a NULL hash is a broken link.
- **Deleting the oldest rows**: `verify_chain` started from the first row's own `prev_hash` (to allow for pruning), so deleting rows from the top went unnoticed. A `chain_meta` table holds the anchor the first row must point at: the genesis hash, or the hash of the last row automatic pruning removed, updated when it prunes. A log pruned before the anchor existed takes its first row's `prev_hash` as the anchor during the migration.
- **`clear()`**: it wiped the table and left nothing, so a cleared log verified as a clean empty one. It now resets the anchor and starts the new chain with an `audit_log_cleared` event recording how many rows it deleted; `test_audit_log.py`'s clear case now expects that one event.
- **Limits, now documented**: the hashes are unkeyed SHA-256, so whoever can write the database can rebuild the chain, and dropping the newest rows is not detectable from the file alone. Both operations docs say so and suggest keeping the last `row_hash` elsewhere.
- **Tests**: `test_audit_chain_audit.py` (new, 5; 4 fail on the previous commit -- the migration case because the old schema has no `chain_meta` -- and the pruning case guards that automatic pruning still verifies).
- **Files**: `remote_desktop/audit_log.py`, `test_audit_log.py`, both `operations_layer_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-18 · 2026-09-24 · User store keeps a damaged file and refuses shared tokens; secret managers stop overwriting each other; malformed vaults are store errors · #security #audit

- **Damaged user file**: `UserStore._load` returned an empty store when `users.json` could not be parsed, and the next `add_user` / `set_role` / `rotate_token` saved that, replacing every user on disk. The store now logs the reason, lets nobody sign in, and refuses to overwrite the file until it is repaired or removed.
- **Tags**: `"tags": 5` in the file made the constructor raise a raw `TypeError`; tags that are not a list read as `[]`, and `add_user` normalises its argument the same way.
- **Shared token**: `add_user` accepted a caller-supplied token another user already had, and `authenticate` returns the first match, so a viewer's token could sign in as an admin. A token in use is refused.
- **Lost updates**: each `SecretManager` wrote back its own cached vault, so two on one file (the GUI and a service process) undid each other's `set` / `remove`. Every operation re-reads the vault from disk; one re-keyed elsewhere locks the manager (`SecretStoreLocked`), which must unlock again. There is still no cross-process lock: two writes in the same instant can race, but no longer a whole session apart.
- **Malformed vault**: a missing `salt` raised `KeyError` from `unlock` and `iterations: 0` a `ValueError`; `_load_vault` checks the fields and raises `SecretStoreError`.
- **Docs**: the `utils/rbac` docstring said the REST and MCP servers consult it and the audit log gained a `user_id` field; neither is true, and it now says it is a building block that nothing uses yet (map row updated).
- **Tests**: `test_stores_audit.py` (new, 9; all fail on the previous commit). Temporary directories and fake values only.
- **Files**: `rbac/users.py`, `rbac/__init__.py`, `secrets/secret_store.py`, `architecture_explore.md` (`utils/rbac/` row, line counts), `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Open items**: RBAC is not wired to the REST API or MCP server (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-19 · 2026-09-24 · REST API: authenticate before reading the body, never lock out the valid token, survive a corrupt audit database · #security #audit

- **Body before auth**: `do_POST` read and parsed the JSON body before `_dispatch` ran the auth gate, so an unauthenticated client could send 1 MB bodies at will, and a bad one got 400 -- never 401 or 429 -- without touching the rate limit, the lockout or the audit trail. The body is now read only after the route and the gate pass. A rejected or unknown-path request has its declared body drained (capped) before the answer, since Windows otherwise resets the connection before the client reads the 401 / 404.
- **Lockout DoS**: `RestAuthGate.check` tested the lockout before the token, keyed by IP alone -- every local client is 127.0.0.1 and every proxied one the proxy -- so eight bad requests a minute from anyone kept the real token holder out indefinitely. A valid token is checked first and always passes; the lockout answers further wrong tokens, and the per-IP rate limit still applies to everyone. The tokens are random, so the lockout was never what stopped guessing.
- **Corrupt audit database**: `_open_audit_log` caught `OSError` / `RuntimeError` / `ImportError`, but `AuditLog()` raises `AuditLogError` for a corrupt file, so `RestApiServer()` raised instead of running without the audit hook as intended.
- **Comment**: the handler's 30 s timeout bounds each read, not the request; the comment said it stopped stalled clients outright. It now says what it does.
- **Tests**: `test_rest_audit_fixes.py` (new, 5; 4 fail on the previous commit, the fifth guards the authorised path). The `/execute` handler is a fake.
- **Files**: `rest_api/rest_server.py`, `rest_api/rest_auth.py`, `test_http_content_length.py` (its REST case sends the token, since an unauthenticated request is now answered 401 before the header is read), both `operations_layer_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-20 · 2026-09-24 · MCP HTTP: anonymous initialize floods cannot evict a session in use; DELETE checks its path; state of a session dropped mid-request is released · #security #audit #mcp

- **Eviction**: at the session cap `SessionRegistry.create` evicted the least recently seen session. A client that ignores the session header mints a new session on every `initialize`, and the transport has no token by default, so 128 of them evicted a session a real client was holding (it then got 404 `unknown or expired session`). The victim is now the oldest session never used after its `initialize` -- the same test `_log_eviction` already used to decide how loudly to report it -- and only when every session is in use the oldest of those.
- **DELETE path**: `do_DELETE` never looked at the path, so `DELETE /anything` with a session header ended the session, while GET and POST answer 404 off `/mcp`. It answers 404 too.
- **Orphaned state**: a session evicted, swept or deleted between `_resolve_session` and `handle_line` still had its `initialize` capabilities stored under the dead id after the drop hook had run, and nothing released them (50 injected drops left 50 entries, still there after `stop()`). After handling a request the transport calls `forget_connection` for a session that was closed meanwhile.
- **Tests**: `test_mcp_http_audit_fixes.py` (new, 4; 3 fail on the previous commit, the fourth guards eviction when every session is in use).
- **Files**: `mcp_server/http_sessions.py`, `mcp_server/http_transport.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-21 · 2026-09-24 · Socket server reads whole pretty-printed commands; the documented client example works · #bugfix #audit

- **Framing**: `_read_command` stopped at the first TCP chunk containing a newline. A newline is ordinary whitespace in JSON, so an indented command was cut at the first chunk boundary and failed with `Expecting value`, and one sent in two segments was refused after the first while the client's second write hit a reset. A command now ends at a newline that is the last byte received *and* after which the text either parses or fails before its end (malformed -- answered with the error at once, as before). A parse error exactly at the end means the command is still arriving. Two commands in one connection are still one malformed command: the protocol is one command per connection.
- **Docs**: the socket driver page's client example sent the JSON without the newline terminator and read one `recv`. The server then waited for the terminator until its 30 s read timeout and dropped the connection, so the example got nothing. The example sends the newline and reads until `Return_Data_Over_JE`, and the protocol table states the request and response framing (both languages).
- **Comment**: the handler's timeout bounds each read, not the command; the comment now says so.
- **Not changed**: result values are still written as `str(value)` lines, so a result containing a newline or the marker can confuse a line-based client. Changing that changes the protocol other tools read.
- **Tests**: `test_socket_framing.py` (new, 7; 6 fail on the previous commit, the malformed case guards the immediate answer). The executor is a fake.
- **Files**: `socket_server/auto_control_socket_server.py`, both `socket_driver_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-22 · 2026-09-24 · Remote desktop host: failed logins free their slot, view-only means view-only, an allowlist of typos admits nobody · #security #audit

- **Slot leak**: handlers join the client table before they authenticate, and the host reaps only handlers whose `_shutdown` is set. On an auth failure `start()` only closed the socket, so two wrong-token attempts against `max_clients=2` refused the real viewer until the host restarted. Every failed handshake now calls `stop()`. An approval callback raising outside `(RuntimeError, ValueError, TypeError)` killed the handshake thread the same way; the callback is user code, and any exception now denies the viewer. An unexpected error in the handshake stops the handler before propagating.
- **Handshake deadline**: the 60 s auth timeout bounded each read, so a peer trickling a byte at a time held the handshake -- and its slot -- indefinitely. A watchdog closes the socket when the whole handshake exceeds it.
- **View-only**: only `INPUT` was gated on `PERMISSION_VIEW_ONLY`, so a view-only viewer could set the host clipboard and write files anywhere on the host (a file in the Startup folder is full control at the next logon). `CLIPBOARD` and every `FILE_*` message are dropped for view-only viewers.
- **Allowlist**: `_compile_ip_allowlist` returned `compiled or None`, and `_ip_in_allowlist` treated an empty list as no filtering, so a list whose every entry was invalid (`192.168.1.300`, `10.0.0.1/33`) admitted everyone -- the opposite of the docstring's promise. Such a list admits nobody now; a list of only blank strings is still no list. `test_remote_desktop_ip_allowlist.py` no longer asserts that a compiled empty list admits everyone and gains the typo case.
- **Not changed**: a host can still push a file to any path on a viewer; that is the open `Progress.md` DECIDE item on confining viewer downloads.
- **Tests**: `test_remote_host_access_audit.py` (new, 5; all fail on the previous commit). Input and capture are fakes.
- **Files**: `remote_desktop/host_client.py`, `remote_desktop/host_access.py`, `test_remote_desktop_ip_allowlist.py`, both `new_features_doc.rst` (view-only and allowlist paragraphs, committed separately because another session has edits in those files), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-23 · 2026-09-24 · Remote desktop stores keep damaged files aside; interrupted uploads are cleaned up; stopping the relay ends its sessions · #bugfix #audit

- **Damaged stores**: `TrustList`, `KnownHosts` and `AddressBook` read a damaged file as empty, and the next `add` / `remember` / `upsert` rewrote it with only the new entry -- for `known_hosts` that silently reset every pinned fingerprint, so the next connection re-trusted whatever answered. A non-UTF-8 address book raised `UnicodeDecodeError`, and `{"entries": 5}` / `{"viewers": 5}` a `TypeError`, from the constructor. New `load_json_or_quarantine()` / `quarantine_file()` in `json_store.py` move an unreadable or wrong-shaped file aside as `<name>.corrupt-<time>` with a warning and let the store start clean; all three use them. `AddressBook._save` and `load_or_create_host_fingerprint` write atomically (0600 from creation) instead of `write_text` then `chmod`.
- **Interrupted uploads**: the host-wide `FileReceiver` never abandoned a transfer whose viewer disconnected or whose host stopped, leaving the `.part` file and its open handle, although the module promises a failed transfer never leaves one. `FileReceiver.abort()` / `abort_all()` close and delete them; each client handler aborts the transfers it began when it stops, and `host.stop()` aborts the rest.
- **Relay**: `RelayServer.stop()` closed the listener and parked peers only; paired sessions kept forwarding on their pump threads. The pumps watch the server's shutdown event and end within one poll interval.
- **Not changed**: the viewer still writes host-pushed files wherever the host names (the open `Progress.md` DECIDE item), and the remote-desktop protocol errors still derive from `RuntimeError` / `ValueError` only.
- **Tests**: `test_remote_stores_and_cleanup.py` (new, 7; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `json_store/json_store.py`, `remote_desktop/trust_list.py`, `remote_desktop/fingerprint.py`, `remote_desktop/address_book.py`, `remote_desktop/file_transfer.py`, `remote_desktop/host_client.py`, `remote_desktop/host.py`, `remote_desktop/relay.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-24 · 2026-09-24 · Masked, sub-pixel, auto-threshold and scale matchers answer in screen coordinates; golden images capture in mouse coordinates · #bugfix #audit #vision

- **Lost origin**: `_masked_scores` and `_score_map` captured through `_haystack_gray`, which drops the frame's origin, so `match_masked`, `match_masked_all`, `match_subpixel`, `match_auto` and `detect_scale` returned positions local to the captured frame. With `region=(100,30,80,50)` a template at (120, 40) came back at (20, 10) -- and on a desktop whose virtual origin is negative, every answer was off even without a region. `AC_match_masked*` and the matching MCP tools were affected. New `_score_map_with_origin()` returns the origin with the map (`_score_map` stays, for callers that only read scores, such as `match_trust`), `_masked_scores` returns it too, and every caller that reports a position adds it. `match_template` and `match_template_all` already did.
- **Golden images**: `visual_regression`'s `_grab` cropped with `ImageGrab.grab(bbox=...)`, which works in physical pixels, so on a 200 % display `take_golden(region=(50,25,100,50))` saved the logical area (25..75, 12..37). It captures through `grab_logical` like every other capture. Region goldens taken on a scaled display before this need re-taking; full-screen goldens and unscaled displays are unaffected.
- **Deferred**: the audit's findings on the Jeffrey_RPA-live path -- template loading from non-ASCII paths and grayscale templates (`cv2_utils/template_detection.py`), region clipping in `monitor_layout/logical_frame.py`, the OCR span search in `ocr/text_span.py`, and centre rounding on negative coordinates in `wrapper/auto_control_image.py` -- are recorded in `Progress.md` with the other input fixes held while that batch runs.
- **Tests**: `test_match_origin_audit.py` (new, 3; all fail on the previous commit). Captures are faked.
- **Files**: `visual_match/visual_match.py`, `match_autothresh/match_autothresh.py`, `subpixel_match/subpixel_match.py`, `scale_detect/scale_detect.py`, `visual_regression/compare.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the deferred findings above (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-25 · 2026-09-24 · Plugin discovery loads only the je_auto_control.commands entry-point group · #security #audit

- **What**: `discover_plugins` / `load_plugins` take a `group` that reaches them from action files (`AC_list_plugins`, `AC_load_plugins`) and MCP calls (`ac_list_plugins`, annotated read-only and kept by `--read-only`; `ac_load_plugins`), and discovery calls every entry point of that group -- `point.load()()` -- even when only listing. `group="console_scripts"` therefore ran the `main()` of every installed command-line tool (80 on this machine, pip and this package's own CLI and MCP server among them) from a read-only tool.
- **Fix**: `_entry_points` refuses any group other than `je_auto_control.commands` with `ValueError` before anything loads. The parameter stays, so existing calls with the default keep working; injected entry points (the test seam) are unaffected. Listing still runs the factories of AutoControl plugins themselves, which is how they report their command names.
- **Tests**: `test_plugin_group_audit.py` (new, 3; 2 fail on the previous commit, the third guards the default group and injected points).

- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-26 · 2026-09-24 · USB passthrough ACL: a deleted signature fails closed, damaged files are kept aside, rule ids are validated, instances stop overwriting each other · #security #audit #usb

- **Deleted signature**: a missing `.sig` was accepted as a legacy unsigned file, so rewriting `usb_acl.json` to `{"default":"allow","rules":[]}` and deleting the sidecar granted every device -- the module docstring promised a rewrite could not grant itself access without forging the signature. Legacy now applies only while no signing key exists; with a key file (or an explicit key) a missing signature fails closed like a wrong one.
- **Damaged file**: a file that failed to parse, had an unsupported version or invalid rules left the in-memory ACL empty, and the next `acl_add` / `set_default` rewrote and re-signed it with just the new rule. It is now moved aside as `usb_acl.json.corrupt-<time>` (the shared `quarantine_file`) and the ACL falls back to deny-all. Saves are atomic and 0600 from creation.
- **Rule ids**: `AclRule` stored ids as given, so a deny rule written `01050`, `1050:0407` or `10_50` never matched and did nothing under an `allow` default. Ids are normalised with `normalize_usb_id` when a rule is built (`0x1050` becomes `1050`) and malformed ones raise `ValueError`. `remove_rule` compares ids the way `matches` does, so a rule stored as `0x1050` can be removed as `1050`.
- **Lost updates**: every instance wrote its own snapshot, so a host session and the GUI undid each other's rules. Each persisted change re-reads the file first.
- **USB/IP**: `_translate_error` computed `-getattr(error, "errno", 5)`, and pyusb raises `USBError` with `errno=None` for `LIBUSB_ERROR_OTHER`; `-None` raised `TypeError`, which the server's handler did not catch, and the client connection dropped instead of the URB returning `-EIO`.
- **Not changed (Progress.md)**: the viewer client matches replies to requests by kind only, so a reply that arrives after its request timed out completes the next one; fixing that needs a request id on the wire.
- **Tests**: `test_usb_acl_audit.py` (new, 9; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `usb/passthrough/acl.py`, `usbip/libusb_backend.py`, both `usb_passthrough_design.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the viewer reply matching (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-27 · 2026-09-24 · Admin console: no token on redirects, whole-request timeout and size cap, unknown labels reported, host files kept intact; USB viewer reassembly capped · #security #audit

- **Redirects**: `_http_request` used `urllib.request.urlopen`, whose redirect handler re-sends the `Authorization` header to wherever a host redirects -- another origin, or https downgraded to http -- and the poll then reported the host healthy. The console now uses an opener that refuses every redirect; a 3xx is an error for that host.
- **Timeout and size**: `timeout_s` bounded each socket read, so a host dripping a byte at a time held a poll or broadcast indefinitely, and `response.read()` took any body whole. Bodies are read with `read1` against a deadline of `timeout_s` for the whole response, capped at 16 MiB.
- **Unknown labels**: `broadcast_execute(labels=[...])` dropped labels that named no host, so a typo looked like a complete broadcast. Each one returns `{"label": ..., "ok": false, "error": "unknown host"}`.
- **Labels**: `add_host` validated before stripping, so a whitespace-only label became `""` and the host vanished on the next load.
- **Host file**: a damaged `admin_hosts.json` read as empty and the next `add_host` replaced it with one host; it is now moved aside (`quarantine_file`). An entry the loader cannot use (an unknown field from a newer version) was skipped and then dropped by the next save, token and all; it is written back unchanged.
- **USB viewer**: `_reassemble` buffered fragments without EOF without limit, for any claim; a message may not grow past 2 MiB (a 1 MiB endpoint transfer base64-encoded, plus framing) and an oversized one is dropped.
- **Not changed (Progress.md)**: a broadcast row's `ok` still means the host answered 200, not that the actions succeeded; and the USB viewer still matches replies to requests by kind only.
- **Tests**: `test_admin_console_audit.py` (new, 7; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `admin/admin_client.py`, `usb/passthrough/viewer_client.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the two above (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-28 · 2026-09-24 · JWT codec: strict base64url, every malformed token a JwtError, NaN expiry refused, alg not overridable · #security #audit

- **Token malleability**: `urlsafe_b64decode` silently drops characters outside the alphabet, so `token + "!!"` verified like the token -- many strings per signature, which defeats a denylist or replay cache keyed on the token. Each segment must now match `[A-Za-z0-9_-]*`.
- **Error family**: `decode_jwt` documents a `JwtError` on any failure, but bad header/payload JSON raised `JSONDecodeError`, a JSON list `AttributeError`, `exp: "soon"` `ValueError` and `aud: 5` `TypeError`; `AC_jwt_decode` catches only `JwtError`, so those tokens produced a failed action instead of `{"ok": false}`. Header and payload must be JSON objects, `exp` / `nbf` finite numbers and `aud` a string or list of strings, each checked with a `JwtError`.
- **NaN expiry**: Python's `json` reads `NaN`, and `now > NaN` is false, so a token with `"exp": NaN` never expired; non-finite time claims are refused.
- **Header override**: `encode_jwt(headers={"alg": "none"})` labelled an HS256-signed token `none` (an HS512 override produced a token that could not be decoded); `alg` is set after the extra headers.
- **Adapter**: `AC_jwt_decode` passed a plain `algorithms="HS256"` to `json.loads` and raised; a string that is not a JSON list is one algorithm name. That hunk is committed separately because another session has edits in `action_executor.py`.
- **Tests**: `test_jwt_audit.py` (new, 8; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `jwt/jwt_codec.py`, `executor/action_executor.py` (adapter), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-29 · 2026-09-24 · Versioned store keeps its high-water marks and locks CAS; config-bundle import copies before writing; cassettes redact credentials and check match fields · #bugfix #security #audit

- **Versioned store (ABA)**: `_high_water` exists so a deleted-then-recreated key does not reuse a version, but it was not saved, so after `save` / `load` the key restarted at version 1 and a stale writer holding the old version 1 succeeded. `save` writes `{"records", "high_water"}` atomically; `load` also reads the older bare-records file.
- **Versioned store (lock)**: `put` / `delete` are check-then-write with no lock, and the store is shared by concurrent servers through `AC_cas_put`; two writers with `expected_version=0` both succeeded. Every operation now holds an `RLock`.
- **Config bundle**: before writing, the importer *moved* the existing file to `.bak.<ts>`; when the write then failed the live file was gone and only the backup remained, although the module promises a non-destructive import. It copies to the backup (`shutil.copy2`) and leaves the original until the atomic write replaces it. Docstrings and both operations docs say copy.
- **Cassette credentials**: request headers were recorded verbatim (`build_call`'s `auth` becomes an `Authorization` header) and response `Set-Cookie` too. `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Set-Cookie`, `X-Api-Key` and `X-Auth-Token` are recorded as `<redacted>`.
- **Cassette matching**: an unknown `match_on` field (`"methd"`) matched every request, and `"headers"` was recorded but never compared, so tenant B was served tenant A's response. Unknown fields raise `ValueError`; `"headers"` compares the recorded headers case-insensitively, skipping redacted ones. Both feature docs updated.
- **Tests**: `test_store_cassette_audit.py` (new, 6; 5 fail on the previous commit, the sixth guards the older file format).
- **Files**: `optimistic/optimistic.py`, `config_bundle/config_bundle.py`, `http_cassette/http_cassette.py`, both `v75_features_doc.rst`, both `operations_layer_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-30 · 2026-09-24 · AC_parallel branches inherit strictness, macro depth and failure counting; string branches are validated before anything runs · #bugfix #audit #executor

- **Strictness**: each branch runs on a new thread, where the thread-local `_STRICT_BODIES` starts false, so a failing action inside a branch was only recorded: `AC_parallel` returned success under `raise_on_error=True`, `AC_try` around it never ran `catch`, and `AC_retry` made one attempt. The parent's strictness is captured before the threads start and passed to each branch's `execute_action`.
- **Failure count**: `recorded_failures()` is per thread, so failures recorded inside branches never reached the caller -- nor the exit code of `je_auto_control run`. Each worker's count is added to the parent thread's after the join.
- **Macro depth**: `_MACRO_DEPTH` is per thread too, so a macro calling itself through an `AC_parallel` branch restarted at 0 on every level and nested without bound (one blocked thread per level; a probe reached 120 levels with `MAX_MACRO_DEPTH` 50). Each branch starts at the parent's depth.
- **String branches**: a JSON-string `branches` is parsed only at run time, after dispatch-time validation, and then ran with `_validated=True`, so the actions before an unknown command had already run. Each parsed branch is validated like the list form before any thread starts. Two flow-control tests that used this gap to trigger an in-thread failure now assert the up-front refusal.
- **Not changed (Progress.md)**: variables seeded by `execute_action_with_vars` or set by one REST / MCP / socket run stay in the global executor's scope for the next run.
- **Tests**: `test_parallel_audit.py` (new, 5; 4 fail on the previous commit and the recursion case does not terminate there); `test_flow_control.py`, `test_flow_control_audit.py` (updated as above).
- **Files**: `executor/flow_control.py`, the three test files, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the variable scope between runs (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-31 · 2026-09-24 · Executor: AC_run_dag expanded once, nested AC_execute_action inherits strictness, every repeated or unwound action keeps its record · #bugfix #audit #executor

- **Double expansion**: `AC_run_dag` was missing from `_DEFERRED_COMMAND_KEYS`, so `definition.nodes[].actions` was placeholder-expanded at dispatch and again when the node ran. A variable whose value contained `${...}` was resolved a second time -- `${secrets.NAME}` included, the leak that map exists to prevent -- and a variable set earlier in the same node failed as unknown. `definition` is deferred like the other nested-action keys.
- **Nested strictness**: `execute_action` computed `raise_on_error or (_validated and inherited)`, and a nested `AC_execute_action` runs with `_validated=False`, so inside an `AC_try` / `AC_retry` body it ran non-strict: its failures were recorded and `catch` never ran. It now inherits the enclosing strictness; a top-level call still sees none, because the flag is restored on the way out.
- **Record keys**: `_record_unwound_signal` (a break / continue / macro-depth signal reaching the top level) wrote the bare key, overwriting an earlier identical action's result, and named the outer action ("AC_call_macro outside a loop"). A dry run also collapsed repeated identical actions to one entry. One `_unique_key` helper now picks the ` #N` suffix for all three, and the message names `AC_break` / `AC_continue`.
- **Not changed (Progress.md)**: variables seeded by `execute_action_with_vars` or set by one REST / MCP / socket run remain in the global executor for the next run -- a behaviour some callers may rely on.
- **Tests**: `test_executor_records_audit.py` (new, 4; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `executor/action_executor.py` (committed alongside another session's uncommitted edits by applying this commit's patch), `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the variable scope between runs (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-32 · 2026-09-24 · Screen recordings play back at their real length; unusable writers are refused; iOS find_element honours its timeout · #bugfix #audit

- **Pacing**: `ScreenRecordThread` and `RecordingThread` wrote frames as fast as capture allowed while the file header declared the frame rate, so a 2 s recording at 30 fps with capture at ~200/s held 120 frames and played for 4 s (and 18 s at 20 fps for `RecordingThread`). New `cv2_utils/frame_clock.py`: `record_paced()` writes exactly the frames the clock owes -- the last frame again when capture falls behind, a (stoppable) wait when it runs ahead -- and `check_fps()` validates the rate. Both recorders use it.
- **Screenshot records**: `ScreenRecordThread` captured through the public `screenshot()`, which records an `AC_screenshot` test action and logs a line per frame, so a recording filled the test record and generated reports. It captures through `pil_screenshot` directly.
- **Unopened writer**: fps 0 / negative / NaN, an unknown codec or a missing directory give a `VideoWriter` that never opens; it was accepted, `start_new_record` returned normally and the thread captured the screen in a busy loop writing nothing. The thread now validates fps and resolution and raises `AutoControlScreenException` (releasing the writer) before it starts. `RecordingThread` contains the same error.
- **iOS**: `find_element` discarded the element `query.wait()` returned and read `query.bounds`, which in facebook-wda looks the element up again with the client's default 30 s timeout: an element gone in between took 30 s and surfaced as `IOSUnavailableError` rather than `ElementNotFoundError`, and every successful lookup made an extra round-trip. It reads the waited-for element's bounds.
- **Docs**: the `je_auto_control.android` docstring showed `AC_android_swipe` with `"ms"`, which the adapter rejects; it is `"duration_ms"`.
- **Tests**: `test_recording_pacing_audit.py` (new, 10; the writer, screenshot-record and iOS cases fail on the previous commit, the rest cover the new helper); `test_r3_vision_capture.py`'s fake writer reports `isOpened()` and its capture hook moved to `_grab`, and it expects at least two frames, since pacing makes the count follow elapsed time.
- **Files**: `cv2_utils/frame_clock.py` (new), `cv2_utils/screen_record.py`, `cv2_utils/video_recording.py`, `ios/find.py`, `android/__init__.py`, the two test files, `architecture_explore.md` (`cv2_utils` rows, line counts), `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-33 · 2026-09-24 · CLI: -d runs files in sorted order inside the directory only, the legacy entry point explains failures, dry-run vars, validate, failed suites, start-server port; pytest plugin robustness · #bugfix #audit #cli

- **`-d` order and scope**: `get_dir_files_as_list` returned `os.walk` order, which is the file system's own (NTFS gave `a, B, _x`, ext4 hash order), and followed directory junctions and symlinks, so a link inside the directory put files from elsewhere on the run list. Directories and files are walked in sorted order, and anything whose real path is outside the directory is skipped.
- **Legacy errors**: `python -m je_auto_control` wrote its failures only to the log file, so a failed `-e` exited 1 with empty stdout and stderr. The message now also goes to stderr as `error: ...`. Exit codes are unchanged; whether a run with failed actions should exit 1 there (as `je_auto_control run` does) is a cross-project question now in `Progress.md`.
- **`run --dry-run --var`**: the variables were interpolated eagerly over the whole tree, so a loop body's `${item}` failed as unknown (and an unlocked vault's `${secrets.X}` would have been printed in the dry-run keys). They are seeded into the executor's scope instead, as `execute_action_with_vars` does.
- **`validate`**: it read the file without unwrapping, so `{"auto_control": [...]}` failed validation although `run` executes it, and `[]` passed although `run` refuses it; it now uses the executor's own unwrap-and-check.
- **Failed suites**: `AC_run_suite` runs its cases strictly, so no failure was recorded and `je_auto_control run` exited 0 for a suite whose result said `success: false`. A failed suite counts as a recorded failure (the payload and reports are still returned).
- **`start-server --port 0`**: printed `listening on 127.0.0.1:0`; it prints the bound address.
- **pytest plugin**: a screenshot that raised `AutoControlScreenException` (no display) or `ImportError` (no cv2) turned a failing `autocontrol` test into an `INTERNALERROR` that ended the whole session; both are reported as a failed capture. Skipped and xfailed tests (`report.passed` is false for them) were screenshotted as failures; only failures are now.
- **Tests**: `test_cli_audit_fixes.py` (new, 8; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `utils/file_process/get_dir_file_list.py`, `__main__.py`, `cli.py`, both `cli_doc.rst`, `utils/pytest_plugin/plugin.py`, `utils/executor/action_executor.py` (`_run_suite`), `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the legacy exit code (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-34 · 2026-09-24 · Computer use: sent under its beta, bounded scrolls and waits, scroll at the model's coordinate · #bugfix #audit #agent

- **Beta**: `ComputerUseAgentBackend` posted the `computer_20250124` tool through the plain `client.messages.create` with no beta, but every computer-use tool version is beta-only, so the real API rejected each request and `AC_computer_use` / `ac_computer_use` / `run_computer_use` could never run. The backend calls `client.beta.messages.create` with the beta matching the tool version (`_TOOL_BETAS`), refuses a version it has no beta for unless `beta=` names one, and now defaults to `computer_20251124` (`computer-use-2025-11-24`) on `claude-opus-5`, which accepts it at the same per-token price as the old default.
- **Scroll**: the model's `scroll_amount` was unbounded, and `int(amount) * 120` overflowed the Win32 call with a `ctypes.ArgumentError` outside every containment tuple, ending the agent run. It is capped at 100 notches. `_scroll_decision` also dropped the action's `coordinate`, so the scroll landed wherever the cursor already was; the point is now passed on and clamped to the display with the other coordinates.
- **Waits**: `wait` and `hold_key` durations went through unbounded, so one action could block far past the run's `wall_seconds`, which is checked only between steps; both are capped at 100 s (the spec's limit for `wait`).
- **Docs**: both `v2_features_doc.rst` name the default tool version and its beta, and how to choose another.
- **Not changed (Progress.md)**: the GA `computer_toolset_20260801` (no beta, one `tool_use` per member, `toolset_name` echoed) needs a different agent loop; and `AC_run_agent backend="openai"` sends ~740 tools, past OpenAI's 128.
- **Tests**: `test_computer_use_bounds.py` (new, 5; all fail on the previous commit); `test_anthropic_computer_use.py` and `test_r3_agent_computer_use.py`'s fake clients expose `beta.messages`, and the default-tool test checks the version and the beta sent.
- **Files**: `agent/backends/anthropic_computer_use.py`, the three test files, both `v2_features_doc.rst`, `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
- **Open items**: the two above (`Progress.md`).

## U-20260924-35 · 2026-09-24 · Agent requests time out and resend only recent screenshots; empty tool filters, empty plans and stray VLM coordinates refused · #bugfix #audit #agent #llm #vision

- **Timeouts**: the three agent backends (`anthropic.py`, `anthropic_computer_use.py`, `openai.py`) called `create()` with the SDK default of 600 s per attempt and two retries, so a stalled connection held one step for up to half an hour while `AgentBudget.wall_seconds` is checked only between steps. Each request now passes `timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT_S` (120 s, `agent/backends/base.py`).
- **Screenshots**: every step appends a full-screen PNG and the whole conversation is resent, so the default 25-step run outgrew the Messages API's 32 MB request limit on ordinary desktop captures and paid for every earlier frame each step. `prune_old_screenshots` keeps the newest `SCREENSHOTS_KEPT` (3) image blocks, including ones nested in `tool_result`, and replaces older ones with a text note; the surrounding blocks stay, so every `tool_use` keeps its answer.
- **Tool filter**: `export_anthropic_tools(only=[])` / `export_openai_tools(only=[])` exported every command, so a filter that matched nothing handed the agent all of them, `AC_shell_command` included. Only `None` means all now.
- **Planner**: an empty `[]` plan passed `plan_actions`, and `run_from_description` then failed in the executor with an unrelated "JSON action is null"; it raises `LLMPlanError("LLM returned an empty plan")`.
- **VLM**: `_COORDS_RE` found pairs inside longer numbers (`"123456, 7"` read as `(23456, 7)`, `"1.5, 2"` as `(5, 2)`); it is anchored now. With no `screen_region` a reply off the captured image went straight to the mouse; `locate_by_description` checks it against the PNG's own size.
- **Not changed**: `agent_loop._dispatch_tool` builds `dict(args)` outside its try, but both shipped backends already reject non-object arguments.
- **Tests**: `test_llm_agent_audit.py` (new, 12; 9 fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: the three agent backends and `base.py`, `llm/planner.py`, `tool_use_schema/schema.py`, `vision/vlm_api.py`, `vision/backends/_parse.py`, both `v2_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-36 · 2026-09-24 · Queues and durable state: numbered claims, failed resumable steps retried, releasable idempotency keys, thread-safe dedup and outbox · #bugfix #audit #queue

- **Work queue claims**: a performer whose item was reclaimed as stale could still `fail` it (requeueing it for a third performer) or `complete` it over the new performer's result; the status check alone could not tell them apart. Each claim is now numbered (`claim` column, added to existing databases by `ALTER TABLE`), `WorkItem.claim` / `AC_queue_next` / `ac_queue_next` return it, and `complete` / `fail` (and `AC_queue_complete` / `AC_queue_fail`, their MCP tools and Script Builder fields) refuse a stale `claim`. Without `claim` they behave as before.
- **Poison items**: a stale reclaim never counted as a retry, so an item that crashed its performer every time was handed out for ever. A reclaim increments `retries`, and `get_next(max_retries=3)` marks an item abandoned that many times `failed` (`error` starts with `abandoned`).
- **Checkpoint**: `run_resumable` ran each step without `raise_on_error`, so a failing step was recorded, checkpointed as done, and the run reported `completed: True` and cleared the checkpoint. The step now raises and the checkpoint stays on it.
- **Idempotency**: a key whose work failed stayed `in_progress` for the life of the store (for ever with the executor's TTL-less stores). `IdempotencyStore.release()` drops it; `get` / `to_dict` take the lock (a concurrent `begin` made `to_dict` raise mid-iteration) and `save` writes atomically.
- **Dedup window**: shared per name across executor threads with no lock, so two callers could both be first-seen and a purge racing an insert raised; every method now locks.
- **Outbox**: enqueue / drain / to_dict lock, drains are serialised (two concurrent drains sent the same entry), and `save` writes atomically.
- **S3 artifact store**: `list()` sent the bare prefix (`run` also listed `run2/...`) and read one page (1000 keys); it matches the prefix as a directory and follows `NextContinuationToken`.
- **Bulkhead**: `next_delay` returned a negative `RateLimit-Reset` as is, which `time.sleep` rejects; it is clamped to 0.
- **Popup watchdog**: an `ImportError` from a rule's lazy platform import ended the watchdog thread and every rule with it; it is contained like the other rule errors, and `hits` keeps the newest 1000.
- **Not changed (Progress.md)**: `AC_idempotency_release` for scripts and MCP.
- **Tests**: `test_queue_state_audit.py` (new, 10; 8 fail on the previous commit, the dedup and outbox thread tests are races that the old code only sometimes lost).
- **Files**: `work_queue.py`, `checkpoint.py`, `idempotency.py`, `dedup_window.py`, `outbox.py`, `s3_store.py`, `bulkhead.py`, `popup_watchdog.py`, `action_executor.py`, MCP `_handlers.py` / `_factories.py`, `command_schema.py`, the v10 / v21 / v103 feature docs (both languages), `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-37 · 2026-09-24 · Process, secret and file boundaries: links recycled not targets, base-relative secret refs, batch-file arguments, fuller log redaction · #bugfix #audit #security

- **Trash**: `move_to_trash` passed `target.resolve()` to the backend, which follows symlinks and junctions, so recycling a link recycled the file it pointed to and left the link. It passes `os.path.abspath`, and a dangling link is no longer `FileNotFoundError`.
- **Secret refs**: a relative `file://` ref was resolved against the working directory and then compared with `base_dir`, so the documented `file://./token` only worked from the right directory. It is joined onto `base_dir` now; `commonpath`'s `ValueError` on another drive becomes `SecretRefError`; and `file:///C:/x` names `C:/x` rather than the drive-relative `C:x`.
- **TOTP**: `verify_code` accepted any Unicode digits, which `hmac.compare_digest` then rejected with `TypeError`; only ASCII digits are a code.
- **DROPFILES**: the POINT was packed unsigned, so a drop left of or above the primary monitor raised `struct.error` (and parsed back as 4294967291); it is signed. Single-byte paths use the ANSI code page (`mbcs`) instead of Latin-1.
- **Log redaction**: `redact_secret_text` masked only the first word of a quoted value (`password="correct horse battery"`), did not know `pwd=`, and left `--password hunter2` visible; all three are masked, and `--token-file path` is left alone.
- **dotenv**: a tab before `#` did not start a comment and `export<TAB>KEY=1` was dropped.
- **ACME**: an authorization Let's Encrypt reused (already `valid`, listing only the challenge that validated it) made `http_challenge()` raise; `request_certificate` skips it.
- **Batch files**: `exec_shell(["run.bat", "x&calc"])` also started calc, because cmd.exe re-parses a batch file's command line and `subprocess` quotes only for CreateProcess. `refuse_batch_metacharacters` rejects cmd syntax in the arguments of a `.bat` / `.cmd` argv list on Windows; a string command is the caller's own command line and is unchanged.
- **start_exe**: a bare name passed the existence check against the working directory and then launched whatever `PATH` found; it launches the resolved path it checked.
- **Tests**: `test_process_secret_audit.py` (new, 14 cases; 12 fail on the previous commit, the symlink case skips on Windows without the symlink privilege); `test_acme_v2.py` gains the reused-authorization case (fails on the previous commit), and its stub now validates a pending authorization when the challenge is answered instead of starting out valid.
- **Files**: `trash.py`, `secret_ref.py`, `remote_desktop/totp.py`, `clipboard_files.py`, `config_redaction.py`, `dotenv.py`, `acme_v2/client.py`, `shell_exec.py`, `start_another_process.py`, both `v85_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-38 · 2026-09-24 · HTTP family: egress matches the connected host, bounded bodies and decompression, safe multipart, RFC-correct Link and URL handling · #bugfix #audit #security #http

- **Egress**: the deny/allow lists matched `urlparse().hostname` literally, but urllib percent-decodes the host before connecting, so `http://%65vil.com/` reached a denied `evil.com`; `evil.com.`, `2130706433`, `0x7f.1` and `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]` walked past a deny list the same way. `_host_of` decodes, drops the trailing dot and reduces IP literals in any spelling (via `inet_aton` for the legacy forms) to their usual form; patterns are canonicalised alike. Names are still not resolved (documented).
- **http_client**: the body was read whole (an endless response exhausted memory); it stops at `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` (64 MiB) with `URLError`. `dict(headers.items())` kept only the last of a repeated header, so a second `Set-Cookie` or `Link` vanished; repeats are joined with ", " and every `Set-Cookie` value is in the new `set_cookie` list.
