A redirect from https to http on the same host kept `Authorization` / `Cookie`; credentials are dropped whenever the scheme or authority changes. `data=5` sent five NUL bytes; an int body is a `TypeError`.
- **http_content**: `gzip` / `deflate` decoding had no output limit (a few KB could expand to GBs) and let `BadGzipFile` / `zlib.error` escape; it inflates at most `max_bytes` (default 64 MiB), handles multi-member gzip, and raises `ValueError`. `q=1e-05` is emitted as `q=0`, and an uppercase `Q=` is read.
- **multipart**: a field name or filename with `"` or CR/LF injected headers and whole extra parts; they are escaped the way browsers do (`%22`, `%0D`, `%0A`) and unescaped when parsed, and a line break in a file's content type is refused. Parsing stripped every CR/LF around a part (`"line\r\n"` came back as `"line"`), split on a boundary string anywhere, broke `filename="a;b.txt"`, dropped bare `name=a` and ignored `Boundary=`; it now splits on CRLF-prefixed delimiters and parses quoted and bare parameters.
- **Link**: a `<` inside a quoted parameter ended the link's parameters, the last `rel` won (RFC 8288 takes the first), and `paginate` never resolved a relative `next`. Links are split on commas outside quotes and `<...>`, the first occurrence of a parameter wins, and `next` is joined onto the current URL.
- **url_canon**: IPv6 hosts lost their brackets (`http://::1:8080/x`), `http://:pw@h` lost its password, `posixpath.normpath` dropped the trailing slash of `/a/b/..` and merged `//`, `mailto:a@b` became `mailto:/a@b`, and the query was decoded and re-encoded (`%FF` became U+FFFD, `flag` became `flag=`). The raw userinfo and bracketed host are kept, dot segments follow RFC 3986 5.2.4, a path without an authority gains no slash, and query pairs keep their spelling.
- **Smaller**: an invalid `Max-Age` no longer overrides a past `Expires` (the deleted cookie stayed); the SSE parser ignores a `retry` in non-ASCII digits instead of raising; a bare `max-age` (parsed as `True`) is not freshness; a problem document with `"type": null` is `about:blank`, not `"None"`.
- **Tests**: `test_http_family_audit.py` (new, 30; 29 fail on the previous commit); `test_http_client.py`'s fake response takes `read(size)` like a file object.
- **Files**: `egress_policy.py`, `http_client.py`, `http_content.py`, `multipart.py`, `link_header.py`, `url_canon.py`, `cookie_jar.py`, `sse_client.py`, `http_conditional.py`, `http_problem.py`, the v5 and v34 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-39 · 2026-09-24 · Code Quality back to green: recorder pacing typed for OpenCV's stubs, redaction key pattern renamed past bandit · #ci #typing

- **typing-stable-api** had failed since d9c79cb9: `record_paced` typed its writer as `Callable[[Any], None]`, and in the gate's bare environment (`pip install -e .` plus mypy 2.3.0, where OpenCV ships its own stubs) the overloaded `cv2.VideoWriter.write` does not match that, so `screen_record` and `video_recording` joined the failing list. The writer is `Callable[[Any], object]`; a developer venv with other packages had passed, which is why the local check missed it. Reproduced and verified in a fresh venv with only the package and mypy 2.3.0.
- **security** (bandit B105) failed on 58b20b70: the regex of credential key names was held in `_SECRET_KEYS`, a name bandit reads as a hardcoded password. It is `_CREDENTIAL_KEY_NAMES`; no suppression needed.
- **Files**: `cv2_utils/frame_clock.py`, `config_redaction/config_redaction.py`, no behaviour change, so no `CHANGELOG.md` entry.

## U-20260924-40 · 2026-09-24 · Governance and supply chain: parsed license expressions, version-exact VEX, case-folded approvers, newline-proof traceparent · #bugfix #audit #security #supply-chain

- **License policy**: any " or " in an expression switched the whole check to "any operand allowed", so `(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND Proprietary` passed an MIT allowlist; a denied operand denied `MIT OR GPL-3.0-only` although MIT is a valid choice; ids matched case-sensitively (`gpl-3.0-only` slipped past a deny list), `GPL-2.0+` never meant `GPL-2.0-or-later`, and a `WITH` exception was read as a license. `evaluate_license` now parses the expression (OR loosest, then AND, parentheses, WITH judged by its license), compares ids case-insensitively and maps `+` and the deprecated bare GNU ids; a malformed expression is `unknown`. The docs said an empty `allow` meant "not constrained" while the code (like the package's other allowlists) accepts nothing; the docs now say so.
- **VEX**: `_product_name` dropped the purl's version, so `not_affected` for requests 2.0.0 also silenced requests 2.31.0, and names were only lowercased, so `pkg:pypi/pyside6-essentials` (what `sbom` emits) never matched `PySide6_Essentials`. A product with a version matches only that version (by `version_key`), and names compare in PEP 503 form.
- **Approvals**: the maker-checker comparison was case-sensitive, so "alice" approved "Alice"'s request; ids compare after NFKC normalisation and case folding.
- **Trace context**: the id / flags regexes ended in `$`, which matches before a trailing newline, so an id ending in "\n" was accepted and written back into outgoing headers; they use `fullmatch`. `extract_context` returns `None` for an invalid `traceparent` (as its docstring and W3C say) instead of raising.
- **Guardrail**: overlapping matches were replaced by their original offsets one by one, garbling the text and leaving part of the sensitive span; spans are merged first.
- **Vulnerability matching**: PEP 440 epochs were ignored (`1!1.0` counted as affected below 2.0), and `1.0a1.dev1` sorted after `1.0a1`; the epoch now leads the release tuple and a dev of a pre-release sorts before it, with the SemVer precedence example and the PEP 440 ordering both verified. A finding's `fixed` was the first fix of any entry in the advisory, even another package's or an earlier range's; it is the next fix above the version, for this package.
- **Feature flags**: `semver_*` compared the first three digit groups (1.2 < 1.2.0, 1.0.0-rc.1 == 1.0.0); it uses `version_key`. `"enabled": "false"` (a string) left the flag on.
- **JSON logs**: `JSONLogFormatter` dropped `exc_info` / `stack_info`, so `logger.exception()` lost its traceback.
- **Baggage**: only the 180-member cap was enforced; members that would push the header past 8192 bytes are dropped.
- **Not changed**: `ConfigField.env` is documented as a hint and validation as a pure function, so it still does not read the environment.
- **Tests**: `test_governance_supply_audit.py` (new, 18; 16 fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `license_policy.py`, `vex.py`, `governance.py`, `trace_context.py`, `guardrail.py`, `vuln_scan.py`, `feature_flags.py`, `canonical_log.py`, `baggage.py`, the v32 / v60 / v68 / v76 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-41 · 2026-09-24 · Time and statistics: bounded sequence tracking, SLO window ends at now, local UNTIL, sane digests and outlier scores · #bugfix #audit

- **Sequence gaps**: `observe('s', 0)` then `observe('s', 5_000_000)` stored five million numbers (3.9 s, 318 MiB) and every seen number was kept for ever. Every number from the first to the high-water mark is either seen or a gap, so only the gaps (plus late numbers below the first) are stored, and `SequenceTracker(max_gap=100_000)` raises `ValueError` for a jump that would leave more missing numbers than that. Classification is unchanged.
- **SLO**: `_window` kept every record at or after `now - window_s`, including ones after `now`, so replaying a log with an injected `now` counted the future (burn rate 90.9 instead of 0); the window ends at `now`.
- **Recurrence**: for a naive DTSTART a UTC `UNTIL` had its offset dropped rather than converted, cutting the last day at +0800; it is converted to local time. A huge `INTERVAL` overflowed `timedelta` / date arithmetic with `OverflowError`; it is limited to 9999 as a rule error (the validation moved into `_parse_limits`, keeping `parse_rrule` under the complexity limit).
- **Latency digest**: NaN / inf crashed `record` with a bare `ValueError` / `OverflowError` from `log10`; they are refused with a clear `ValueError`. Percentiles are clamped to the recorded min/max: rounding put p99 of a lone 1234.5 at 1230.0, and values <= 0 all reported 0.
- **Anomalies**: when more than half the values are identical MAD is 0 and every score was 0, hiding `[10]*6 + [1000]`'s outlier; the scores fall back to the mean absolute deviation (x1.253314).
- **stats.percentile**: NaN broke the sort, so the result depended on its position; NaN is refused.
- **Time travel**: snapshot windows were closed at both ends, so an action exactly on the next frame's timestamp appeared in two snapshots; between frames the window is half-open (`actions_in_window(..., include_end=False)`, the default unchanged).
- **Settle detector**: `quiet_samples=0` made every sample settled, a spike included; it must be at least 1.
- **Not changed**: `stats.describe` still reports population variance (a semantic choice, left as is), `next_occurrence` still stops searching 400 years out, and run durations still use the wall clock.
- **Tests**: `test_time_stats_audit.py` (new, 9; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `sequence_gap.py`, `slo.py`, `recurrence.py`, `percentiles.py`, `anomaly.py`, `stats.py`, `time_travel/player.py`, `settle_detector.py`, the v101 / v105 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-42 · 2026-09-24 · Data formats: workbook cells stay data, strict JSON Patch / JSONPath, ICU offsets and quoting, subtree ignores, safer masking and parsing · #bugfix #audit #security

- **Excel export**: openpyxl stores any string starting with `=` as a formula, so a scraped value such as `=HYPERLINK(...)` or `=cmd|...` in `write_workbook` / `AC_write_workbook` ran when the file was opened. Every appended cell typed as a formula is set back to a string (verified against real openpyxl: the cell is saved as inline text). Keys missing from the first row were dropped from every row; the columns are now every key in order of first appearance.
- **JSON Patch**: a missing `path` / `value` / `from` raised `KeyError`, a non-object op `AttributeError`, and a non-string path went further; `_checked_op` raises `PatchError`. RFC 6901 escapes other than `~0` / `~1` (`~2`, a trailing `~`) are refused.
- **JSONPath**: slices, unions and `[]` were looked up as keys and a filter value such as `1 && @.b==2` (or Python-only `1_000`, `nan`) was compared as text, all silently matching nothing, although the docstring promised `ValueError`; they raise now, and filter values are JSON literals. A filter on an object tested the object itself; it selects among the member values (RFC 9535), which also stops `$..[?(...)]` from matching a dict twice.
- **ICU MessageFormat**: the plural keyword ignored `offset:` ("you and 1 others"), `''` inside a quoted section ended the quote, an empty chosen branch fell through to `other`, a stray `}` cut the rest of the message off silently, and a `None` plural argument raised `TypeError`; each is fixed or a `ValueError`.
- **XML**: `<b/>` read back as `None`, which `dict_to_elements_tree` rejected with `TypeError`, so empty elements could not round-trip; `None` writes an empty element and numbers write their text.
- **Schema compatibility**: an enum of arrays crashed (unhashable), `true` and `1` counted as the same enum value, and `integer` -> `number` was a breaking `type_changed` instead of a widening; enums compare as canonical JSON and integer counts as a number.
- **JSON contracts**: `ignore=["$.ts"]` did not cover a difference at `$.ts.s`; it ignores the subtree.
- **Data quality**: `partial` masking of a value up to 4 characters showed all of it; values shorter than 8 are fully masked. `unique` on lists / dicts crashed; they are keyed by their JSON.
- **Locale parsing**: Babel's lenient mode read "1,5" in en_US as 15 and `parse_number("1.5")` truncated to 1; parsing is strict and a fraction is refused by `parse_number`.
- **Not changed**: deep nesting still hits Python's recursion limit in `apply_patch` / `json_query` / XML conversion; `locale_collation` accent positions at tertiary strength.
- **Tests**: `test_data_format_audit.py` (new, 25; 24 fail on the previous commit, the real-openpyxl case skips without the `office` extra).
- **Files**: `office.py`, `json_patch.py`, `jsonpath.py`, `message_format.py`, `change_xml_structure.py`, `schema_compat.py`, `json_contract.py`, `data_quality.py`, `locale_parse.py`, the v14 / v19 / v43 / v51 / v70 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-43 · 2026-09-24 · Locators and geometry: no fake sideways scroll, unique stable ids, points inside their cells, A/B stats that survive other writers · #bugfix #audit

- **Scroll-to-find**: the default scroller sent `right` / `left` as `down` / `up`, and `mouse_scroll` has only a vertical wheel on Windows and macOS, so a sideways search scrolled vertically and could never find anything off to the side. Sideways directions use the X11 / Wayland horizontal axis where the backend has one and otherwise raise `ValueError` (pass `scroller=`). Vertical scrolls now pass `scroll_direction="scroll_up"` so a negative count means down on every backend.
- **Element diff**: `assign_stable_ids` let two current elements inherit the same prior id; each prior id is now used once. (`iou_threshold=0` pairing boxes that do not touch is deliberate: `observation_delta` uses it to follow a moved element.)
- **Monitors**: `remap_point` rounded (999, 999) on 1000x1000 to (100, 100) on a 100x100 monitor, one pixel off it; results are clamped to the target.
- **Screen grid**: `cell_for_point` truncated a ratio while cells are built from rounded edges, so x=3 of a 10 px three-column grid returned A1 (right edge 3); it bisects the same edges.
- **Window grid**: each cell was `round(cell_w)` wide, leaving pixel 666 of 1000 / 3 uncovered; widths come from consecutive rounded edges.
- **A/B locator stats**: the file was read once and the whole cache written back on every record, so two stores (or processes) on one file erased each other's counts, and a damaged file was read as empty and overwritten. `record` re-reads under the file lock; unparseable JSON is quarantined, while a read error (a file locked by an antivirus scan) still propagates without touching the file.
- **A/B runner**: `TypeError` / `AttributeError` from a malformed strategy (image locator with no template) escaped `pool.map` and ended the run; it is recorded as that strategy's failure.
- **Not changed**: `monitor_layout` still reports mss's physical-pixel rectangles with scale 1.0 and takes the first monitor as primary; fixing that touches the logical/physical mapping the live Jeffrey_RPA batch relies on, so it waits. Borderless-table detection still clamps boxes to non-negative x.
- **Tests**: `test_locator_geometry_audit.py` (new, 9; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `scroll_find.py`, `element_diff.py`, `monitor_layout.py`, `screen_grid.py`, `window_layout.py`, `ab_locator/store.py`, `ab_locator/runner.py`, both `v4_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
## U-20260924-44 · 2026-09-24 · Text processing: whole phone and Amex masking, linear PII and secret scans, invisible-character-proof confusables, TR39 script mixing, fuzzy .po entries skipped · #bugfix #audit #security

- **PII text**: the phone pattern matched at most 15 characters, so `+1 (555) 123-4567` became `[phone]67` and `+44 20 7946 0958` `[phone]8`; it runs to 20 and is anchored at word edges. Card numbers required 4-4-4-(1..4) groups, so a 15-digit Amex was half-matched as a phone (leaking digits); cards are 13-19 digits in any grouping and must pass the new `luhn_valid`. The email pattern's unbounded local part rescanned to the end from every position (0.85 s on 20 KB of letters, quadratic); it is bounded and boundary-anchored.
- **Screenshot redaction**: the card rule matched only 16 digits, so Amex numbers stayed readable under every policy, and `+44 20 7946 0958` matched no phone rule; both are caught now.
- **Secrets scan**: `url-credentials` let a scheme start after any `.`, so `"a." * 8000` took 1.4 s and grew quadratically; the scheme cannot start inside a word and its parts are bounded.
- **Confusables**: NFKC keeps zero-width spaces, soft hyphens and joiners, so `pa\u200bypal` was not confusable with `paypal`; `skeleton` drops format characters and variation selectors first. `normalize_text` does the same (`strip_format=True`). `detect_homoglyphs` reported indices in the NFKC string, which shift after a ligature; it indexes the input. Fullwidth Latin counted as no script, hiding a Cyrillic letter in a fullwidth word, while ordinary Japanese and Korean text was flagged as mixed; `is_mixed_script` follows UTS #39's highly restrictive sets.
- **Bidi**: `base_direction` took a strong character inside an isolate, contrary to UAX #9 rule P2; isolates are skipped.
- **gettext**: `#, fuzzy` entries were served as translations (msgfmt drops them; the header is kept); octal / hex escapes (`\101`, `\x41`) and `\a \b \f \v` were not decoded; a truncated `.mo` raised `struct.error`, now `ValueError`.
- **i18n catalogue check**: every `{word` counted as an argument, so ICU case bodies (`male {He}` vs `male {Il}`) were reported as placeholder mismatches; case bodies are skipped while real arguments inside them still count.
- **Tests**: `test_text_processing_audit.py` (new, 15; 14 fail on the previous commit with `luhn_valid` stubbed, the remaining one guards email / URL-credential detection).
- **Files**: `pii_text.py`, `redaction/rules.py`, `secrets_scan.py`, `confusables.py`, `text_normalize.py`, `bidi_check.py`, `gettext_catalog.py`, `i18n_test.py`, the v55 / v109 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-45 · 2026-09-24 · Workflow and test tooling: merged shard reports keep errors, waits honour deadlines, strict hit policies and predicates, assert_poll is an assertion · #bugfix #audit #testing

- **Shard merge**: `merge_results` summed `errors` and joined `results`, but `TestSuiteResult.to_dict()` writes `errored` and `cases`, so a sharded run with errors merged as clean with no cases. Both spellings are read and written. `shard_flows(window=0)` divided by zero; it is a `ValueError`.
- **Test data**: a date field with no `end` ranged up to `date.today()`, so the same seed gave a different date the next day; the default end is 2030-12-31.
- **Smart waits**: every wait slept its full `poll_interval_s`, so a 0.1 s timeout polled at 1.5 s returned after 1.5 s; `_pause` caps each sleep at the deadline (all ten waits).
- **Decision tables**: any hit-policy name was accepted, and a typo such as `COLECT` meant "every match"; unknown policies raise, and `ANY` is supported with DMN's rule that matching rules must agree.
- **State machine**: a non-callable `predicate` (a string from JSON) made its transition always fire; it raises `StateMachineError`, which now derives from `AutoControlException` (and still `RuntimeError`).
- **assert_poll**: raised `AutoControlActionException`, which the suite runner scores as *errored* and `raise_on_error=False` swallows; it raises `PollAssertionError`, an `AutoControlAssertionException` that is still an `AutoControlActionException`.
- **Soft assertions**: when the block raised, the checks that had already failed were lost; they are added to the exception as a note (Python 3.11+).
- **Flow debugger**: stepping the same action twice produced the same record key and overwrote the first result; repeated keys are numbered.
- **Process mining**: repeats were counted with overlap (`A A A A` held three `A A`); counts are non-overlapping.
- **Loop guard**: a threshold above `window` could never be reached, so `LoopGuard(critical=25)` never tripped; the constructor requires `1 <= warn <= critical <= window`.
- **DeterministicRun**: seeded numpy but restored only `random`; numpy's state is restored too.
- **Not changed**: `AC_expect_poll` still treats a failing polled assertion as "not yet satisfied" and returns `ok: False` on timeout; that is the command's documented polling contract (use `assert_poll` for an assertion).
- **Tests**: `test_workflow_tooling_audit.py` (new, 12; 11 fail on the previous commit; the date case passes there only because today is inside the range).
- **Files**: `test_shard.py`, `test_data.py`, `smart_waits/waits.py`, `decision_table.py`, `state_machine/engine.py`, `expect_poll.py`, `soft_assert.py`, `flow_debugger.py`, `process_mining.py`, `loop_guard.py`, `deterministic.py`, the v18 / v53 / v142 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-47 · 2026-09-24 · Stores set a file aside only when its content is damaged, not when a read fails · #bugfix #audit #remote-desktop

- **Defect**: `json_store.load_json_or_quarantine` moved a store file aside on any `OSError`, so a transient read failure (an antivirus scan holding the file, a permission hiccup) was treated as damage: the healthy file was renamed `*.corrupt-<time>` and the store started empty. For `known_hosts` that reset every pinned host fingerprint (the next connection re-trusted whatever answered); the trust list and address book lost their entries the same way. `AdminConsoleClient._load` had its own copy of the same catch.
- **Fix**: only content that is not UTF-8 JSON is quarantined; a missing file is `None`; any other `OSError` propagates, so the caller fails instead of silently resetting. `AdminConsoleClient` uses the shared helper (which also accepts a UTF-8 BOM).
- **Found by**: the session running the concurrent locator batch, while fixing `ab_locator/store.py`.
- **Also**: the 2026-09 entry count in `docs/updates/README.md` was one short after two concurrent batches were merged; it is recounted from the batch file.
- **Tests**: `test_quarantine_read_errors.py` (new, 4; the two read-error cases fail on the previous commit, the other two guard damaged-content quarantine and a missing file).
- **Files**: `json_store/json_store.py`, `admin/admin_client.py`, `docs/updates/README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
## U-20260924-46 · 2026-09-24 · Config and plumbing: redacted failure tickets, contained plugin directories, Unicode search terms, sturdier chatops and trace spans · #bugfix #audit #security

- **Failure hooks**: `FailureReport` went to Jira / Linear / GitHub with `error_text`, `log_tail` and `metadata` as they were, so a log tail holding `Authorization: Bearer ghp_...` was filed in a public issue. `FailureHookManager.fire` sends `report.redacted()` (the same `redact_secret_text` / `redact_config` the failure bundle uses). `_invoke` caught only `RuntimeError` / `OSError` / `ValueError`, so a backend's `KeyError` (or the Linear backend's `AttributeError` on `{"data": []}`) ended the fan-out; any exception becomes that backend's failed `TicketResult`, and the Linear parser checks the response shape.
- **Plugin loader**: `load_plugin_directory` imported whatever a `*.py` symlink resolved to, even outside the directory; such files are skipped with an error.
- **Search index**: tokens were `[a-z0-9]+`, dropping every non-ASCII character (`登入` never indexed, `café` became `caf`); they are case-folded letter/digit runs in any script. Stop words were not folded, so `stop_words=["The"]` never removed `the`.
- **Agent trace**: `operation()` passed `**kwargs, **fields` to `record`, which raised `TypeError` when a field repeated an argument (`model`) or was not one (`cost_usd`) -- after the block ran, and on the error path in place of the caller's exception. Fields now override arguments and unknown keys become attributes.
- **Chatops**: commands are registered lowercased but looked up as typed, so `/Help` was unknown; lookup folds case. The Slack poller read one page of 50 and then moved `last_seen_ts` past it, losing older messages; it follows `next_cursor` (up to 20 pages). A handler's `ImportError` / `ArithmeticError` escaped both the router and the poller boundary and ended `run_forever`.
- **Metrics**: `Counter.inc(nan)` passed the `< 0` check and left the counter NaN; it is refused.
- **Diagnostics**: the CLI exited 1 for a failed *warning* (no mouse on a headless box) although it documents "1 on errors"; `DiagnosticsReport.has_errors` decides the exit code.
- **Not changed**: `layered_config.deep_merge` lets an explicit `None` override (documented as "override wins leaves"); `ResourceProfiler` samples and the default `AgentTrace` still grow for the life of the process.
- **Tests**: `test_plumbing_audit.py` (new, 9; 8 fail on the previous commit, the symlink case skips without the symlink privilege).
- **Files**: `failure_hooks/report.py` / `manager.py` / `backends.py`, `plugin_loader.py`, `search_index.py`, `agent_trace.py`, `chatops/router.py` / `slack_bot.py`, `observability/metrics.py`, `diagnostics/diagnostics.py` / `__main__.py`, the v2 / v64 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-48 · 2026-09-24 · Agent and action helpers: assertions are never healed, releases follow healed presses, live actionability gates, innermost grounding · #bugfix #audit #agent

- **Self-healing replay**: `_run_step` caught `AutoControlException`, which includes `AutoControlAssertionException`, so a failed assertion was "healed" -- the VLM picked a new point, the step re-ran there and the replay could report success. Assertions now propagate. Healing rewrote only the failing step, so a healed `mouse_press` was followed by the recorded `mouse_release` at the old point (a drag, not a click); the release of a healed press follows it.
- **Relocation**: `relocate_action` called `dict()` on list-form `["AC_x", {...}]` entries and aborted the whole `relocate_recording`; they pass through, as in `enrich_action`.
- **Actionability**: a `(x, y, 0, 0)` box counted as visible; zero-size boxes are not. `enabled_probe` is `Optional[bool]`, but `None` (unknown) was read as disabled and timed the gate out.
- **act_in_view**: the gate got the constant box `(cx, cy, 1, 1)`, so its visibility and stability checks could never fail and the action fired at a stale point; the target is located again on every poll.
- **Grounding**: `snap_to_element` snapped to the first containing element (often the window around a button) instead of the innermost one, and `in_bounds` truncated `-0.9` to 0 and accepted it.
- **Agent memory**: `remember(tags=[1, None])` stored non-strings and every later `recall` raised `TypeError`; tags are stored as text and read defensively.
- **Signing key file**: a process that lost the `O_EXCL` race read the key before the winner wrote it, got 0 bytes, and was told to delete the file (invalidating the winner's key); it waits up to 2 s for the key.
- **Run history**: `prune` deleted runs still `running`, so their `finish_run` found no row and the result was lost; a running row started in the last 24 h is kept (older ones are orphans of a crashed process and are still pruned).
- **Not changed**: a signature is not bound to the file's name, so a signed script can be copied over another with its `.sig` (a signature-format change; left for the signing-authority decision in `Progress.md`).
- **Tests**: `test_agent_action_audit.py` (new, 10; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `semantic_recording/self_healing.py`, `semantic_recording/replay.py`, `actionability.py`, `act_in_view.py`, `action_grounding.py`, `agent_memory.py`, `action_signing/_key_file.py`, `run_history/history_store.py`, the v137 / v153 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-49 · 2026-09-24 · System and device helpers: keep-awake that ends with the process, real content types, strict checksums and compliance, D-Bus and window-capture fixes · #bugfix #audit

- **File association**: `content_type` queried `ASSOCSTR` 12 (`QUICKTIP`), so `file_association("x.pdf")` returned a property list instead of `application/pdf`; it is 14 (`CONTENTTYPE`, verified on this machine).
- **Keep-awake**: `caffeinate -i` and `systemd-inhibit ... sleep infinity` outlived the Python process when a script ended without `allow_sleep()`, so the machine never slept; `caffeinate -w <pid>` and `tail --pid=<pid>` end with the process, and `allow_sleep` runs at exit. On Windows `SetThreadExecutionState` is per thread, so `AC_keep_awake_on` / `AC_allow_sleep` on different executor threads did not pair up and the request died with its thread; each request is held by a dedicated thread. `test_system_utils_audit.py`'s fake kernel32 now keeps per-thread state, as Windows does.
- **Checksums**: Verhoeff and Damm accepted `""`, `"abc"` and `"--"`; `"4\u00b2"` crashed `luhn_validate` (`isdigit()` accepts superscripts); `mod97_10_check_digits` returned `00` / `01` for some inputs (41 of the first 2000) where ISO 7064 / IBAN require 02-98.
- **Compliance**: an unknown or differently spelt framework (`"SOC 2"`) produced a report with zero controls that looked clean, and the string `"false"` counted as satisfied evidence. Framework names are normalised and unknown ones raise `ValueError`; false-like strings are gaps.
- **D-Bus**: a failed authentication or `Hello` left the socket open (and `with SessionBus()` never reached `__exit__`); it is closed. Every int variant was `u`, so `-1` or `2**32` raised `struct.error`; ints get `u` / `i` / `x` / `t` by range, or a `DBusError`.
- **Window capture**: `GetWindowRect` includes the ~7 px invisible borders on Windows 10 / 11 and a minimized window's (-32000, -32000) rect was captured; the DWM extended frame bounds are used and minimized windows return `None`.
- **System volume**: pycaw 2025+ returns an `AudioDevice` without `Activate()`; its `EndpointVolume` is used when present (verified against pycaw 20260921).
- **Not changed**: IME composition state for windows of other processes (`ImmGetContext` returns NULL there) needs `ImmGetDefaultIMEWnd` + `WM_IME_CONTROL` and a real IME to verify; left for a separate change.
- **Tests**: `test_system_device_audit.py` (new, 16; 15 fail on the previous commit, the pycaw case skips without pycaw and was run in a scratch venv with it).
- **Files**: `file_assoc.py`, `idle_keepawake.py`, `checksum.py`, `compliance_report.py`, `dbus_client/session_bus.py`, `window_capture.py`, `system_volume.py`, `test_system_utils_audit.py`, the v37 / v204 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-50 · 2026-09-24 · Image matching: screen coordinates everywhere, masked rotated templates, flat templates refused on every path, per-blob peaks · #bugfix #audit #vision

- **Screen origin**: `rotated_match`, `match_trust`, `feature_match`, `edge_match`, `shape_locator`, `edge_lines` and `saliency` read the screen through `_haystack_gray`, which drops `grab_logical`'s frame origin, so a `region=` search (or any search on a desktop with a monitor left of / above the primary) answered in frame-local coordinates the mouse cannot use. They use `_haystack_gray_with_origin` and the new `visual_match._to_screen`, which moves a match dataclass or a box / segment dict by the origin.
- **Rotated templates**: the enlarged canvas of a rotated template is padded with black and the corners took part in the correlation -- on a white background a 30 degree match scored 0.32 at the wrong place instead of 1.0 (verified). The rotation's mask is passed to `matchTemplate` (non-finite masked scores become -1), and `sqdiff_normed` is inverted before the maximum is taken (it picked the worst spot).
- **Flat templates**: only `match_template` / `match_template_all` rejected a uniform template; `match_subpixel`, `match_auto`, `auto_threshold`, `detect_scale`, `match_with_trust` and `match_rotated` returned a score-1.0 match at (0, 0). The check now sits in `_score_map_with_origin` (and the rotated sweep).
- **Auto-threshold**: `_peak_in_box` searched a blob's bounding box, which can contain another blob, so one peak was reported twice and the other lost; `_blob_peaks` searches each labelled blob.
- **Feature match**: with `min_inliers <= 3` and 1-3 good matches `cv2.findHomography` raised a raw `cv2.error`; fewer than 4 matches is `None`. `feature_match`, `edge_match*`, `match_rotated*`, `score_peaks` and `match_with_trust` are wrapped in `_contain_cv2_error`.
- **Image loading**: palette (`P`) images were read as palette indices, `LA` gave a 2-channel array `cvtColor` rejected and 16-bit images failed in `matchTemplate`; other PIL modes are converted to RGB, and 1- / 2-channel arrays use their first channel.
- **Match trust**: `peak_ratio` divided by a negative best peak (best -0.2, second -0.5 gave 2.5, "ambiguous") and a negative second made any match look unique by going negative; a non-positive best is ambiguous (1.0) and the second is floored at 0.
- **Not changed**: `hsv_segment` / `color_region` still grab through `pil_screenshot` (primary monitor, physical pixels); left to the colour-region change in progress elsewhere.
- **Tests**: `test_image_matching_audit.py` (new, 12; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `visual_match.py`, `rotated_match.py`, `match_trust.py`, `feature_match.py`, `edge_match.py`, `shape_locator.py`, `edge_lines.py`, `saliency.py`, `match_autothresh.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-51 · 2026-09-24 · Media and analysis: exact bucket edges, converged KS p-values, normalised histogram intersection, honest video motion and visual-diff percentages · #bugfix #audit

- **Origin**: drafted by the concurrent AutoControlGUI session before it ended; its uncommitted diff was reviewed, completed with tests and docs, and landed here.
- **Time series**: `0.3 // 0.1` is `2.0`, so a point exactly on a float bucket edge landed in the previous bucket and resampled grids drifted (`0.6000000000000001`); bucket indices tolerate float error and bucket starts are rounded.
- **Data drift**: below lambda ~0.2 the alternating Kolmogorov series had not converged in 100 terms and returned 0.02 for lambda 0.001 (true value 1.0), so near-identical large samples were reported as drifted; small lambdas return 1.0.
- **Histograms**: `intersection` returned the raw sum of minima (up to about 3 x bins), so red vs blue scored 2.0 and passed a 0.9 threshold; it is normalised to 0..1.
- **Video motion**: an unreadable file measured 0.0 motion and passed `expect_motion=False`; an unopened capture raises `ValueError`, and so does a segment with fewer than two frames.
- **SSIM ignore boxes**: a box starting at a negative coordinate sliced from the far edge (`keep[:, -5:5]`, empty) and ignored nothing; it is clamped.
- **Visual regression**: masked pixels still counted in the total, so a mask over 90 % of the image turned a 100 % change of the rest into 9 %; they are excluded.
- **Colour regions / perceptual diff**: a 2-D grayscale array went through as-is, so `image[..., 0]` read its first column and two different grayscale screenshots compared equal; 1- and 4-channel arrays are brought to RGB.
- **Video report**: `cv2.imread` could not open a non-ASCII path and a `pathlib.Path` or PIL frame was not loaded; paths go through `read_image` and PIL frames become BGR arrays.
- **PDF**: pypdf's `DependencyError` (an AES-encrypted file without `cryptography`) is not a `PyPdfError` and escaped raw; it becomes `AutoControlActionException`.
- **Tests**: `test_media_analysis_audit.py` (new, 9; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `timeseries.py`, `data_drift.py`, `img_histogram.py`, `media_assert/media.py`, `ssim.py`, `visual_regression/compare.py`, `color_region.py`, `video_report.py`, `pdf_reader.py`, both `v145_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-52 · 2026-09-24 · Input and form helpers: platform key names for computer use, linted flow bodies, working gamepad clicks and dpad, RTF code pages · #bugfix #audit #agent

- **Key names**: the computer-use backend mapped xdotool names to fixed aliases (`Return` -> `enter`, `escape` -> `esc`, `page_down` -> `pagedown`, `BackSpace` -> `backspace`, `alt`, `super` -> `win`), but the Windows key table spells them `return` / `escape` / `next` / `back` / `menu` / `lwin` (measured: none of the aliases exists there), so Enter, Esc, paging, Backspace, Alt and Super from `AC_computer_use` failed on Windows. CUA translation (`to_ac_command`) passed names through unchanged, so OpenAI's `CTRL` / `ENTER` failed everywhere. `cua_action.resolve_key_name` picks the running platform's spelling (case-insensitive), both paths use it, and `split_key_combo` keeps a trailing `+` key.
- **CUA mapping**: a double click became one `AC_click_mouse`; it is `AC_loop` over two. Scrolls did not pass `scroll_direction`, so X11 / Wayland scrolled their default way ("up" went down) and a sideways scroll became vertical; vertical scrolls carry `scroll_up` as the positive direction and sideways ones use the horizontal axis where the backend has one, raising otherwise.
- **Action lint**: flow-control commands (`AC_loop`, `AC_try`, `AC_if_*`, about 30) live outside `event_dict`, so valid files failed with `unknown-command` and nothing inside their bodies was checked; they are known and their bodies / branch lists are linted with a trail (`AC_loop.body[0]: ...`). The shape check now matches the executor: a positional `[name, [args]]` is fine, a third element or a falsy non-dict (`""`) is an error.
- **Gamepad**: `click_button` pressed with `update=False` and released with `update=True`, so the only report sent said "not pressed"; each state is sent. `set_dpad` called `directional_pad()` and `XUSB_*_DPAD_UP_LEFT` / `_NONE`, which `VX360Gamepad` and `XUSB_BUTTON` do not have (checked against the vgamepad 0.1.0 source), so every direction raised; diagonals press two buttons and `none` releases all four.
- **Clipboard history**: `xclip -o` exits 1 on an empty clipboard and the `CalledProcessError` / `TimeoutExpired` killed the poll thread; subprocess errors are a missed capture.
- **File dialogs**: `wait_for_window`'s timeout (`AutoControlActionException`) escaped `wait_window` instead of returning `{"handled": False}`.
- **Postconditions**: `compile_postcondition` never passed a `before` frame, so `disappears` was always false and `appears` meant "present"; it takes `before=`.
- **RTF reader**: `\'XX` bytes were decoded one at a time as cp1252, ignoring `\ansicpg` (`\ansicpg936 \'b0\'a1` read as two Latin letters); bytes are collected and decoded in the document's code page. `\~`, `\_`, `\emdash`, `\endash`, `\bullet` and the quote words produce their characters, and a `-` after a control word is text unless a digit follows.
- **Smaller**: an unknown easing name fell back to linear silently (now `ValueError`); a negative `pause_delay` produced negative typing delays (the clamp now comes after the pause).
- **Not changed**: `mouse_relative` truncates fractional deltas (the executor already passes ints).
- **Tests**: `test_input_form_audit.py` (new, 13; all fail on the previous commit); `test_anthropic_computer_use.py` now pins both the X11 and the Windows spellings with a stubbed key table, and `test_cua_action_batch.py` expects the explicit scroll direction.
- **Files**: `cua_action.py`, `agent/backends/anthropic_computer_use.py`, `action_lint/linter.py`, `gamepad/_facade.py`, `clipboard_history.py`, `file_dialog.py`, `postcondition.py`, `clipboard_rich_formats.py`, `tween_drag.py`, `humanize/typing.py`, both `v151_features_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-53 · 2026-09-24 · File-dialog confirm and popup-watchdog keys resolve to the platform's key names · #bugfix #audit

- **Defect**: `handle_file_dialog`'s default `confirm_key="enter"` and a popup-watchdog rule's key action ("esc", "enter") went to `type_keyboard` as written, and the Windows key table spells them `return` / `escape`, so the default confirm step and those dismiss rules failed on Windows. Found while fixing the same problem in computer use and CUA translation (U-20260924-52).
- **Fix**: `FileDialogDriver.confirm` and the watchdog's key action resolve the name through `cua_action.resolve_key_name`.
- **Tests**: `test_key_name_callers.py` (new, 2; both fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `file_dialog.py`, `watchdog/popup_watchdog.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-54 · 2026-09-24 · Observation and state: an emergency stop that wakes a sleeping script, non-overlapping mark labels, strict placeholders, CloudEvents and coordinate spaces · #bugfix #audit

- **Emergency stop**: `_thread.interrupt_main()` only sets a flag, so on Windows a main thread inside `time.sleep` / `Event.wait` / a lock wait stopped when the wait ended (measured: a 3 s sleep stopped after 3.0 s) and never during a wait without a timeout. `signal.raise_signal(SIGINT)` goes through the handler that also wakes those waits (0.2 s, measured); it is used when Python's SIGINT handler is installed, falling back to `interrupt_main` otherwise. The listener also read the key once before watching (the Windows state carries "pressed since the last call", which fired it at start) and re-arms after the key is released instead of ending after the first press.
- **Set-of-marks labels**: when no candidate spot fit, the fallback was clamped and returned without checking placed labels, so crowded marks got identical, overlapping labels; without `bounds`, a mark at y=0 got a label at y=-16. Candidates must start at >= 0 and the fallback is stepped down / across until it clears every label.
- **Script variables**: `"${a.${b}}"` replaced the inner name and passed `"${a.b}"` as literal text to the action; nested placeholders now raise `ValueError` as the module promises for unresolved names. (`"$${n}"` still means a dollar sign followed by the value.)
- **Lock waits**: `sleep(interval_s)` was not capped to the time left (`wait_for_unlock(timeout_s=1, interval_s=30)` blocked 30 s); it is capped and clamped by `clamp_poll_interval`.
- **Observer**: a manual `poll_once()` racing the poll thread (or a thread left from a `stop()` whose join timed out) evaluated rules concurrently and could fire one transition twice; evaluations are serialised by an `RLock`.
- **CloudEvents**: empty `source` / `type` produced an invalid envelope, a caller's `time` was not checked, and bytes were put in `data` (failing later in `json.dumps`); the required attributes are enforced, `time` must be RFC 3339 with an offset, and bytes go in `data_base64`.
- **Coordinate spaces**: a zero size raised a bare `ZeroDivisionError` on first use and negative sizes gave nonsense; sizes must be positive (`ValueError`).
- **Screen state**: an unchanged box given as a tuple, or off by float noise, was reported as moved; boxes compare element-wise within half a pixel.
- **Not changed**: `observation_delta` keeps pairing at `iou_threshold=0` (deliberate, see U-20260924-43); `CoordinateSpace` has no monitor origin (a feature, not a defect); embedded values keep Python's `str()` formatting.
- **Tests**: `test_observation_state_audit.py` (new, 9; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `critical_exit.py`, `marks_layout.py`, `script_vars/interpolate.py`, `lock_session.py`, `observer.py`, `events/cloud_events.py`, `coordinate_space.py`, `screen_state.py`, both `critical_exit_doc.rst`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-55 · 2026-09-24 · Image analysis and packaging: Machado CVD simulation, square-aware widget classes, repair that does not re-act, schema-valid registry manifests · #bugfix #audit

- **CVD simulation**: the matrices were Coblis-style and applied to gamma-encoded values, so deuteranope green (0, 128, 0) came out (48, 38, 38) instead of about (119, 106, 24) and blue lost ~30 %, giving wrong `colors_collide` answers. It now uses Machado et al. (2009) in linear RGB (verified against hand-computed values).
- **Widget classification**: a square's circularity (pi/4 = 0.785) sits inside the range measured on drawn circles (0.80-0.86), so every checkbox read as a radio; a hollow rectangle of aspect ~2.6 measures the same circularity as a filled pill (0.59), so text fields read as toggles. `box_features` adds `vertices` (approxPolyDP at 2 %: 4 for squares, 8 for circles, measured), radios need non-4 vertices, toggles need a filled track, and hollow boxes from aspect 1.8 are text fields.
- **Step repair**: after `escalate` the action ran again (`act, escalate, act`), repeating an action that had just changed the wrong target; escalation now ends the repair. For a `changed` verdict the action is not repeated (it toggled back / submitted twice); the tactic is applied and the result re-verified.
- **SBOM**: names were only lowercased, so `typing_extensions` / `typing-extensions` (and two more) appeared twice (111 components with duplicate purls, measured), and every installed optional extra was listed; names are PEP 503 normalised, `extra` requirements are skipped and other markers are evaluated with `packaging` when available.
- **MCP registry manifest**: the description was 161 characters where `server.schema.json` allows 100 (checked in modelcontextprotocol/registry), and the tool list sat under a `_meta` key named after the server instead of `io.modelcontextprotocol.registry/publisher-provided`; both follow the schema and a longer description raises.
- **Stubs**: keyword-only parameters were rendered without the bare `*` (`def f(a=1, *, b)` became a SyntaxError) and positional-only without `/`; a docstring with a trailing quote, a backslash or `"""` broke the stub. Markers are emitted and the docstring escaped.
- **Provenance**: `verify_provenance(stmt, {})` reported no mismatches with nothing checked; unchecked subjects are reported (`actual: None`), and `build_provenance` refuses two subjects with one name (`dist/a/x.whl` and `dist/b/x.whl` both became `x.whl`).
- **Smaller**: "3.14" no longer ends a sentence in the readability counts; `simhash(bits>64)` raises instead of zeroing the high bits.
- **Not changed (Progress.md)**: the registry server name / repository URL still use the old `Intergration-Automation-Testing` organisation; renaming is a publishing decision.
- **Tests**: `test_image_packaging_audit.py` (new, 11; all fail on the previous commit); `test_icon_classify_batch.py` expects the new `vertices` feature.
- **Files**: `cvd_simulate.py`, `icon_classify.py`, `step_repair.py`, `sbom.py`, `mcp_registry/registry.py`, `stubs/generator.py`, `provenance.py`, `readability.py`, `near_dup.py`, the v69 / v214 / v219 feature docs (both languages), `Progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-56 · 2026-09-24 · Emergency stop wakes a sleeping main thread on Linux and macOS too · #bugfix #ci

- **Defect**: U-20260924-54 made the emergency stop raise a real SIGINT with `signal.raise_signal`, which wakes a sleeping main thread on Windows. On POSIX `raise_signal` from the listener thread signals that thread, so the main thread slept on (measured in a python:3.12 Linux container: 3.0 s for a 3 s sleep) and CI's Linux and macOS `pytest-headless` jobs failed `test_the_emergency_stop_wakes_a_sleeping_main_thread`.
- **Fix**: where `signal.pthread_kill` exists (Linux, macOS) the SIGINT is sent to the main thread (0.2 s in the same container); Windows keeps `raise_signal`.
- **Also**: the batch landed with its local full-suite run cut short by the old critical-exit tests (they stubbed `_thread.interrupt_main`, not the new seam); d7bd1353 fixed those tests.
- **Files**: `critical_exit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-57 · 2026-09-24 · Type-check the SBOM's optional packaging import in CI's bare install · #ci #typing

- **Defect**: U-20260924-55 added a lazy `from packaging.markers import ...` to `sbom._applies`. CI's `typing-stable-api` job installs only the package and mypy, where `packaging` is absent, so mypy reported `import-not-found` on all three platforms; the local check passed because its scratch venv had `packaging` installed.
- **Fix**: the import carries `# type: ignore[import-not-found]`, as the optional `usb.core` imports do; runtime behaviour is unchanged (without `packaging`, markers are treated as satisfied).
- **Verified**: `typing_contract_verify.py` in a venv without `packaging`: 0 modules failing on win32 / linux / darwin.
- **Files**: `sbom.py`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts, if changed).

## U-20260924-59 · 2026-09-24 · Triggers, scheduler and data sources: serialised engine start/stop, poison-proof email polling, quoted mailboxes, answerable webhook verbs, numeric max_runs, contained .xlsx errors · #bugfix #audit

- **Trigger engine lifecycle**: `TriggerEngine.start()` / `stop()` had no lock (the Scheduler's has one), so two concurrent starts created two polling threads and every trigger fired twice, and a `stop()` between creating and starting the thread raised `cannot join thread before it is started`. Both run under a lifecycle `RLock` and only a live thread is joined. `EmailTriggerWatcher.stop()` now takes the lock `start()` already held.
- **Poison email**: `email.policy.default` parses headers on read, and a malformed address (`From: <"`, found by fuzzing) raised `IndexError` outside every guard; the UID was never marked processed, later UIDs in that pass were skipped, and the next poll hit the same message, so the mailbox stopped for good. Headers that do not parse fall back to their raw text (the script still fires), and a message that cannot be read at all is logged and skipped.
- **Mailbox names**: imaplib sends `select`'s argument unquoted, so `Sent Items` or `[Gmail]/All Mail` went out as a BAD command; the name is now sent as an IMAP quoted string.
- **Webhook verbs**: any verb was accepted, but the handler answers only GET / POST / PUT / DELETE, so `PATCH` or `HEAD` webhooks registered fine and always got 501. `PATCH` is now handled and other verbs are refused by `add()`.
- **Scheduler `max_runs`**: `"2"` (as JSON or an MCP call sends it) passed validation, then `runs >= "2"` raised inside every tick before the next run time advanced, so the job ran on every tick forever. The validated `int` is stored.
- **Excel sources**: openpyxl's `BadZipFile` / `InvalidFileException` derive from plain `Exception` and escaped the executor's containment (`raise_on_error=False` included); they are re-raised as `AutoControlActionException`.
- **Docstring**: `FilePathTrigger`'s docstring sat below a `ClassVar`, so it was not the class docstring.
- **Tests**: `test_trigger_lifecycle_audit.py` (new, 12; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `trigger_engine.py`, `email_trigger.py`, `webhook_server.py`, `scheduler.py`, `data_source.py`, the webhook section of the new-features docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-60 · 2026-09-24 · AC_idempotency_release: scripts and MCP clients can free a key whose work failed · #feature #done

- **Gap (Progress.md)**: `IdempotencyStore.release()` existed only in the headless API. The executor's named stores have no TTL, so in a JSON script, over MCP or from the Script Builder a key whose work raised stayed `in_progress` for the life of the process and every retry was told to wait.
- **Added**: `AC_idempotency_release` (`{name, key}` -> `{released}`), MCP tool `ac_idempotency_release`, and the Script Builder **Flow** command *Idempotency: Release*. A completed key is kept (`released: false`).
- **Counts** (measured): 775 `AC_*` commands, 678 MCP tools (659 `ac_*` + 19 aliases), in `architecture_explore.md`, `README.md` and both translations.
- **Stubs**: `actions.pyi` regenerated with the generator, which also picks up signatures that had changed since the last regeneration (`AC_queue_complete`'s `claim`, `AC_shell_command`'s `command`).
- **Tests**: `test_idempotency_batch.py` gains the begin -> in_progress -> release -> new round trip and the kept-completed case; the wiring test covers all three surfaces.
- **Files**: `action_executor.py`, `_handlers_executor_bridge.py`, `_factories.py`, `command_schema.py`, `actions.pyi`, the v103 feature docs (both languages), `Progress.md`, `README.md`, `README/README_zh-CN.md`, `README/README_zh-TW.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md`.
## U-20260924-58 · 2026-09-24 · Small utilities: implicit-SSL port, a profiler frozen at stop, strict Content-Length, WebRunner screenshots, notifications that report failure · #bugfix #audit

- **Mail**: `use_ssl` without a port connected implicit SSL to 587, the STARTTLS port, so the handshake failed; it now defaults to 465 (RFC 8314).
- **Resource profiler**: FPS and duration were measured up to the `report()` call, so they kept falling after `stop()`, and without `start()` the duration was the whole monotonic clock (240,480 s, measured); the duration now ends at `stop()` and is 0 before `start()`. The speedscope export lacked `$schema`, which speedscope's importer needs to recognise the format. Spans on two threads that ended out of order left the first thread's tag on every later sample; each span now removes its own entry.
- **HTTP headers**: `Content-Length` accepted `+5`, `1_000` and non-ASCII digits because `int()` does; only ASCII digits are accepted now (RFC 9110 `1*DIGIT`). `Transfer-Encoding: xchunkedy` counted as chunked; `chunked` must be the final coding.
- **WebRunner bridge**: `web_screenshot` sent `file_name`, but WebRunner's `save_screenshot(self, file_path)` takes `file_path`, so it always failed against the real package; the test's fake had copied the wrong name.
- **Notifications**: a notifier that exited non-zero was reported as `shown=True`; it is now `shown=False` with the exit code. Windows toasts used the unregistered app id `AutoControl`, which Windows drops silently; they use PowerShell's registered id (confirmed with `Get-StartApps`).
- **Watchers**: `LogTail.emit` fell back to `getMessage()`, which failed the same way, so a bad `%` log call raised in its caller while the Live HUD was attached; it now goes to `handleError`. `PixelWatcher.sample` let the Windows backend's `AutoControlException` (GetPixel failed) through instead of returning `None`.
- **Keyboard layout**: `char_table` merged the US table under the layout's, so a key the layout leaves out (German dead key `^` on 0xDC) came back as US `\`; the US table now stands in only when the layout gives nothing.
- **Images**: a box or highlight given from its bottom-right corner raised ValueError in Pillow; the rect is normalised. `region_color_stats` counted the black padding `crop()` adds past the image edge (a red image read 75 % black); the region is clipped. Annotate, QR and colour stats closed multi-frame files only at garbage collection.
- **Smaller**: a project path containing `"` produced executor files that did not compile (the path is now a Python literal); a time before the epoch raised `OverflowError` from TOTP instead of `TOTPError`; a SQLite file that could not be opened raised `sqlite3.Error` past the executor instead of `AutoControlActionException`.
- **Tests**: `test_small_utils_audit.py` (new, 19; 17 fail on the previous commit); `test_keyboard_layout.py` and `test_webrunner_bridge.py` pin the new behaviour.
- **Files**: `email_sender.py`, `resource_profiler.py`, `http_headers.py`, `webrunner_bridge/bridge.py`, `notifier.py`, `watcher.py`, `keyboard_layout.py`, `annotate.py`, `color_stats.py`, `qr.py`, `project/create_project_structure.py`, `project/template/template_executor.py`, `remote_desktop/totp.py`, `sql_query.py`, the v4 / v5 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-61 · 2026-09-24 · Data utilities: JWT expiry and canonical segments, bounded similarity, framework DAG errors, strict time-series and schema arguments, safe flag serves · #bugfix #audit

- **JWT**: a token was still accepted at its `exp` second; RFC 7519 4.1.4 says not "on or after". A non-ASCII payload segment raised `UnicodeEncodeError`, a deeply nested header raised `RecursionError` before the signature was checked, and `exp: 10**400` raised `OverflowError`; all are `JwtError` now. The unused low bits of a segment's last character were ignored, so one signature had several valid token strings (defeating a denylist keyed on the token); only the canonical spelling is accepted.
- **Text similarity**: `jaccard`/`dice` with `n=0` scored unrelated strings 1.0 and `jaro_winkler` with `prefix_weight=1.0` returned 1.33; `n < 1` and weights outside `[0, 0.25]` raise `ValueError`.
- **DAG**: `DagDefinitionError` derived from `ValueError` only and escaped every `AutoControlException` boundary; it now derives from both.
- **Time series**: samples sharing a timestamp were sorted by value, which made up counter resets (`ts_increase` gave 14 instead of 9); a mistyped `fill` turned every gap into `None`, and `bucket_s=inf` returned a NaN bucket. Unknown fills and non-finite buckets raise `ValueError`.
- **Data quality**: NaN and inf passed `min`/`max` rules; an unhashable value against an `allowed` set raised `TypeError`. The `ipv4` preset matched `999.999.999.999` and `phone` matched ISO dates and dotted addresses.
- **Feature flags**: an empty `rollout` raised `StopIteration` and `{"variant": "on"}` raised `TypeError`; the latter is now accepted and anything else serves the default variant with reason `ERROR`.
- **Schema compatibility**: a field made required without a `properties` entry was missed, and an unknown `mode` (e.g. `Backward`) reported every change as compatible; it now raises.
- **Text regions**: a 1x1 image raised `cv2.error`; both finders are wrapped like the other matchers.
- **Test data**: CSV output failed on rows with different keys; the header is the union of keys.
- **Tests**: `test_data_utils_audit.py` (new, 16; 15 fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `jwt_codec.py`, `text_similarity.py`, `dag/graph.py`, `timeseries.py`, `data_quality.py`, `feature_flags.py`, `schema_compat.py`, `text_regions.py`, `test_data.py`, the v68 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-64 · 2026-09-24 · Per-flow history for flow selection and sharding, depth-safe XY-cut, config env and lossless ints, null-aware uniqueness, CLDR unit lists, MIME-typed A2A modes, UIA Value / RangeValue ids · #bugfix #audit

- **Flow selection / sharding**: `rank_flows` and `shard_flows` read one global newest-`max(100, window*len(flows))` window of run history, so a flow whose runs sat behind that many unrelated runs scored as never run (`runs: 0`, 0.8, even with 10 failing runs) and sharding gave it the default weight. `HistoryStore.list_runs` gains a `script_path` filter applied before `limit` (one static parameterised query), and both read per flow.
- **Reading order**: each XY-cut is binary and took the first of equal gaps, so stacked paragraphs were peeled off one per level; nine paragraphs used up `max_depth=8` and the two columns below fell back to the top/left sort, interleaved (`A0, B0, A1, ...`). Only a change of axis now costs depth, and same-axis cuts merge into one n-ary node (a naive n-ary cut at every valley would slice the column rows and interleave them again).
- **Config schema**: `ConfigField.env` was parsed and never read; a missing field now comes from that variable before the default (pydantic-settings order), with `environ=` injectable. An `int` field refused nothing: 2.9 became 2; a float with a fraction is now an error.
- **Referential checks**: `check_unique_key` counted null keys as duplicates although the module follows dbt, whose `unique` test filters `where col is not null`; single-column keys skip nulls, composite keys still group them like SQL.
- **List formatting**: the unit style was comma-only in every locale, but CLDR (cldr-json listPatterns) ends es / fr / pt unit lists with the conjunction and de with `und` (two items stay `A, B`).
- **A2A**: `defaultInputModes` / `defaultOutputModes` were `["text"]`; the 0.3.0 AgentCard schema defines them as MIME types, so they are `["text/plain"]`.
- **Locator chain**: `within((x, y, w, h))` kept a centre at `x + w`, one pixel outside the region.
- **Windows accessibility**: `read_state` checked Value support with 30029 (`IsGridItemPatternAvailable`) and RangeValue with 30034 (`IsScrollPatternAvailable`); UIAutomationClient.h (10.0.26100) gives 30043 and 30033, so edits and sliders lost their value. The two tests that pinned the wrong ids now use the header's.
- **Tests**: `test_layout_data_audit.py` (new, 9; all fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `windows_state.py`, `history_store.py`, `test_select.py`, `test_shard.py`, `reading_flow.py`, `config_schema.py`, `referential.py`, `list_format.py`, `agent_card.py`, `locator_chain.py`, the v86 / v95 / v112 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).
## U-20260924-65 · 2026-09-24 · Make the poison-email regression test independent of the CPython patch release · #ci #test

- **Defect**: U-20260924-59's `test_a_malformed_header_neither_blocks_the_mailbox_nor_the_message` asserted the raw `From` text of `From: <"`. Locally (CPython 3.14.4) the email package raises `IndexError` on it, so the raw-header fallback returned `<"`; the CPython builds on CI parse it to `<>` instead, and the test failed on every `pytest-headless` square of PR #489.
- **Fix**: that test now asserts only what the fix guarantees (both messages fire once). The fallback itself is covered deterministically by `test_an_unparsable_header_falls_back_to_its_raw_text`, whose message class raises from `get("From")` on every version.
- **Files**: `test_trigger_lifecycle_audit.py`.

## U-20260924-62 · 2026-09-24 · Text, config and registries: whole -or-later licences, strict REST booleans, .po entries without blank lines, CLDR plural operands, host-bound file URIs, coturn and XML injection · #bugfix #audit #security

- **Licence policy**: the operator split used `\bOR\b`, and `\b` also matches at hyphens, so `GPL-3.0-or-later` became `GPL-3.0-`, `or`, `-later` and passed a denylist naming it (and failed an allowlist). Operators now count only between whitespace or parentheses.
- **REST USB endpoints**: `bool("false")` is True, so `{"enabled": "false"}` switched passthrough on and `{"allow": "false"}` added an allow rule. `enabled`, `allow` and `prompt_on_open` must be JSON booleans; anything else is a 400.
- **gettext**: `.po` entries were split on blank lines only, but msgfmt needs none, so consecutive entries merged into one (`ab` -> `AB`) and so did every entry of a CRLF file. An entry now ends at a blank line, a comment or a new `msgctxt`/`msgid` after its `msgstr`.
- **MessageFormat**: plural rules took the signed value, so -1 was `other` ("-1 days") and ordinal -1 was "-1th"; CLDR's operands are absolute. Integers stayed floats, so `#` printed 12345678901234568 for 12345678901234567.
- **MCP file URIs**: `file://server/share/x.txt` dropped the host and read the local `/share/x.txt`; a URI naming a host other than `localhost` is refused.
- **Tracing**: with `record_args=True`, an argument whose `__repr__` raised failed a call that would have worked; the attribute falls back to the type name.
- **coturn bundle**: a newline in the realm, user or secret wrote its own directives into `turnserver.conf` (e.g. `allow-loopback-peers`); line breaks, and `:` in the user, raise `ValueError` (exit 2 from the CLI).
- **Presence**: `PresenceError` derived from `ValueError` only; `register` stripped the id but lookups did not, so `unregister(" v1 ")` missed; a NaN cursor and a non-string role raised bare errors; one listener raising `KeyError` skipped the rest after the row was stored.
- **XML**: element and attribute names were written unchecked, so a key like `b><evil/><c` injected markup; names must be XML Names.
- **Tests**: `test_text_and_config_audit.py` (new, 15).
- **Files**: `license_policy.py`, `rest_handlers.py`, `gettext_catalog.py`, `message_format.py`, `mcp_server/_protocol.py`, `observability/tracing.py`, `remote_desktop/turn_config.py`, `remote_desktop/presence.py`, `change_xml_structure.py`, the operations-layer and USB-passthrough operator docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-63 · 2026-09-24 · JSONPath quoting and RFC 9535 ordering, OCR fields read below their label, hardware encoders that really open, odd-sized H.264 frames · #bugfix #audit

- **JSONPath**: `$['a','b']` was looked up as the single key `a','b` and a lone quote in a filter value was the empty string; both raise `ValueError` now. Escapes in quoted names were kept literally (`['a\'b']` looked for `a\'b`); RFC 9535 escapes are decoded. A parenless filter (`[?@.a==1]`) ended at the next filter's `)]` further along the path. `<` ordered `false < true` the Python way and `<=` missed `null <= null`; only two numbers or two strings are ordered, and `<=` is `<` or `==`.
- **OCR structure**: `min_table_rows=0` reached `min()` of an empty table; a label with nothing to its right took the next row's first cell, so in a two-column form `Age:` read the name, and `Name:` read the neighbouring label `Age:` as its value. The value is now the nearest cell below that is not itself a label.
- **Hardware H.264**: `available_hardware_codecs()` only created a context, which works for any encoder FFmpeg was built with, so `h264_qsv` was listed on a machine without an Intel GPU and failed at the first frame, past the libx264 fallback. Encoders are now opened with the host's settings, both when listing and when switching; NVENC refused libx264's `tune=zerolatency` (every frame failed) and buffered frames without `delay=0`, so each encoder gets its own low-latency options (checked on NVIDIA and AMD here: `h264_nvenc` and `h264_amf` listed, `h264_qsv` not).
- **H.264 provider**: a 101x75 frame made libx264 refuse to open (yuv420p needs even sizes), and `close()` then raised `av.error.ExternalError` and left the container open. Frames are cropped to even sizes and the container is closed even when the flush fails.
- **Tests**: `test_parsers_and_codecs_audit.py` (new, 13).
- **Files**: `jsonpath.py`, `ocr/structure.py`, `remote_desktop/hw_codec.py`, `remote_desktop/video_codec.py`, the v51 feature docs (both languages), `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-66 · 2026-09-24 · Audit tests that only see their own file's leaks and pass Codacy · #test #ci

- **Flaky leak check**: `test_multi_frame_files_are_closed` called `gc.collect()` while recording `ResourceWarning`s, so it also caught files other tests had left unclosed; one Windows square of PR #489 failed that way. Garbage is collected before recording, and only warnings naming the test's own file count.
- **Codacy**: the malformed-format case was a real logging call with too few arguments ("Not enough arguments for logging format string"), and the failing notifier returned a `subprocess.CompletedProcess` (Semgrep's subprocess rule). The test now hands `LogTail` a hand-built record, and the fakes return an object with only `returncode`; `test_notify.py`'s fake does the same.
- **Files**: `test_small_utils_audit.py`, `test_notify.py`.

## U-20260924-67 · 2026-09-24 · Remote desktop: RFC 6455 masking and control-frame rules, a handshake that survives a bad key, file transfers to no file, an honest encrypted recorder, restartable mic, host voice kept on · #bugfix #audit #security

- **WebSocket handshake**: a non-ASCII `Sec-WebSocket-Key` raised `UnicodeEncodeError`, which neither the WebSocket host nor the host's accept loop catches, so the handshake thread died with the socket left open. The key must now be base64 of exactly 16 bytes (RFC 6455 4.2.1); anything else is a 400 and `WsProtocolError`.
- **WebSocket frames**: a client answered PINGs with an unmasked PONG, which RFC 6455 5.1 requires a server to treat as a protocol error; the reply is masked like the client's other frames. The server accepted unmasked client frames and control frames over 125 bytes (5.5); both are refused. The PONG was written without the channel's send lock, so it could interleave with a frame another thread was sending; it now takes the lock.
- **File transfer**: a `dest_path` of `.`, `/` or `C:\` made `with_name` raise `ValueError` outside the guarded block, which ended the viewer's receive loop; the transfer now fails through `on_complete`. `"size": true` was accepted as 1.
- **Encrypted recorder**: a write that failed before any frame had succeeded kept its frame number, so the manifest's `frame_count` was ahead of its entries; the rollback no longer depends on earlier frames. `verify_manifest` raised `binascii.Error` / `TypeError` / `JSONDecodeError` on a tampered or malformed manifest instead of returning `False`. A manifest that could not be written was ignored silently; it is logged.
- **Viewer auth**: a token with a lone surrogate (valid in JSON) raised `UnicodeEncodeError` before the rejection path, so the viewer got no `auth_fail` until the grace deadline.
- **Mic uplink**: the capture / player was stored before `start()`, so a busy device left it set and every later `start()` returned early without capturing.
- **Host voice**: aiortc reuses the host-voice transceiver as the viewer's audio slot, so turning off viewer audio set it `inactive` and silenced the host too; a transceiver that is sending becomes `sendonly`.
- **Tests**: `test_remote_desktop_wire_audit.py` (new, 11); a lone-surrogate token in `test_webrtc_host_auth.py`; host voice in `test_webrtc_host_media.py`, whose fake transceiver gained a `sender`.
- **Files**: `ws_protocol.py`, `transport.py`, `file_transfer.py`, `jpeg_recorder_encrypted.py`, `webrtc_host_auth.py`, `webrtc_mic.py`, `webrtc_host_media.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-68 · 2026-09-24 · MCP requests that always get a reply, a drag that releases, sampling over HTTP, waits that look once; USB credits and claims without races; assertions through callbacks; gamepad, clipboard and volume errors in the family · #bugfix #audit

- **MCP dispatch**: `tools/call` contained `OSError`, `RuntimeError`, `ValueError`, `TypeError` and `KeyError`, but `1e400` in an argument raised `OverflowError` and a short colour list `IndexError`; the stdio worker died and that request never got a reply (reproduced through `serve_stdio`: replies came back for ids 0 and 2 only). `ArithmeticError` and `LookupError` are contained now, and `to_physical` / `to_model` / `audit_contrast` reject non-finite numbers and colours that are not three numbers in 0..255 with `ValueError`.
- **drag**: the button was pressed before the end point was checked, and a refused move (an off-screen `end_x`) left it held down; it is released where it was pressed.
- **Sampling**: `request_sampling` built its own pending slot without the connection id, so over HTTP every reply was discarded as another session's (elicitation worked, sampling timed out), and its ids were sequential. It now goes through the same connection-aware, unguessable-id path as the other client requests.
- **wait_for_image / wait_for_pixel**: `poll=1e400` reached `time.sleep` and raised `OverflowError`; `timeout=0` checked the deadline before the first look and never looked (`flow_control` treats 0 as look once); `timeout=1e400` sent `NaN` / `Infinity` progress, which is not JSON. They probe first, clamp the poll, never sleep past the deadline and omit an infinite total.
- **USB viewer client**: a credit granted between the credit check and the wait was lost (`set(); clear()`), so the transfer timed out with credit available; the event is now cleared under the lock by the waiter. Two transfers on one claim shared one pending slot, so one caller received the other's data; exchanges are serialised per claim. A failed send left its pending entry behind. A `CREDIT` of `null` / `[1]` / `1e999`, a reply without `claim_id` and undecodable transfer data raised `TypeError` / `OverflowError` / `KeyError` / `binascii.Error`; they are ignored or `UsbClientError`.
- **Callbacks**: `callback_function` turned a trigger's `AutoControlAssertionException` into `None`; assertion failures propagate, as everywhere else.
- **Gamepad**: `GamepadUnavailable` derived from `RuntimeError` only, and vgamepad's bare `Exception` at import and `AssertionError` from the constructor (ViGEmBus missing) escaped `is_available()`; it returns `False` and the constructor raises `GamepadUnavailable`, now an `AutoControlException`.
- **Clipboard**: `GlobalAlloc(GMEM_MOVEABLE, 0)` returns a discarded block that `GlobalLock` refuses, so an empty payload could never be set; at least one byte is allocated.
- **Volume / combinators**: `set_volume(nan)`, `inf` or `"abc"` raised bare `ValueError` / `OverflowError` (reachable through `AC_set_volume`), and `assert_eventually(timeout="5")` a `TypeError`; they raise `AutoControlActionException` / `AutoControlAssertionException`.
- **Tests**: `test_mcp_and_devices_audit.py` (new, 25; 24 fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `mcp_server/_protocol.py`, `server.py`, `_client_requests.py`, `tools/_handlers_input.py`, `tools/_handlers_screen.py`, `tools/_handlers_locators.py`, `tools/_handlers_qa.py`, `usb/passthrough/viewer_client.py`, `callback_function_executor.py`, `gamepad/_facade.py`, `win32_clipboard_api.py`, `system_volume.py`, `assertion/combinators.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-69 · 2026-09-24 · USB passthrough opens identical devices by serial, checks endpoint direction and releases interfaces; agent, self-heal, anchor, USB/IP and a11y fixes · #bugfix #audit

- **USB passthrough (libusb)**: `open` asked for the first device with the vendor:product ids and then rejected it when the serial differed, so the second of two identical devices could not be opened at all; every matching device is tried now. A viewer's `direction` was not checked against the endpoint address, and libusb takes the direction from the address, so `"in"` on OUT endpoint 0x02 wrote zero bytes to the device and `"out"` on 0x81 read and discarded; a contradiction raises. `close()` reattached kernel drivers while pyusb still held the interfaces it claims on the first transfer (`LIBUSB_ERROR_BUSY`, logged at debug only), so the host kept losing the device; the resources are disposed first.
- **Error family**: `UsbIpError` (a `ValueError`), `SelfHealError` (a `RuntimeError`) and `AnchorLocatorError` (a `ValueError`) escaped `AutoControlException` boundaries; they derive from both now.
- **Agent loop**: a backend decision of `None`, or an `input` that was a string or list, raised `AttributeError` / `ValueError` out of `AgentLoop.run()`; the run stops with a message, or records the step's error and continues.
- **Self-heal**: a failed screenshot in the VLM fallback raised `AutoControlScreenException` past `self_heal_locate` even with `raise_on_miss=False`, and the attempt never reached the heal log; framework errors are a miss like the others.
- **a11y audit**: the `tab` role hint matched `table`, `table cell` and `DataTable`, so every unnamed table cell was reported as a missing label.
- **Tests**: `test_usb_agent_locator_audit.py` (new, 17; 14 fail on the previous commit).
- **Files**: `usb/passthrough/backend.py`, `usbip/protocol.py`, `agent/agent_loop.py`, `self_healing/locator.py`, `anchor_locator/locator.py`, `a11y_audit/audit.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-70 · 2026-09-24 · WebRTC media: screen frames stamped at their real rate, host voice mixed down for mono players, audio device errors contained, a reusable viewer, mDNS and bridge cleanup · #bugfix #audit

- **Screen track timestamps**: `ScreenVideoTrack.recv` used aiortc's `next_timestamp()`, which adds 1/30 s per frame and paces to 30 fps on its own, so 10 fps frames were stamped a third of real time apart and `set_target_fps(60)` was capped at 30 (measured 9.1 fps / 29.7 fps). Frames are stamped from the wall clock on a 90 kHz clock and the track keeps its own pacing.
- **Opus playback**: aiortc's Opus decoder always yields 48 kHz stereo, and the receiver wrote it unchanged into a mono player, so voice played at half speed and twice the length (60 ms sent, 120 ms played). Frames are resampled to the player's layout and rate; float PCM is scaled properly instead of truncated.
- **Audio devices**: `sounddevice.PortAudioError` derives from `Exception` directly, so it escaped every `RuntimeError` / `OSError` guard; a missing output device made the viewer's `process_offer` fail whenever the host sent voice. A stream whose `start()` failed was kept, so `is_running` stayed true and every later `start()` did nothing. Device errors now become `AudioBackendError` (now an `AutoControlException`), a failed stream is closed, late writes are dropped with a log line, and a capture block carrying an overflow flag is still delivered.
- **Viewer reuse**: `stop()` set `_closed` and nothing cleared it, so a viewer given a second offer connected but its video loop ended at once (0 frames); a new offer now tears the previous session down fully and clears the flag.
- **mDNS**: a `NonUniqueNameException` from `register_service` (the name taken on the LAN) left the `Zeroconf` instance's sockets and thread running; the advertiser and the browser close it when setting up fails.
- **Asyncio bridge**: `stop()` closed a loop whose thread was still running (`RuntimeError`) and left it registered, and never cancelled pending tasks, whose futures then hung; tasks are cancelled first and the loop is closed only after its thread has ended.
- **Tests**: `test_webrtc_media_audit.py` (new, 6; all fail on the previous commit); `test_webrtc_opus_audio.py` now drives the receiver with real `av.AudioFrame`s and adds a stereo-to-mono case; the fake sounddevice in `test_remote_desktop_audio.py` gained `PortAudioError`.
- **Files**: `audio.py`, `webrtc_audio.py`, `webrtc_transport.py`, `webrtc_viewer.py`, `lan_discovery.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `architecture_explore.md` (line counts).

## U-20260924-71 · 2026-09-24 · Wayland: libei devices survive a pause and are released once, wlr-randr sizes follow rotation and scale, a bad capture override is a screen error · #bugfix #audit

- **libei pause**: `DEVICE_PAUSED` was handled like `DEVICE_REMOVED`, dropping and unreffing the device, so the `DEVICE_RESUMED` that libei sends after a screen lock or VT switch found nothing to resume and every emission fell back to ydotool for the rest of the process. A pause now only stops emission until the resume.
- **libei references**: a later `DEVICE_REMOVED` unreffed the already-released device again, and a removed device the sender had never kept (none of its capabilities wanted) was unreffed too; both release memory libei still owns. The sender now tracks the references it took and releases each exactly once, including at teardown.
- **wlr-randr layout**: output rectangles used the physical mode, so a 90-degree 1920x1080 output counted 1920 wide and a scale-2 4K output 3840 wide, and `size()` was wrong on any rotated or HiDPI layout. `Transform:` and `Scale:` are read and the logical size is used.
